# GLM review 2026-11-01 (glm-5.3): 363 findings, 164 high severity, 11 shard(s)

Revision cef92d3bf9ca. Shards: agentbox#2, agentbox#3, dream-engine, estate, nostr-rust-forum, ontomark, visionclaw#2, visionclaw#3, visionflow, visiontouch-rs, vowl-wasm. Unverified hypotheses: reproduce each with a failing test or check (build-with-quality) before acting.

## agentbox#2 / critical

### F-01 — The governance/approval pipeline intercepts almost no real traffic
- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-14.2, AB-14.11, AB-14.15
- Evidence: AB-14.1's own DIVERGENCE note: action-plane wires exactly one surface (POST /v1/tasks, `action-plane.js:115`); "every OTHER surface above (direct tool call, code-mode, ACI shell, consultant, dream, beads, alt harness) still reaches EXEC with no interceptor". The dream engine "RECORDS each side effect … but nothing there approves or denies" (`routes/exec-record.js:63`). AB-14.2's note: the 9-stage state machine is "fully implemented and contract-tested … but is never reached by real traffic". AB-14.15 Open: EXP-B8 signs forum posts and opens PRs recording "neither through POST /v1/exec/record or the authority journal".
- Failure: an agent performs a spend/egress/secret-class action through any normal surface — no approval is requested (the approvals inbox stays empty), no journal row exists, and post-hoc audit cannot reconstruct who authorised what. The entire approvals UI (AB-14.4/14.5) governs traffic that mostly never arrives.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE labelled "TOP OPEN RISK", DOC-DRIFT, Open)

### F-02 — Shipped default custody is one uid: every process can read every key, including the root operator key the gateway signs with
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.5, AB-16.13, AB-16.14, AB-14.14, AB-14.16
- Evidence: AB-16.1 N4: with role_isolation off (the shipped default) every supervised program, MCP server, skill and agent runs as devuser, so "each can read every other's 0600 key file and the mirror key, and uid 1000 is admitted to the session socket by design". AB-16.5: the gateway's default identity is 'operator', so "out of the box the gateway holds and signs with the ROOT operator key" (`gateway.cjs:155`). AB-16.14 DEBT: 38 DEVUSER_CLASS credentials "stay in every agent process by exception". AB-14.14 Debt: a same-container process "can stamp any pubkey into the journalled agent_did" over loopback. AB-16.14 Tension: with the flag off the faucet treasury key is still read from the workspace bind (`agentbox.toml:1926`). The identity port that would fix this is inert by default (AB-16.16 OFF subgraph, `identity_port/mod.rs:92-96`).
- Failure: one prompt-injection or bug in any single devuser process — hook, skill, MCP server, consultant CLI — reads the root operator nsec and all 38 provider credentials, forges journalled attribution, and can sign commands the nostr-gateway accepts as adminPub (`gateway.cjs:151,157`). Single compromise = operator identity compromise.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — N4, the gateway DIVERGENCE, the DEVUSER DEBT and the agent_did Debt are each marked; the composed one-compromise→operator-identity consequence is not drawn anywhere.

### F-03 — One shared secret is simultaneously the voice LLM bearer, the bridge bearer and the break-glass identity bypass
- Topics: AB-16.6, AB-16.9, AB-12.3, AB-12.7
- Evidence: AB-16.9 C3: the proxy break-glass bearer NIP98_PROXY_ALLOW_BEARER is "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4". AB-12.7: BRIDGE_TOKEN is KYUTAI_LLM_API_KEY, held and sent by the Unmute backend container on every `/v1/chat/completions` call. AB-16.6: a matching break-glass token "bypasses NIP-98 entirely", is accepted on port 9096 over the LAN and "via ?access_token= / ?bearer= on WS upgrades"; ADR-2027: "unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime", "unset scope is unrestricted", and the ADR is decision proposed / implementation none / activation inactive. AB-12.3 note: browser WS cannot set headers, so the bridge token rides in query strings.
- Failure: the token every voice request carries (container env, URL access logs, any devuser read per F-02) is, by configuration, a full identity bypass through the operator proxy with placeholder downstream attribution, no expiry and no scope. Only `/v1/approvals/:id/decide` refuses it (`approvals.js:139-144`); everything else behind the proxy accepts it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the AB-16.6 DIVERGENCE and the same-value note exist; the cross-service blast radius of one secret serving three roles is not drawn as one finding.

### F-04 — Voice LAN doors published 0.0.0.0 outside the sanctioned gate chain into a Bash-allowlisted child that can drive tmux
- Topics: AB-12.7, AB-12.3
- Evidence: AB-12.7 DIVERGENCE: "port 8444 and port 8443 are published 0.0.0.0 by docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41, while only port 9096 is the ADR-045 D2 sanctioned NIP-98-gated LAN door covered by the loopback CI gate". The Caddyfile handles shown (`handle /embed*` → frontend:3000, `handle_path /api/*` → backend:80) include no auth step. The backend then calls tab0-bridge with the bearer, and the bridge spawns `claude -p` whose metaAllowedTools is a Bash allowlist including "tmux and session-control.cjs list and send patterns" (`server.mjs:320-329`).
- Failure: a LAN peer opens the cockpit/realtime path and speaks an instruction; the voice loop's allowlisted Bash child types into the coordinator tmux session — control of the coordinator without NIP-98, through doors the CI gate does not cover.
- Confidence: medium — the ungated publication is stated; the absence of auth at Caddy is inferred from the material drawing none there.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes for the publication (DIVERGENCE); the injection chain is not marked.

### F-05 — The corpus contradicts itself on whether session content leaves the box redacted
- Topics: AB-13.9, AB-13.17, AB-13.10, AB-16.11
- Evidence: AB-13.9 shows the mirror calling `redactForEgress(body)` with "null, skipped (fail-closed)" on redaction failure (`nostr-live-mirror.cjs:453-457`), and AB-13.17 marks "RESOLVED ADR-2026 … ONE policy contract: … mandatory redaction … cross-checked by one fixture". AB-16.11 states the opposite: the mirror "composes UNREDACTED selected text", "one of them unredacted before wrapping", concluding "there is no single answer to 'is session content leaving this box, and redacted how'". The same panel also omits the digest's redaction that AB-13.10 draws before Z.AI.
- Failure: whichever panel is stale, an operator or auditor asking the privacy-critical question gets opposite answers from the same corpus. If AB-16.11 matches the code, unredacted session text leaves for a cloud relay on every Stop event.
- Confidence: high that the two panels conflict (the corpus's own RESOLVED markers weight AB-13.9 as current, but the code must be checked).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — each panel carries its own ADR-2026 marker; neither flags the mutual contradiction.

### F-06 — Irreversible sats spend is ungoverned and no surface can state total spend
- Topics: AB-15.6, AB-15.7, AB-15.8, AB-14.11
- Evidence: AB-15.6 DEBT: "spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile" — the flock-guarded cost_cap ledger (`ledger.rs:75`), the pod sats balance (`payments.js:205`) and the marketplace token_budget (`llm-marketplace.js:85`) — "No surface sums them, so no single number answers what this container has spent". AB-14.11 DEBT: the classification table names payment_settlement zero-tolerance (`agentbox.toml:1405`) "but no production path passes that actionClass … routes/payments.js never reaches authority.js:226" — the only call sites are tests (`authority.test.js:129,:248`).
- Failure: `/v1/pay/buy`, `/withdraw` and `/deposit` move sats with no human-approval gate ever firing; per-store caps don't compose, so an agent (or a stolen identity) can spend through three channels at once and no report can show the total.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (two DEBT markers)

### F-07 — Dream PRs are not bound to the evaluated tree, and a crash after Persisted silently loses the publication
- Topics: AB-23.6, AB-23.2, AB-23.7
- Evidence: AB-23.6 Open: "the PR request carries a branch name, not the verified commit hash" (`seams.rs:341`); "a successful create command returns Some even for empty stdout" (`seams.rs:531`); the tested boundary "does not validate the URL or bind the pushed head to the evaluated tree". AB-23.2 Tension: "a crash between the journal write and the pull request leaves a night marked finished with no draft", "Nothing records the forge or ledger outcome after Persisted"; recovery is a manual `reset-night` (`main.rs:142-144`).
- Failure: the branch moves between evaluation and publication (operator commit or racing push) and the human merges a PR whose head the gate never evaluated — the merge gate's whole assurance is void. Or nights finish with no draft and no record that publication was lost.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, Tension)

### F-08 — Content egress is the default posture: nightly repo content to Z.AI, every routed prompt to an external judge
- Topics: AB-23.15, AB-23.2, AB-22.2
- Evidence: AB-23.15 Debt: default `llm_provider = "zai"` (`agentbox.toml:2585`) so "nightly repository content leaves the LAN on every unattended night"; the LAN Loom path is opt-in (`llm.rs:205`). AB-22.2 Debt: router jev is the default (`agentbox.toml:1293`), so "a turn that routes at all and misses the cascade leaves the container". AB-22.2 OPEN: whether ADR-2090's egress acceptance covers "the descriptions of the operator's synced claude.ai skills, now sent as candidate rubrics" is "not recorded" (`ADR-2091:38`).
- Failure: private repository content and every routable prompt (plus synced-skill descriptions beyond the recorded acceptance) leave to third parties by default, with no per-project gate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (documented choices, but the rubric-egress question is unresolved and the nightly egress is marked as debt)
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, Open)

### F-09 — Out-of-tree editable crontabs are an unsupervised execution and persistence surface
- Topics: AB-13.1, AB-23.19, AB-14.15
- Evidence: AB-13.1: forum-backup-cron's crontab is "OUT OF TREE, editable without rebuild — only the stanza is baked" (`flake.nix:3082`), with PATH pinned to coreutils/grep/findutils/**curl**/jq/gzip and CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN required. AB-23.19 Debt: the checkout crontab still ticks EXP-B8 every 30 minutes under a program named podcast-cron. AB-14.15 Open: EXP-B8's stopping rule fires a signed forum post (`exp-b8-label-log.cjs:550-563`) and a `gh` PR (`:573-606`) with no journal record.
- Failure: any devuser-writable compromise (trivial per F-02) persists by editing the crontab and gets scheduled curl with the Cloudflare token for exfiltration; meanwhile an unattended experiment already performs outward side effects on a schedule nothing supervises or journals.
- Confidence: medium — each fact is stated; the persistence-vector composition is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the EXP-B8 scheduling and unjournalled effects are marked (Debt, Open); the crontab-editability vector itself is unmarked.

### F-10 — The relay consumer can go deaf for up to an hour, and deafness turns approvals into timeout denials
- Topics: AB-13.7, AB-14.6, AB-13.13
- Evidence: AB-13.7 note: the Cloudflare DO relay "stops PUSHING to an idle subscription ~20s after its last REQ regardless of socket liveness, and only begins pushing again for a FRESH subId" — id reuse "is why junkiejarvis went deaf" (`nostr-bridge.js:301-306`); the refresh default is now 3600000 ms because each tick replays the full filter history (`nostr-bridge.js:314-339`). AB-14.6: the decision waiter resolves only when the exact 31403 arrives through the consumer; otherwise "timeout fires … null, gate denies".
- Failure: the consumer reconnects with a reused id and goes deaf; every pending approval waits its full bounded timeout and then denies — governance outcomes are decided by timeout, not by the operator, potentially for an hour per cycle, with no error surfaced.
- Confidence: medium — the mechanism is stated; the denial consequence is implied by AB-14.6, not drawn as a scenario.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the deafness mechanics appear only as explanatory notes)

### F-11 — The custody hardening flag silently disables dream governance, and the identity port can never sign what the engine publishes
- Topics: AB-23.20, AB-23.17, AB-14.16
- Evidence: AB-23.20 Tension: the port's loader refuses kinds 31400-31405 to any key (`acl.rs:65-73`) yet dream-engine publishes the 31400 panel and 31402 cases signed as JunkieJarvis (`governance.rs:9-10`) — "As written, the port can never sign what AB-23.17 sends". Open: under role_isolation devuser cannot read ab-identity's 0400 key, and `load_key` "treats the refusal as fail-open and skips" (`governance.rs:920-928`); the W3b cutover is owed (`ADR-2122:244-245`).
- Failure: the operator turns on role_isolation to improve custody; the forum governance round trip stops publishing panels, cases and withdrawals with no error anywhere — open alerts and questions accumulate unseen until someone notices the panel is stale.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Open)

### F-12 — The SONA learning engine silently discards everything while reporting success
- Topics: AB-20.11
- Evidence: AB-20.11 DIVERGENCE D1: the prebuilt binary "HARDCODES embedding_dim = 256. A 384-dim learn returns status 'learned' but ACCUMULATES NOTHING — verified live"; the feeder's `fed` count is "acceptance by learn and NOT proof of accumulation" (`ruvector-sona-feeder.mjs:527-528`); the added `engine_accumulating` signal is the only honest reading.
- Failure: the learning loop runs nightly, reports "N accepted by learn", and discards every trajectory; operators believe the engine is accumulating patterns and nothing downstream will ever apply them — silent data loss of the entire learning investment.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE D1)

### F-13 — The shipped vault CLI lacks the very verbs the governed elevation path spawns
- Topics: AB-14.13, AB-22.11
- Evidence: AB-14.13 Drift (2026-10-30 close-out): the baked `vault 0.1.0` no longer carries `find`, `retrieve`, `tree`, `edit`, `create` or `apply`, which "moved to the ontomark CLI" (`ADR-2108:238`) — and "`vault propose`, the verb this section draws, is not in that list", while the code still builds and spawns `vault propose` argv as the 31402's `propose_command`.
- Failure: on the shipped image, every governed ontology elevation carries a command that fails at execution; the operator approves 31402 cards whose propose_command cannot run — the governed door is broken at the point of use, with the failure surfacing only when a human tries to apply an approved proposal.
- Confidence: high (stated at the stamped close-out; whether a rebuild has since fixed it is not in the material)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-14 — The system's own status surface and governing documents misstate what is enforced
- Topics: AB-14.8, AB-23.2, AB-16.9, AB-22.14, AB-14.5, AB-15.15, AB-23.22
- Evidence: AB-14.8 DOC-DRIFT: `routes/system.js:26,61` still calls the old `buildExecutionCoverage(live)` with `live={}`, so GET /v1/system "still reports status: declared always, even though a live instance now genuinely exists". AB-23.2 Drift: `GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:513` still says ADR-2071 is proposed while the journal is live. AB-16.9 Drift: the custody register still lists the retired AoE daemon. AB-22.14 Open: ADR-2150 is `proposed` yet the video-production skill is already baked, mapped and routable. Pattern: Jev approval-review, consult-advice, memory rerank and dream triage are configured `true` in the manifest but "staged until a rebuild" (AB-14.5, AB-15.15, AB-20.3, AB-23.22).
- Failure: an operator or auditor working from `/v1/system`, the governing docs or `agentbox.toml` believes enforcement states that are false in both directions — governance "declared" when live, consumers "on" when inert, an unaccepted skill routable. Every control assessment made against these artefacts assesses a fiction.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — each instance carries its own marker (DOC-DRIFT, Drift, Open, "staged"); the systemic declared-vs-running gap is not drawn as one problem.

### F-15 — Memory custody has no erasure propagation and no recovery point
- Topics: AB-20.7, AB-20.10, AB-20.12
- Evidence: AB-20.7 DIVERGENCE D5: the VisionClaw `deleteAgentMemory()` has "NO REVERSE TOMBSTONE into RuVector, so deleting the pod copy does not revoke the RuVector-held agent memory. No point-in-time RuVector backup exists … no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO for memory today". AB-20.10: bulk deletion "degrades the HNSW graph silently" and "nothing in the query path reports it" — the recall harness is the only detector.
- Failure: an erasure request or a store corruption event leaves the vector store serving deleted personal memory with no way to prove removal, and a routine `memory_sweep_episodic` can silently degrade recall with no signal until someone thinks to run the harness.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE D5; the index-law notes)

## Not judgeable from this material

- The code truth behind F-05: `nostr-live-mirror.cjs` and `egress-policy.cjs` at the running revision, to settle whether mirror egress is redacted or not.
- The full `voice/console/Caddyfile` and the Unmute frontend/backend auth behaviour, to settle whether ports 8444/8443 carry any authentication before the voice loop (F-04).
- Which container image is actually running in production relative to the "staged until a rebuild" manifest flags and the vault/ontomark cutover (F-13, F-14) — the corpus pins source revisions, not deployment state.
- The cloud relay and forum implementations (Durable-Object subscription persistence, kind-5 hard-delete semantics), which F-10 and AB-23.17's own OPEN note place outside these sources.
- The test suite's actual coverage: which fail-open/fail-closed postures drawn here (break-glass lifecycle, secret-backup restore on real keys, spend reconciliation, tmux fallback ordering) are pinned by tests versus asserted only in prose — the corpus cites tests for some paths (pipeline contract spec, recall harness) and none for others.

## agentbox#2 / premortem

**Post-mortem — agentbox estate incident (twelve months on).**
Basis: the diagram corpus at `verified_commit 6500582dfae23bf8a38a6942916d208154e43625` (plus the stamped dream-engine/ADR revisions it cites). Nothing else was inspected; every claim below carries the citation the material gives. All chains are reconstructions from stated defects — the realisation of any chain is inferred, the defects themselves are not.

---

### R-1 — The shipped same-UID boundary puts every signing key inside the reach of every prompt-injectable agent session
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.7, AB-16.13, AB-16.5, AB-16.4, AB-12.5, AB-13.5, AB-13.8, AB-14.14, AB-17.2
- Chain of events:
  1. The container ships with `[security].role_isolation` off; every supervised program, MCP server, skill and agent session runs as devuser uid 1000 (AB-16.1).
  2. With the flag off the boot's capture/scrub functions "return at their first line, so every other ROLE variable stays in PID 1 and reaches every child" (AB-16.13, `entrypoint-unified.sh:432`, `:476`); the bridge key file is 0400-devuser (`entrypoint-unified.sh:1111-1120`) and per-profile DID keys are 0600-devuser (`agent-identity.js:165-166`). Any same-uid peer "read — SUCCEEDS" on both (AB-16.7).
  3. The operator root Nostr key is inside that boundary: with the flag off `operatorKeyHex` reads `AGENTBOX_PRIVKEY_HEX` / `AGENTBOX_BRIDGE_SK` / `OPERATOR_NOSTR_PRIVKEY` from the environment (`operator-key.cjs:58`, AB-16.5), and the gateway's **default** identity is `'operator'`, i.e. it "holds and signs with the ROOT operator key" (AB-16.5, `gateway.cjs:155`). The mirror child secret is additionally returned to devuser by the identity port (`port.rs:635-637`, AB-16.5 TENSION).
  4. Text can be injected into live agent sessions from several ungated directions: `POST /tab0/send` types arbitrary clean text into the coordinator pane (AB-12.5), the nostr-gateway `/say` "broadcast sendKeys to every agentWindows" and free-form instructions route through one model call (`gateway.cjs:292-300`, `:526-579`), and any boot-time checkout is pre-trusted by trust-seed (AB-16.4) so repo content an agent reads is already inside the trust perimeter.
  5. One injected instruction needs only `env` or `cat`: keys, `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` (devuser-class env, AB-16.14) and the session socket (uid 1000 admitted by design, `session-plane-acl.json:8-11`) are all reachable, and exfiltration leaves via the sanctioned egress surfaces.
  6. With the operator key the attacker mints gift-wrapped commands whose only sender check is "sealed sender == commanderPub" (AB-13.5) — driving `/spawn`, `/exit` and fleet-wide `/say` — while post-hoc attribution fails, because a same-UID loopback peer "can stamp any pubkey into the journalled `agent_did`" (AB-14.14 Debt, TODO-unified X-1; same at AB-17.2 Debt).
- Evidence: AB-16.1 N4 ("each can read every other's 0600 key file and the mirror key, and uid 1000 is admitted to the session socket by design"); AB-16.7 alt "role_isolation off, the shipped default … PEER->KF: read — SUCCEEDS, same uid"; AB-16.13 O3/O4; AB-16.5 DIVERGENCE ("out of the box the gateway holds and signs with the ROOT operator key"); AB-14.14 and AB-17.2 both name "the same-UID boundary, TODO-unified X-1".
- Warning signs visible today: X-1 is named as an open TODO in two topics; the entire role-isolation, role-accounts and identity-port machinery (AB-16.13, AB-14.16) exists precisely because of this boundary and ships off; ADR-2122's boot rehearsal gate is still pending (AB-16.13, AB-36 reference).
- Confidence: high that the boundary and key reachability are exactly as stated; the exploitation chain is inferred (no event is recorded).
- Severity: high

### R-2 — One shared secret is simultaneously the voice backend's API key and the unbounded, unattributed break-glass identity bypass
- Topics: AB-16.6, AB-16.9, AB-12.2, AB-12.3, AB-12.5, AB-12.7, AB-12.9, AB-12.14
- Chain of events:
  1. `NIP98_PROXY_ALLOW_BEARER` is "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4" (AB-16.9 C3).
  2. That same value is distributed outward as the voice credential — the Unmute backend calls the bridge with "Authorization Bearer BRIDGE_TOKEN aka KYUTAI_LLM_API_KEY" (`voice/README.md:93`, AB-12.7) — inside the voice stack whose ports "8444 and 8443 are published 0.0.0.0 by docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41" (AB-12.7 DIVERGENCE).
  3. The break-glass branch: matching the token plus "unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime", "unset scope is unrestricted"; acceptance logs a fingerprint, never the token, and forwards a sentinel `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` that is "NOT a real pubkey, so every downstream attribution for this request is a placeholder" (`proxy.mjs:533-567`, AB-16.6). It is also accepted "via ?access_token= / ?bearer= on WS upgrades" (AB-16.6 DIVERGENCE note).
  4. So the voice container's ordinary working credential is, verbatim, a full-identity LAN bypass on port 9096 — and the tab0-bridge bearer, whose "every surface except /health requires the bearer … including /v1/chat/completions, /v1/models, /feed, /tab0/send, /nostr/*, /turns, /tabs*, /sessions" (AB-12.3 INVARIANT, ADR-044 finding 1).
  5. Through `/tab0/send` the holder types into the coordinator session (AB-12.5); through `/v1/chat/completions` the holder drives a `claude -p` child whose Bash allowlist includes "tmux and session-control.cjs list and send patterns" (`server.mjs:320-329`, AB-12.7); `/turns` and `/feed` expose the operator's last 50 turns (`server.mjs:685-687`); `/nostr/send` posts outbound under the mirror identity (AB-12.14).
- Evidence: AB-16.9 C3 equality; AB-12.7 token naming and `docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41`; AB-16.6 ADR-2027 open lifecycle ("matching the token alone is not sufficient … unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime; unset scope is unrestricted; durable per-use receipt is unproven"); AB-12.3 route-table invariant.
- Warning signs visible today: the material itself schedules the equality to persist "until Q4" with no gate drawn over it; ADR-2027 remains "decision proposed, implementation none, activation inactive"; break-glass is documented as "a full identity bypass while enabled" (AB-16.6).
- Confidence: high that the equality, capabilities and unbounded defaults are as stated; any specific compromise is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Client data leaves the estate unredacted by default: nightly repo content to Z.AI, operator prompts and governance content to the System One vendor
- Topics: AB-23.15, AB-13.10, AB-13.17, AB-16.11, AB-22.2, AB-20.3, AB-21.4, AB-14.5
- Chain of events:
  1. The dream engine's default provider is external: `llm_provider = "zai"` means "nightly repository content leaves the LAN on every unattended night" (AB-23.15 Debt, `agentbox.toml:2585`). No redaction step is drawn on this path — the shared egress-policy contract covers only the mirror and the kind-30840 digest (AB-13.17), and even the digest "still sends FLATTENED, now REDACTED transcript text to Z.AI" (AB-13.10).
  2. The skill router is jev by default (`agentbox.toml:1293`) and "a turn that routes at all and misses the cascade leaves the container" (AB-22.2 Debt) — raw prompt text to the System One judge, whose acceptance (ADR-2090) covers the prompt only; the OPEN question records that synced claude.ai skill descriptions now travel too (AB-22.2 Open).
  3. Once ADR-2155 activates, the memory-rerank, trajectory-classify and approval-review consumers all default to the proxy because `local_only` defaults to 0 (AB-20.3, AB-21.4 note, AB-14.5 note) — memory rows, judged trajectory patterns and pending governance approvals go to the selected vendor backend.
  4. The two session-egress paths deliberately differ — the mirror "composes UNREDACTED selected text" before wrapping while the digest redacts — so "there is no single answer to 'is session content leaving this box, and redacted how'" (AB-16.11).
  5. A client's code, prompt or approval content arrives at a third party with no per-tenant gate anywhere in the drawn path.
- Evidence: AB-23.15 Debt line verbatim; AB-22.2 Debt and Open lines verbatim; AB-13.17 "shared redaction fixtures do not imply equal recipient semantics"; AB-16.11 DIVERGENCE and its consequence note; AB-20.3 "default 0 … live once the new image is activated".
- Warning signs visible today: every one of these is recorded as Debt, Open or DIVERGENCE in the corpus; the egress fixture (`tests/fixtures/egress-redaction.v1.json`) covers mirror+digest only, not the dream or System One paths.
- Confidence: high that the default egress paths exist as drawn; medium that client-identifying data rides them (workload-dependent, inferred).
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — A spend-capable faucet treasury key sits on the host-visible workspace bind
- Topics: AB-16.14, AB-16.7
- Evidence: the manifest points at `/home/devuser/workspace/sidestr/agents/treasury.key` (`agentbox.toml:1926`); the Tension note: "a spend-capable key sits on a host-visible bind, outside the `agentbox-secrets` volume this topic's custody model inventories"; narrowed at `8f55d9a43` but "the migrate step copies the legacy file once and never deletes it … with the flag off the manifest still points the faucet at the bind" (`role-accounts.json:78`, ADR-2122:232-235).
- Failure: any devuser process — every agent session — reads the treasury key and signs spends; the legacy twin survives even after a volume cutover.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-02 — Secret backups are plaintext ZIPs in the workspace, and the encrypted backup tool is not in the running container
- Topics: AB-16.8, AB-16.9, AB-16.12
- Evidence: "the backup script invokes ordinary zip and unzip integrity testing with NO encryption flags and no explicit umask … The script was not run and no backup contents were inspected" (AB-16.8 BackedUp note); the artefact is "scripts/backup-secrets.sh to workspace/secret-backups ZIP + manifest" (AB-16.9 C7); the age-encrypted replacement's closeout says "The active container has not been rebuilt with this source change" and "SelfTest proves restore works on synthetic data only" (AB-16.12 N2/N4).
- Failure: an unencrypted secret archive on a bind readable by every devuser process; no tested recovery of real secrets.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes ("PARTIAL and unverified"; G17 closeout note)

### F-03 — Memory has no RPO/RTO: pod deletion has no reverse tombstone and no cross-store restore exists
- Topics: AB-20.7
- Evidence: "the VisionClaw Solid Pod deleteAgentMemory() has NO REVERSE TOMBSTONE into RuVector … No point-in-time RuVector backup exists (SQLite-only backup-sqlite.sh), so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO for memory today" (D5, cross-referencing `docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md`).
- Failure: a pod-side erasure or a RuVector data loss leaves the two stores permanently divergent; any right-to-erasure flow corrupts rather than complies.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-04 — Losing one JSONL file permanently orphans cross-repo provenance; the content-address scheme has no recovery
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.6, AB-17.10
- Evidence: execution/kg crossings mint a "FRESH sha256-12" with "no algebraic inverse. Recovery is store-dependent, so store loss is identity loss. No recovery receipt exists for the durable store" (AB-17.6); the store is one JSONL file, last-writer-wins, at `/var/lib/agentbox/code-harness/bc20-urn-mappings.jsonl` (AB-17.4); `roundTrips()` "proves the algebra, not the deployment" (AB-17.4); additionally the JS/Rust content-address "byte-parity asserted nowhere" (AB-17.10).
- Failure: a same-uid process truncates the file and every `urn:visionclaw:execution|kg` stops resolving to its agentbox origin, permanently; a byte-parity mismatch silently mints two addresses for one object.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, in three notes)

### F-05 — Agent identity persistence failure is non-fatal, so a session can run on a key that vanishes at restart
- Topics: AB-16.7 (see AB-11.2)
- Evidence: "persistence failure is NON-FATAL (`agent-identity.js:168-171`), so a container can run a whole session on an in-memory key that vanishes at restart. 'Key persistence failure can produce a valid but unstable identity.'"
- Failure: events signed under an identity that never persists break attribution, replay and any later verification of that session's provenance.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-06 — Publisher revocation requires a full image rebuild: the compromise window is one build-deploy cycle
- Topics: AB-16.8, AB-13.3
- Evidence: "The relay allowlist is baked at nix build (relayAllowedPubkeysCsv), so publisher revocation needs a full rebuild — the compromise window is one build-deploy cycle. ADR-2027 Context" (AB-16.8); "no runtime mutation path — no auto-add, no fallback" (AB-13.3).
- Failure: during an active key-compromise incident the revoked publisher remains admitted for the whole build+deploy duration, still writing to pods' inboxes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (marked "NOT IMPLEMENTED, and structurally expensive" in the state note)

### F-07 — Derived keys: bespoke HMAC, tag-bump "rotation" with no revocation, and the port hands the mirror child secret to devuser
- Topics: AB-16.5, AB-16.14
- Evidence: "Both are bespoke HMAC-SHA256 constructions rather than a standard KDF (HKDF), and rotation is 'bump the env tag' with no revocation of the previously derived child at any relay" (AB-16.5); the TENSION note records the port granting devuser `mirror_key` with the secret returned (`port.rs:635-637`), making "the phone's key … devuser-readable" — recorded as ADR-2101's one named export exception at agentbox `fd08841cd` (AB-16.14).
- Failure: a leaked child keeps reading the operator's self-DM thread indefinitely after a "rotation"; under the shipped flag the whole container can read it now.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE and TENSION)

### F-08 — The audit hash chain cannot detect deletion at the tail
- Topics: AB-14.9
- Evidence: "reorder is equivalent to a splice at the first moved record — deletion at the tail is the ONE mode a bare chain cannot see" (`audit-chain.js:13-16`); the JSONL records are ordinary devuser-writable files.
- Failure: an intruder with uid 1000 removes the most recent spend/authority rows; `verifyLines` passes over the truncated chain and `readTail` resumes from the new tail.
- Confidence: high (mechanism stated; the deletion attack is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a plain limitation note)

### F-09 — Every agent side-effect surface except POST /v1/tasks still bypasses the one policy pipeline
- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-14.14
- Evidence: "every OTHER surface above (direct tool call, code-mode, ACI shell, consultant, dream, beads, alt harness) still reaches EXEC with no interceptor — the gap is narrower, not closed" (AB-14.1 DIVERGENCE1, restating GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:339-342 as the TOP OPEN RISK).
- Failure: an injected instruction (R-1/R-2) executes mutation or egress through code-mode or a consultant with at most a spend cap — no approval, no journal, no capability token.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-10 — The unattended EXP-B8 tick takes outward side effects recorded by no journal
- Topics: AB-14.15 (see AB-23.19)
- Evidence: "the EXP-B8 tick takes two outward side effects when its stopping rule fires, a signed forum post (`scripts/experiments/exp-b8-label-log.cjs:550-563`) and a pull request opened with `gh` (`:573-606`), and records neither through POST /v1/exec/record or the authority journal".
- Failure: a 30-minute unattended cron (still riding the podcast crontab, AB-23.19 Debt) publishes and opens PRs that cannot be reconstructed, attributed or rolled back from any record.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-11 — Dream PR publication is not bound to the evaluated tree
- Topics: AB-23.6
- Evidence: "the PR request carries a branch name, not the verified commit hash" (`seams.rs:341`); "A successful create command returns Some even for empty stdout" (`seams.rs:531`); "The tested command boundary does not validate the URL or bind the pushed head to the evaluated tree".
- Failure: the branch head is moved (same-uid push) between gate and merge, and the operator merges content that was never gated, trusting an unverified URL.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — publication identity)

### F-12 — sidestr-wallet ships only a permissive SpendPolicy and nothing supplies the authority gate it defers to
- Topics: AB-15 (closing Drift note; see AB-33.7 referenced)
- Evidence: "sidestr-wallet consults a SpendPolicy before every signature and ships only a permissive implementation, leaving the authority gate (ADR-2100) to the caller, which nothing in this topic yet supplies".
- Failure: a signing wallet whose only spend control is "allow", reachable from the same-UID boundary that also holds its key file (AB-16.14), signs un-reviewed value-moving transactions.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-13 — Voice ports 8444/8443 are published 0.0.0.0 outside the only sanctioned, CI-gated LAN door
- Topics: AB-12.7
- Evidence: "port 8444 and port 8443 are published 0.0.0.0 by docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41, while only port 9096 is the ADR-045 D2 sanctioned NIP-98-gated LAN door covered by the loopback CI gate."
- Failure: whatever the console/Caddy/Unmute stack accepts on those ports is LAN-reachable with no NIP-98 gate and no CI gate watching it — the same stack that holds the R-2 token.
- Confidence: high (exposure stated; impact inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-14 — Recall correctness fails silently: the 2000-char embedding tail and undetected HNSW degradation
- Topics: AB-20.2, AB-20.3, AB-20.10
- Evidence: "bge-small embeds only the first EMBED_PREFIX_CHARS (2000) of a value — THE TAIL IS INVISIBLE TO SEARCH" (`memory-tools.js:57,:258`); the degraded ILIKE fallback returns literal matches at flat score 0.5 (`memory-tools.js:656-671`); "Recall drops with no error anywhere — nothing in the query path reports it, which is why the harness is the only detector" (AB-20.10), and the harness is a manual run.
- Failure: after a sweep or an embedding outage, agents act on confidently wrong or absent recall with no alert; facts stored past 2000 chars are systematically unfindable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as invariants/warnings, not flagged as Tension/Debt/Drift/Open)

### F-15 — Spend is accounted in three ledgers that never reconcile, and the zero-tolerance settlement class is never wired to the gate
- Topics: AB-15.6, AB-14.11
- Evidence: "spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile: the flock-guarded cost_cap ledger file, the pod-held sats balance … and the marketplace token_budget … No surface sums them, so no single number answers what this container has spent" (AB-15.6 DEBT); "the classification table names payment_settlement zero-tolerance … but no production path passes that actionClass to the gate … routes/payments.js never reaches management-api/lib/authority.js:226" (AB-14.11 DEBT).
- Failure: a cost overrun spread across the three stores goes undetected; an actual sats settlement executes with no governance gate at all.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, twice)

## agentbox#3 / critical

### F-01 — The custody boundary does not exist in production: every agent can read the sovereign, chain and break-glass keys today
- Topics: AB-36 (intro, .3, .7), AB-32.4, AB-34.6
- Evidence: ADR-2122 opens by stating that until it lands "every secret in the container reached every process... every long-running program ran as devuser (uid 1000), so a 0600 key file protected nothing from a co-resident agent". `[security].role_isolation` ships **false** (`agentbox.toml:2506`); ADR-2122 is `proposed / partial / inactive` (ADR-2122:5-7); the only committed rehearsal receipt is `STAGED` with the flag false (`docs/estate-closeout/x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:4,8`); with the flag off the boot chowns the secrets volume root to devuser (`entrypoint-unified.sh:557`), so `/var/lib/agentbox/secrets/` — sovereign Nostr key, `sidestr-dreamlab*.key` signer keys (AB-32.4), break-glass bearer — is devuser-readable.
- Failure: any compromised or simply mistaken agent tool reads the sovereign identity key (signs as the estate on Nostr/forum), the settlement-chain signer key (mints blocks, moves pegs), and the break-glass credential. No detection, no boundary.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, throughout AB-36)

### F-02 — The host Docker socket stays world-accessible and voids every container boundary, including role isolation
- Topics: AB-36.7
- Evidence: the socket "is a bind of the host inode that earlier boots widened, and skipping the chmod under the flag does not narrow it" (`docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`); a flag-on boot can only record `degraded:docker-socket` (`entrypoint-unified.sh:614`); ADR-2122 counts that marker as a failure (`ADR-2122:162`) while the host rehearsal "records the mode for the owner without gating on it" (`role-isolation-rehearsal.host.sh:18-19`).
- Failure: devuser — or any agent tool running as devuser — talks to the Docker socket, mounts the host root or starts a privileged container, and is host root: it reads the role-isolated secrets volume, every bind mount, and every key F-01 was meant to protect, from outside the container.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, R2)

### F-03 — Even with role isolation activated, 37 secrets including MANAGEMENT_API_KEY stay ambient in every agent process
- Topics: AB-36.6
- Evidence: `env-classes.json:90-92` — 37 DEVUSER_CLASS names "stay in devuser's ambient environment in BOTH modes" by the Q8 default, explicitly including `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, provider API keys, System One vendor keys and "the ssh-agent socket path that grants use of every key the agent holds" (`env-classes.json:195,203,211`; ADR-2122:178-179).
- Failure: after the full custody cutover, a compromised agent still lifts MANAGEMENT_API_KEY and drives the management API (payment-gated routes, config surfaces), BRIDGE_TOKEN, and every key loaded in the agent's ssh-agent. The isolation is partial by design and the exception list is the attack surface.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-04 — The spend-capable faucet treasury key remains on a host-visible bind, devuser-readable, in both modes
- Topics: AB-34.3, AB-36.3
- Evidence: "the migrate step copies that legacy file once and never deletes it, so a spend-capable key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge" (ADR-2122:232-235); the role table names the workspace file as the legacy twin (`role-accounts.json:78`); with the flag off the faucet defaults to the workspace path (`run-faucet.sh:27`, `agentbox.toml:1926`).
- Failure: any devuser process drains the DREAM/testnet treasury that pays member wallets; the exposure survives the role-isolation activation and every rebuild until someone remembers a manual purge.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, AB-34.3)

### F-05 — The mainnet containment gate is a field-presence check, while the record says mainnet cannot proceed until the gate "exists as code"
- Topics: AB-31.9, AB-32.2, AB-32.3
- Evidence: PRD-024 invariant — the mainnet phase "cannot begin until the regulatory gate exists as code rather than as an assertion" (`sovereign-settlement.md:329-330`); the genesis test's case 3 checks only that a mainnet parent *has* a `p21Receipt` field — "Resolving the receipt is NOT checked" (`sidechain-genesis.test.sh:86`); ADR-2103:229-230 records that the receipt-resolution CI check, the ban on level-1 chains over mainnet parents, and a node that refuses to open one are all unbuilt; and the seal established that genesis commits no parent or containment at all (ADR-2103:207-208) despite D3 claiming it does (:73-74).
- Failure: a chain document with a mainnet parent and a dangling `p21Receipt` passes CI and boots; the only barrier between the estate and real value is a JSON key, which is exactly the "assertion, not code" the record forbids.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (high the moment a mainnet parent is selected)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension AB-32.2, Debt AB-32.3) — but the record still calls the containment the precondition while shipping a stub

### F-06 — Two governing documents mandate different key hierarchies: PRD-024 derives spend keys from the identity key, the registry forbids it
- Topics: AB-31.7, AB-34.4, AB-35.5
- Evidence: PRD-024 derives `k_spend` and `k_sign` from `k_id` via `derive_subkey` (`sovereign-settlement.md:171-173`); the registry row, since the 2026-10-02 ADR-2097 D3 amendment, says the keys are "independent, never derived" (`PROTOCOL-registry.md:172`); AB-34.4's DR note records the contradiction; the invariant "the identity key never spends" (`INGRESS-identity.md:445`) and the known-answer test that `k_spend ≠ k_id` are both unrun (ADR-2101:205-209).
- Failure: whichever document the implementer follows, the other's security property breaks. Built per PRD, compromising `k_id` yields spending keys — identity compromise becomes theft. Built per registry, PRD's recovery path is wrong. Two compliance surfaces currently disagree about the estate's most sensitive key separation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, AB-34.4)

### F-07 — The governed-write door's attestation can be outrun: a pathspec-limited commit does not constrain the branch push
- Topics: AB-25.12
- Evidence: "A pathspec-limited commit does not constrain the later branch push to that commit. A second writer after the ahead-count check was [reproduced with real local Git]" (AB-25.12 Tension, 2026-10-31); the apply path records commit failure and never throws (`management-api/lib/ontology-apply.js:365,821`).
- Failure: two governed-write applications race; the first commits its attested pathspec, the second writer's commits ride the same branch push, and the corpus ships changes no signed kind-31403 decision covers — while the attestation ledger asserts it didn't. The human-approval loop is the system's write control; this bypasses it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, AB-25.12)

### F-08 — Genesis commits no parent: the chain's parent and containment claims are unverifiable from the chain itself
- Topics: AB-32.1, AB-32.2, AB-34.2
- Evidence: buildGenesis commits only the chain id, pegs, genesisTime and the signer witness; parent, signers and every containment field are NOT committed (ADR-2103:206-208); "Until the pin exists, the binding of parent and containment is the committed document plus its genesisHash" (`sidechain/README.md:77`); the client rule trusts a mirror whose document signer matches the announcement author and whose tip is not ahead (AB-34.2).
- Failure: the signer-key holder (devuser-readable today, per F-01) republishes a variant `chain.json` claiming a different parent or containment with the same genesisHash; clients that verify block-0 hash and signer match accept both documents as "the chain". The parent claim — the exact field the P21 gate keys on — can be rewritten without touching the chain.
- Confidence: high that the parent is uncommitted (stated); the dual-document acceptance path is inferred from the stated client rule
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension AB-32.2) — the mirror-trust consequence is not marked

### F-09 — The email gateway looks like a sixth, hand-rolled facade client that ADR-2084 prohibits
- Topics: AB-24.1, AB-24.9, AB-24.10, AB-24.11, AB-28.13
- Evidence: ADR-2084's invariant — "hand-rolled chat-completions construction against a facade is PROHIBITED - a sixth caller takes the crate" (`ADR-2084:37`); the five crate callers are enumerated as dream-engine, podcast-ingest (×2), explainer loom-draft and web-summary (AB-28.13); yet the email gateway consumes the facade directly at `REASONER_BASE_URL http://loom:8080/v1` (`loom/README.md:19-21`, AB-24.9) and appears in no list of crate consumers anywhere in the material.
- Failure: if the gateway builds its own requests (the material never shows it using loom-client), it inherits precisely the traps ADR-2084 catalogued: a sub-1536-token ask returns EMPTY content read as success, and a scaffolded corpus answer about the wrong subject — the ADR-139 incident class — is emailed out as established fact.
- Confidence: medium; inferred — no source states which client the gateway uses
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — The grounding hook mandates searching a store measured at 9–11% recall before agents assert
- Topics: AB-26.8
- Evidence: the hook injects "a GROUNDING directive to call search_ruvnet before asserting" plus up to two redirect lines steering classical substitutes (pinecone→ruvector, langchain→ruflo); the same notes state ruvnet-kb and knowledge-* namespaces are "LOW-RECALL — scoped R@10 about 9-11 percent — until candidate-bounded hybrid lands", that `memory_hybrid_search` on ruvnet-kb "measured about 72 s", and that the namespace is capped at ~40% of the recall harness.
- Failure: agents obey the injected directive, search a store that misses roughly nine of ten relevant chunks (or time out at 72 s), then confidently assert upstream-library internals or adopt a redirected estate substitute on the strength of "grounded" evidence — wrong technical claims produced faster and with more confidence than no grounding at all.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE notes, AB-26.8)

### F-11 — ComfyUI cancellation can kill the wrong job: the ID-bearing interrupt crosses a snapshot race
- Topics: AB-38.3
- Evidence: the client POSTs an interrupt with the owned `prompt_id` (`comfy_client.py:325`), but the pinned upstream handler "checks a snapshot before signalling"; the audit reproduced "owned A finishes; B starts" and "upstream global interrupt can reach B"; the request acknowledgement "is not terminal history" (`comfy_client.py:309,366`); the fix (atomic per-job cancellation route) is marked REQUIRED.
- Failure: an operator cancels render A; between snapshot and signal A completes and an unrelated queued job B starts; B is killed mid-run — lost GPU-hours and a corrupted or missing segment in the production video pipeline, while the cancel is reported acknowledged.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (CONTROLLED/GAP, AB-38.3)

### F-12 — A TUI save silently resets hand-edited router spend controls to baked literals
- Topics: AB-28.10, AB-29.8
- Evidence: `tui_sections.rs:193-200` emits every non-enabled `[model_routing.neural]` key as a fixed literal (provider "openrouter", quality_bar 0.50, `cost_ceiling_usd_per_mtok 0`, trajectory, assets_dir); "a TUI save therefore RESETS provider/quality_bar/cost_ceiling/trajectory/assets_dir to these literals even if the operator hand-edited agentbox.toml" — no read-back-and-preserve path, unlike the ADR-2031 consultant-model rule.
- Failure: an operator hand-tightens the cost ceiling or quality bar for a sensitive period; the next unrelated TUI edit silently reverts them. If `0` is read as "no ceiling" (the material does not state the interpretation), the spend cap on the OpenRouter-billing console is removed by a save that touched nothing billing-related.
- Confidence: high on the reset (stated); the "0 = unlimited" reading is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, AB-28.10) — the spend-control consequence is not marked

### F-13 — Manifest-true but runtime-false: at least four gates are configured in config and exported off in code until an image rebuild, with no divergence alarm
- Topics: AB-24.6, AB-25.8, AB-26.1, AB-28.2
- Evidence: Jev proposal verification is "off in code, configured and staged until a rebuild" (AB-25.8); headroom evidence ranking is "staged until a rebuild, so not live" (AB-26.1); Loom-side Jev is "configured, live when the loom service is recreated and the new agentbox image is activated" (AB-24.6); the System One shell projection is "configured: live at the next image activation, ADR-2155 staged" (AB-28.2). No mechanism is shown that reports a gate the manifest declares on but the running image exports off.
- Failure: an operator reads `agentbox.toml`, sees the verification and evidence layers enabled, and believes proposal verification, evidence ranking, Jev gating and vendor isolation are active — while the running image has them off indefinitely, because nothing pairs the manifest with the built image's actual exports.
- Confidence: high (each instance stated); the systemic absence of an alarm is my synthesis
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — each instance is marked "staged"; the declared-vs-effective divergence risk as a class is not

### F-14 — The HP Loom door reports green while serving nothing or stale grounding: dead route, fail-open reconcile, and a baked vault too old to promote
- Topics: AB-24.7, AB-24.13, AB-24.15
- Evidence: "HP's old 192.168.2.48 is DEAD — a stale model-backend route black-holes every synthesis while /health still answers" (AB-24.7 note); the boot reconcile is background, fail-open, and "unreachable, deferred, refused and failed never stop a container" — exit 0 (`hp-loom-reconcile.sh:13-15`, `entrypoint-unified.sh:1209-1219`); as of 2026-10-02 the door serves `visionGraph@ae913f93…` while `015ca2c1f` is built and promoted, and the baked `vault` 0.1.0 "cannot produce a promotable bundle" until an image rebuild (AB-24.13 Debt).
- Failure: dream nights and ontology condense ground every answer on a stale corpus — or produce nothing through the dead route — for an unbounded period while `/health` returns 200 and the boot reconcile exits 0. An indefinite wrong-or-absent-grounding outage with no alarm.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE note AB-24.7, Debt AB-24.13; the fail-open-while-green combination as a monitoring gap is not marked)

### F-15 — The egress compliance register cites lines that have drifted empty, and its CI check can never catch it
- Topics: AB-24.14
- Evidence: the register is stamped `0919dc39a` (`egress-policy.json:51`) and its loom-door and zai-night rows cite manifest lines 2179 and 2163 and flake line 3438 (`egress-policy.json:231-232,355`); at this revision line 2179 is the loom-raw seed's `slug` key, 2163 is empty, `llm_provider` is `agentbox.toml:2585` and the façade default is `flake.nix:3957`; the renderer's `--check` "only tests that a cited line falls inside the file" (`render-egress-register.js:110-119`).
- Failure: the prompt-egress artifact a privacy review signs against points auditors at empty lines and wrong gates after any manifest growth; because the check is bounds-only, the register's claims about what leaves the estate silently stop tracking reality between stamps — and the material shows 300+ lines of drift already accumulated.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, AB-24.14)

## Not judgeable from this material

- Which HTTP client the email gateway actually uses against `http://loom:8080/v1` — the published loom-client crate or its own construction (decides F-09).
- `secret-backup`'s fixed filename allowlist (`main.rs:58,66,178`) against the full role-accounts secrets inventory — which keys (chain signer, sovereign, faucet treasury) have a tested off-host restore; AB-37.3 shows new filenames are silently omitted.
- Network exposure of the sidecars: xr-runtime's `x11vnc -nopw` mirroring on VNC 5904 (AB-27.13) and code-server bound `0.0.0.0:8080` reachable bridge-side (AB-27.6) — compose port publishes and firewall posture are not shown.
- The current mode of the host Docker socket on the production host (AB-36.7 records it but does not gate it) — whether the earlier widening has been reversed out-of-band.
- Where identity-port receipts persist and their disk coupling — `port.rs:324` makes every signing operation fail-closed if the receipt log is unwritable, so a full receipts volume would take down NIP-42 auth, pods signing and the gateway at once.

## agentbox#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — custody/data failure, twelve months on

Scope note: every claim below is taken from, or directly implied by, the estate's own diagrammed record and its `path:line` citations. Where I reason past what the material asserts, Confidence says "inferred". Nothing here has been re-verified against live code; each item must be checked before action.

---

### R-1 — Spend-capable and sovereign keys remain readable by every process in the container, because role isolation is staged but not activated
- Topics: AB-34.3, AB-34.6, AB-36.3, AB-36.6, AB-36.7
- Chain of events: an agent tool or a dependency it loads runs as devuser (uid 1000) → with `[security].role_isolation = false` (the shipped state), supervisord passes PID 1's environment to every child and every long-running program runs as devuser, so the sovereign key variable is ambient, `/run/secrets` is chowned 1000:1000, and the chain programs run as devuser reading their keys directly → the tool reads the faucet treasury key (spend-capable), the sidestr signer keys, `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, `BRIDGE_TOKEN` and the ssh-agent socket that authorises use of every key the agent holds → funds are drained via the faucet, blocks are signed by an impostor, or the management API is driven with its own key. The designed mitigation (the identity port, per-role uids) is not live: the flag is off, the port's ACL declares only two keys, and the consumer cutover (gateway, dream-engine, JunkieJarvis, live-mirror) is still owed.
- Evidence: AB-36 intro — "every secret in the container reached every process… a 0600 key file protected nothing from a co-resident agent (AB-16.1, AB-16.7)"; flag ships false at `agentbox/agentbox.toml:2506`; ROLE-class env names are "captured to files and unset under the flag only" (`agentbox/config/entrypoint-unified.sh:419`, `agentbox/config/custody/env-classes.json:33`), so the sovereign key stays ambient with the flag off; with the flag off `/run/secrets` is "chown 1000:1000, chmod 0700 as before" (`agentbox/config/entrypoint-unified.sh:396`) and chain programs "run user=devuser" (`agentbox/flake.nix:3107`, `agentbox/flake.nix:3142`); the faucet treasury key defaults to the workspace path (`agentbox/config/sidechain/run-faucet.sh:27`, set at `agentbox/agentbox.toml:1926`) and its legacy twin is "never deleted and stay[s] devuser-readable by design until a manual purge" (`ADR-2122-role-service-accounts-run-secrets-and-the-identity-port.md:232-235`); 37 DEVUSER_CLASS names including `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, `BRIDGE_TOKEN` and the ssh-agent socket remain in every devuser process by exception Q8 (`agentbox/config/custody/env-classes.json:90-92`, `:195`, `:203`, `:211`); the only committed rehearsal receipt is verdict STAGED with flag false (`agentbox/docs/estate-closeout/x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:4`, `:8`); the port's grants declare only core and junkiejarvis (`agentbox/config/custody/identity-port-acl.json:17-30`) and W3b consumers are owed (`ADR-2122…md:244-245`).
- Warning signs visible today: the material's own business text — "the stored copies of those keys are still readable from inside the box" (AB-34, For the business); the flag's activation depends on an owner's flag-on PASS receipt that does not exist; the catalogue already warns a boot-only flip degrades to `degraded:devuser-sudo` (`agentbox/management-api/lib/system-manifest.js:97-98`).
- Confidence: high — the exposure is stated outright by the record; the specific exploit chain (which tool, which key first) is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Two writers race on the governed corpus: the pathspec-limited commit does not constrain the later branch push, and push failure is recorded, never thrown
- Topics: AB-25.12, AB-04.13 (referenced), AB-24.13, AB-24.15
- Chain of events: a signed forum decision reaches `applyOntologyDecision` → the apply path makes a pathspec-limited commit and passes its ahead-count check → a second writer (a second applied decision, or any other committer to the same branch) commits after the check and before/during the ordinary branch push → the first push now carries the second writer's commit, or is rejected; either way "commit failure recorded, never thrown" means the caller is told success semantics while the corpus and the signed decision record diverge → the corpus the Loom door serves (`agentbox/scripts/hp-loom-reconcile.sh:142` builds from a clean worktree at HEAD) and the chain-hashed attest ledger (`loom/crates/loom-facade/src/routes/attest.rs:113`) now describe different histories → consumers ground on, or clients are billed against, a corpus whose content does not match what was approved.
- Evidence: AB-25.12 Tension (2026-10-31) — "A pathspec-limited commit does not constrain the later branch push to that commit. A second writer after the ahead-count check was [reproduced with real local Git]" (`estate-review/2026-10-31-corpus-consumer.md#commit-and-attestation-boundaries`); the apply path is "pathspec-limited commit, then ordinary branch push (commit failure recorded, never thrown)" at `agentbox/management-api/lib/ontology-apply.js:365` and `:821`.
- Warning signs visible today: the race is not hypothetical — it was reproduced with real local Git and the topic was explicitly "qualified" by it; multiple independent writers exist (the apply door, and any operator committing to the same branch the reconcile then builds from).
- Confidence: high that the race exists (reproduced); medium, inferred, that it is this incident's cause rather than a co-factor.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Client material leaves to a vendor LLM by default with no accepted egress record, and the register that should catch it only checks line bounds
- Topics: AB-24.9, AB-24.14, AB-23 (referenced), AB-26.1, AB-28.2
- Chain of events: the dream engine's default provider is Z.AI, a vendor (`agentbox/agentbox.toml:2585`; night provider defaults to zai at `agentbox/services/dream-engine/src/config.rs:184`) → nightly and default-path jobs send prompts and corpus-derived content off-estate → the egress register's own row for this carries marker `no-accepted-record` — "nothing accepts the corpus leaving" (`agentbox/config/egress-policy.json:346-361`) → when reviewed, the register's evidence is stale anyway: it is stamped `0919dc39a` (`egress-policy.json:51`) and cites manifest/flake lines that have since shifted by 300+ lines (`egress-policy.json:231-232`, `:355`), while its renderer's `--check` only tests that a cited line falls inside the file (`agentbox/scripts/ci/render-egress-register.js:110-119`) → a client's data leaves to a third party with no acceptance record and no working tripwire; separately, staged Jev/System One consumers flip to cloud backends (TypeSafe) at the next image rebuild because `[system_one].local_only` is empty (`agentbox/services/agentbox-manifest/src/system_one.rs:522-536,564-577`; the loom sidecar's Jev reaches the proxy with `LOOM_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD=1`, `docker-compose.unified.yml:387-395`), so a routine rebuild widens egress with no gate edit.
- Evidence: as cited above; AB-24.9 N1 records the Z.AI default as a deliberate resolved choice (ADR-2053), which conflicts with the register's `no-accepted-record` marker — the governance statement and the compliance artefact disagree.
- Warning signs visible today: the `no-accepted-record` marker is live in the register; the demonstrated citation drift with a bounds-only checker; "staged until a rebuild" Jev options in three separate topics (AB-24.6, AB-25.8, AB-26.1).
- Confidence: medium-high — the egress state and the checker weakness are stated; the incident linkage is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Compressed bead payloads reference originals in a 30-minute TTL, 1000-entry LRU store
- Topics: AB-26.2, AB-26.5, AB-26.9
- Evidence: the beads compression slot is on (`agentbox/agentbox.toml:2471`); originals live in the CCR store with `ttl_minutes = 30`, `max_entries = 1000` and LRU eviction (`agentbox/agentbox.toml:2462-2464`; `agentbox/crates/headroom-napi/src/ccr_store.rs`), and `headroom_retrieve` errors on expiry/eviction (`agentbox/management-api/lib/headroom-mcp-tools.js:34` path). Nothing in the material shows a bead-lifetime guarantee shorter than the CCR TTL.
- Failure: a bead closed with a compressed payload (outcome or tags carrying CCR sentinels) becomes permanently unretrievable 30 minutes later or under load-driven eviction — silent, irreversible data loss in the work-DAG record.
- Confidence: medium — the components are stated; the persistence of sentinels inside stored bead records is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-02 — The host Docker socket stays world-accessible; the flag can only record the degradation
- Topics: AB-36.7, AB-36.1
- Evidence: `agentbox/docker-compose.override.yml:116-117` — the socket is a bind of a host inode earlier boots widened, and skipping the chmod under the flag does not narrow it; flag-on boot "can only detect and record `degraded:docker-socket`" (`agentbox/config/entrypoint-unified.sh:614`); the host rehearsal half records the mode without gating (`agentbox/scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.host.sh:18-19`) while ADR-2122 counts it as a failure (`ADR-2122…md:162`); uid 965 is reserved because the Docker daemon is root on the host (`agentbox/config/role-accounts.json:6`).
- Failure: devuser (or any co-resident process) reaches the host daemon and becomes root on the host, past every in-container boundary.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Flag-off image builds bake devuser into wheel with a NOPASSWD sudo drop-in
- Topics: AB-36.7, AB-36.2
- Evidence: built with `role_isolation` off (how it ships), devuser stays in `wheel` (`agentbox/flake.nix:4045`) and a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in is baked (`agentbox/config/bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`, called at `agentbox/flake.nix:4059`); the grant is inert only because `no-new-privileges` is set (`agentbox/docker-compose.yml:129`) and no setuid sudo ships (`agentbox/flake.nix:3812-3816`); "Reverting either guard on a flag-off build turns devuser back into root, past every role boundary."
- Failure: any future change that reintroduces a setuid sudo binary or drops the compose guard silently reactivates an unconditional root grant for the uid every agent runs as.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — `SIDESTR_ALLOW_UNPINNED=1` reopens mutable consensus code with no bound on duration
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: with the override set, the producer runs the workspace checkout "at any commit" (`agentbox/config/sidechain/run-producer.sh:85`), the unpinned state is only warned ("warns, never refuses", `run-producer.sh:86-93`), and the record states "DEBT: while the override is set, the exposure the bake closed is back, a dirty checkout runs, and nothing bounds how long it stays set."
- Failure: a devuser-writable, possibly dirty, consensus tree runs the chain's block production indefinitely; the invariant that the producer "executes only consensus code its own user cannot modify" (`run-producer.sh:9-16`) is bypassed by one environment variable.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — The live level-2 round runs on ephemeral Nostr kinds the review rejected for safety-critical traffic
- Topics: AB-35.3, AB-34.5, AB-34.4
- Evidence: kinds 23510–23514 are "EPHEMERAL under NIP-01: relays are not expected to retain them" and `chain` is not a portable indexed filter (`REVIEW-kofn-consensus-gpt6-astra.md:217`); the corrected profile (stored kinds, certificates persisted locally and re-fetched by digest) is required but unallocated (`ADR-2101-federation-topology-and-key-separation.md:162-168`; `agentbox/docs/PROTOCOL-registry.md:167`); meanwhile "Level 2 usable: sidestr-round 0.1.0's cosign co-signs with the JS signers live" (`agentbox/docs/proposals/sovereign-settlement.md:336`) and sidestr-round ships the availability-tolerant round, "not the reviewed BFT shape" (AB-35 drift note).
- Failure: relay eviction or restart silently drops round messages and there is no durable certificate store to re-publish from — lost votes and unreconstructable finality on a live signing path.
- Confidence: medium — the wire kinds and the live round are both stated; that the live round uses exactly those kinds is implied, not quoted.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — Secret-backup's fixed filename allowlist omits new custody keys, so they are never backed up
- Topics: AB-37.3, AB-36.3
- Evidence: backup scans a configured root (`agentbox/services/secret-backup/src/main.rs:154,156`) against a fixed filename allowlist (`main.rs:58,66,178`) and skips every other basename (`main.rs:179`); the audit demonstrated that "Synthetic federation-key filenames are omitted even inside the scan root"; the proposed independent node key lives under role secrets (`agentbox/docs/PROTOCOL-registry.md:255`) and role runtime copies live under `/run/secrets/<role>/` (`agentbox/config/lib/role-custody.sh:218`), outside any stated allowlist entry.
- Failure: a chain signer, faucet treasury or mesh key is created per the custody design, never enters a backup, and a single disk loss makes the key — and whatever it controls — unrecoverable.
- Confidence: high for the demonstrated omission; inferred for whether a specifically named estate key is affected.
- Marked by authors: no (the audit panel marks it REQUIRED, not Tension/Debt/Drift/Open)
- Severity: high

### F-07 — The corpus door serves a stale generation because the baked vault binary cannot promote a new bundle until an image rebuild
- Topics: AB-24.13, AB-24.15
- Evidence: "the next generation `visionGraph@015ca2c1f` is built, promoted and verified, but the door still serves `visionGraph@ae913f93…`, and the `vault` 0.1.0 baked into the agentbox image predates the portable-records file, so it cannot produce a promotable bundle" (`loom/docs/design/ADR-141-loom-consumes-the-vault-build.md:141-150`); the source pin is fixed (`agentbox/flake.nix:31-32`, vaultSrc `f3bbb430ea`) "but that reaches a running `vault` only at the next image rebuild."
- Failure: every consumer grounds on a corpus that is at least one generation behind what was approved, while the reconcile reports success at the HEAD it can see; answers are confidently stale.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-08 — The mainnet containment gate is a field-presence check: `p21Receipt` existing is enough
- Topics: AB-32.3, AB-32.5
- Evidence: "case 3 enforces only that a mainnet parent carries a `p21Receipt` field. ADR-2103 D4 asks for a CI check that the receipt *resolves*, a second check forbidding a level-1 chain on a mainnet parent, and a node that refuses to open one; none is built" (`ADR-2103…md:229-230`; `agentbox/tests/config/sidechain-genesis.test.sh:86`).
- Failure: a mainnet-variant chain document with any string in `p21Receipt` passes CI; real value moves onto a chain whose legal/regulatory containment was never verified.
- Confidence: high that the check is a stub; the failure requires mainnet to be engaged, which is gated today.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-09 — A stale model-backend route on the HP black-holes every synthesis while `/health` still answers
- Topics: AB-24.7, AB-24.1
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE: HP's old 192.168.2.48 is DEAD — a stale model-backend route black-holes every synthesis while /health still answers" (AB-24.7 note).
- Failure: consumers' completions silently disappear behind a green healthcheck after a model move; monitoring shows a healthy door while all delegated work is lost.
- Confidence: high that the trap exists as described.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift/DIVERGENCE note)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — The live façade lacks the loaded identity fields the stricter client needs, and a new generation report does not reload the corpus
- Topics: AB-24 (Audit qualification 2026-09-07), AB-24.3, AB-24.4
- Evidence: "the live façade was probed and still reports lexical generation 2026-08-22 versus semantic 2026-08-17, without loaded identity/embedding fields. A coordinated bundle/server rollout is required before activating the stricter client"; "The graph loader still uses the shared default graph, so ADR-2073 remains open. Generation reporting is not an automatic corpus reload."
- Failure: identity-verified retrieval stays unactivatable, so cache discriminators and response-identity checks run weaker than designed; a published generation change leaves the served bytes unchanged with no signal.
- Confidence: high (probed live).
- Marked by authors: no (Audit qualification section, not a Tension/Debt/Drift/Open marker)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — The setup default seed ships learning and SEO-watch enabled while claiming every learning/hygiene gate is off
- Topics: AB-30.3
- Evidence: the seed comment says all gates ship off (`agentbox/setup/server/src/main.rs:70-72`, `agentbox/setup/agentbox.default.toml:5-6`) but the shipped default sets `[memory_learning] enabled = true` (`agentbox.default.toml:451`) and `[seo_watch] enabled = true` (`agentbox.default.toml:845`).
- Failure: a fresh-install operator, told nothing is on, has client material learned/retained and outbound SEO queries made from the first boot — a retention/egress mismatch discovered only after the fact.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — Staged Jev and System One consumers switch to cloud backends at the next image rebuild, not at a visible gate
- Topics: AB-26.1, AB-24.6, AB-25.8, AB-28.2
- Evidence: `[system_one].local_only` is empty by default, so Jev consumers "export *_LOCAL_ONLY=0… so it takes the proxy and TypeSafe" (`agentbox/services/agentbox-manifest/src/system_one.rs:528,564-577`); the loom sidecar's Jev reaches the agentbox proxy with `LOOM_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD=1` — "configured, live when the loom service is recreated and the new agentbox image is activated" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:387-395`, `loom/crates/loom-facade/src/jev.rs:230-243`); the shell projection is eval'd a second time before supervisord (`agentbox/config/entrypoint-unified.sh:1260-1263`).
- Failure: an unrelated rebuild silently turns on vendor-backed evaluation over prompt-bearing content (headroom excerpts, ontology proposals, loom retrieval) with no manifest gate changing.
- Confidence: high for the staging and activation trigger; medium, inferred, for treating TypeSafe as an off-estate vendor.
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — The identity port authorises account + kind, not workload or catalogue semantics, and its receipts are append-only
- Topics: AB-36.4, AB-36.5, AB-37.2
- Evidence: authorisation is peer uid plus a closed op list (`agentbox/services/nostr-pod-bridge/src/identity_port/port.rs:361,362,378`) with a kind-in-grant check that passes caller tags, content and timestamp through unchanged (`port.rs:537`, `:559-564`); the audit showed "a synthetic 30078 grant signs an unrelated namespace with no revision" and that UID authorisation requires an exclusive account to identify one workload; receipts are appended before answering (`port.rs:320,326,328`; `receipts.rs:132,136`) and the audit qualifies them as "not a crash-durable protocol inbox" (AB-36.4).
- Failure: once the port is live with additional grants, any process under an authorised uid signs arbitrary content in an allowed kind, and a crash between signing and receipt flush leaves an admission with no record.
- Confidence: high (demonstrated synthetically).
- Marked by authors: no (audit panel, not a Tension/Debt/Drift/Open marker)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Missing roles config or delivery plan fails open to the insecure shape, with a log marker only
- Topics: AB-36.2, AB-36.3
- Evidence: if the roles config or plan is absent, boot proceeds as flag-off — "ROLE-ISOLATION-UNAVAILABLE: boot as flag off" (`agentbox/config/entrypoint-unified.sh:365`); "An image that lacks either artefact boots as if the flag were off, and says so loudly" (AB-36.2); secret delivery "fails open with a counted, grep-able marker" (`agentbox/config/lib/role-custody.sh:11-14`).
- Failure: an operator flips the flag expecting the custody boundary, a partial image or a failed delivery silently boots the today's-insecure shape, and nothing blocks the container from serving.
- Confidence: high (stated design).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-15 — ComfyUI job cancellation is not atomic: a global interrupt can kill the successor job, and request acknowledgement is not terminal history
- Topics: AB-38.3
- Evidence: the client POSTs an interrupt with `prompt_id` (`agentbox/skills/comfyui/scripts/comfy_client.py:325`) but the pinned upstream handler checks a running snapshot before signalling (upstream `server.py#L1258`); the audit reproduces "owned A finishes; B starts → upstream global interrupt can reach B"; "Request acknowledgement is not terminal history" (`comfy_client.py:309,366`); the MCP layer's running-job cancellation is at `agentbox/skills/comfyui/mcp-server/server.js:149`.
- Failure: cancelling one job destroys a different, unrelated running job — lost render work and possibly mis-attributed billing, with no terminal confirmation for either.
- Confidence: high (reproduced as a controlled case).
- Marked by authors: no (labelled GAP/CONTROLLED/REQUIRED, not one of the four markers)
- Severity: medium

## dream-engine / critical

Ranked by production damage: security first, then data loss/custody, correctness, operability. Where the authors' own markers (Tension, Drift, Open) cover a finding I say so; eleven of the fifteen are unmarked.

### F-01 — rustfmt can write files outside the candidate worktree, invisibly to every check
- Topics: DE-02.2, DE-01 (header "Open" paragraph)
- Evidence: The `#[path` guard is a literal substring check (`candidate.rs:115`), which misses valid `#[ path` syntax; the audit's controlled probe had rustfmt follow an absolute path attribute to a temporary canary outside the worktree and change it, while detection relies on git status inside the worktree (`candidate.rs:147`), which cannot see external writes. Formatting runs as a local process on the control plane (`candidate.rs:129-131`), and the later re-guard inspects only the git diff (`candidate.rs:206`).
- Failure: A model-authored candidate contains a spaced `#[ path = "/abs/path"` attribute; rustfmt rewrites that host file during candidate preparation; no status check, guard, or receipt records the write. Host/control-plane files are modified by unreviewed model output, with no evidence trail.
- Confidence: high — the counterexample was executed and reproduced against production source.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes — Tension (ADR-DE-009 confinement), which notes the documented out-of-worktree-write closure is contradicted by the probe.

### F-02 — No step of push, draft creation, or merge is tied to the evaluated commit
- Topics: DE-04.3, DE-04.2
- Evidence: `DraftPr` names only checkout, slug and branch — "no expected commit field" (`seams.rs:341`); the merge command "has no expected-head guard" (`seams.rs:481`); the audit states "Neither the request nor merge command carries the evaluated commit hash."
- Failure: Between evaluation and auto-merge, the branch is advanced or rewritten (by another actor, or by an evaluator per F-03); the squash merge publishes and merges a head that was never evaluated, and nothing in the retained record ties the merged commit to the recorded tree hash. (The audit explicitly does not claim this happened live — the absent guard is what is stated.)
- Confidence: high that the guard is absent; the branch-movement scenario is inferred from that absence.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — Evaluators can modify the working tree after the recorded hash, and nothing re-checks
- Topics: DE-02.2
- Evidence: The tree hash is recorded before build/evaluate (`candidate.rs:546-558`) and "`candidate::run` returns the prior hash without a post-evaluation tree check"; DE-02.2 states plainly "Evaluators can also modify working files after the recorded tree hash."
