- Failure: A test run as part of an ACCEPTed candidate rewrites source files in the worktree; the run's evidence (hash, receipts, gate inputs) describes a tree that no longer exists, and whatever is later committed or pushed from that worktree carries unreviewed modifications under a verdict certifying the pre-evaluation tree.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated only in "Why it is this way" prose)

### F-04 — Crash after the Persisted marker silently swallows an accepted experiment's publication
- Topics: DE-03.1
- Evidence: The verdict is recorded at Persisted before forge and ledger (`runstate.rs:615`); on restart a matching Persisted record is finalised (`runstate.rs:415`); the process-kill probe showed that a kill after Persisted "returned AlreadyComplete despite a different run ID." DE-03's business note concedes the trade: "possible missing drafts for fewer duplicate attempts."
- Failure: The host is killed between the Persisted write and `Forge` publication; on restart the night reports AlreadyComplete, the draft PR is never created, no error is raised, and no record says publication was skipped. Accepted work is lost with a clean bill of health.
- Confidence: high — reproduced with a real fixture process kill.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (acknowledged in prose, no marker)

### F-05 — The pure gate accepts on an empty required set, with config-digest checking off by default
- Topics: DE-02.3, DE-02.4
- Evidence: ACCEPT requires only "claimed and no vetoes" (`gate.rs:489`); "An empty required set does not itself veto acceptance"; "`GateContext::new` omits config-digest checking"; the gate does not prove the candidate tree exists. Replay, the ostensible backstop, "checks consistency of retained inputs — not execution authenticity or tamper resistance" (`replay.rs:10`).
- Failure: A config regression leaves all evaluators optional (or the caller bypasses readiness, which is the component that rejects empty evaluator sets per DE-02.1); candidates are ACCEPTed on zero required evidence, undetected because the digest check is off, and the merge path (F-02) proceeds on untested changes.
- Confidence: high for the stated gaps; the misconfiguration scenario is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Admission checks file existence, not tracking — an untracked script passes, and evaluators can plant one
- Topics: DE-02.1, DE-02.2 (for the chain)
- Evidence: Readiness inspects "script existence" (`readiness.rs:241`); "an untracked script can pass admission" because the check "does not verify tracking at the dispatched commit"; also "a shell-wrapped constant command is not recognised as non-probative" (`readiness.rs:127`).
- Failure: An evaluator in run N writes a helper script into the working tree (possible per F-03, which says evaluators can modify working files); in run N+1 readiness sees the file exists and admits an evaluator whose code is in neither the patch nor the dispatched commit — command-digest evidence then references unreviewable, persistent, locally-planted code. (The persistence chain across runs is inferred; each half is stated.)
- Confidence: high for the stated gaps; medium for the cross-run chain.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Self-reference protection matches three fixed directory names, not crate identity
- Topics: DE-04.3
- Evidence: The self-reference screen uses "known slug, protected paths or three fixed directories" (`automerge.rs:114`, `automerge.rs:144`); "A tracked crate named dream-gate under a different directory is not recognised by the fixed-path check."
- Failure: A PR that relocates the engine's own crate to a non-fixed path passes the screen; the engine opens and possibly auto-merges a change into the repository it is itself running from — the exact outcome the self-reference rule exists to prevent.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — The AGPL adapter is publishable: no `publish = false`, and packages omit the NOTICE
- Topics: DE-05.2, DE-01.1
- Evidence: "the adapter manifest has no publish=false restriction" and the adapter "has a package version" (`services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml:4`); ADR-DE-002 "prohibits publishing the adapter" but "a written prohibition alone does not enforce the second" boundary; "Cargo package lists also omit the repository NOTICE from the five crates" (DE-01.1, DE-05.2).
- Failure: A maintainer runs `cargo publish` from the wrong workspace and AGPL-licensed product code uploads to crates.io, breaching the licence fence that motivated the whole extraction; independently, every published permissive crate ships without the promised provenance NOTICE.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated in prose, no marker)

### F-09 — Night mutual exclusion is a caller obligation, not an enforced property
- Topics: DE-03.1
- Evidence: The recovery flow begins at "Caller holds night lock" (`runstate.rs:261`); DE-03's business framing conditions the guarantees on "the host following the lock contract." Nothing in the material shows the engine enforcing exclusion on the run path (only reset "takes lock," `runstate.rs:441`).
- Failure: Two scheduler instances (failover, overlapping timers, a second operator) process the same night directory; journal writes interleave and both can pass the Persisted/completion check before either records, yielding duplicate publication, duplicated model spend, or a corrupted journal. The recovery guarantees are single-instance only.
- Confidence: medium — inferred: the material shows the lock as a precondition, not an enforcement.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Deterministic evidence filenames silently overwrite earlier records
- Topics: DE-02.4
- Evidence: Receipts are written under a slugged name (`receipts.rs:288`) and "distinct names can share a filename — later receipt overwrites earlier one" (`receipts.rs:297`, e.g. `a b` vs `a-b`); the frozen-manifest archive is renamed by run ID and "same run ID can overwrite prior archive," while damaged manifests share "one fixed archive name" (`manifest.rs:305`, `manifest.rs:314`). DE-01's header confirms these were reproduced.
- Failure: A night with two evaluators whose names slug identically retains only the later receipt; a repeated model change within one run ID destroys the earlier frozen manifest; a second corrupt read destroys the first damaged-manifest archive. The evidence needed to reconstruct or audit a failed run is silently absent.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Persistence is neither synced nor crash-atomic
- Topics: DE-03.1
- Evidence: Journal saves use "temporary file and rename — no file or directory sync" (`manifest.rs:328`); reset is a multi-file hard-link sequence (`runstate.rs:441`, `runstate.rs:466`) in which "rollback errors are ignored" (`runstate.rs:477`), and DE-03.1 states it "do[es] not make it a crash-atomic transaction."
- Failure: Power loss after the OS acknowledges the Persisted write but before flush leaves the journal without the entry on boot; the night re-runs model calls and publication — duplicating the external effects the Persisted-first ordering exists to prevent. Separately, a crash mid-reset leaves the night in a partial state, and swallowed rollback errors hide stray archives and source names.
- Confidence: high for the fsync absence (the probe is process-kill only, as DE-03.1 says); the power-loss consequence is the authors' own Open item.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — Open (storage fault model) for the sync gap; the reset non-atomicity is unmarked.

### F-12 — A ready-then-merge-failure leaves a PR publicly ready while the docs say it stays a draft
- Topics: DE-04.2
- Evidence: `Published`'s error-field documentation says "the PR stays a draft" (`persist.rs:278`), but the flow marks ready (`seams.rs:471`) then merges (`seams.rs:480`), and on ready-success plus merge-failure there is "No draft-restoration or outcome query" (`persist.rs:326-330`); the offline fixture "reproduces that sequence and absence of an undo command."
- Failure: autoMerge enabled; the ready command succeeds and the merge command fails (permissions, network); the PR is now ready-for-review while the system records it as still a draft. Reviewers' gating assumptions are broken, others may merge it, and no code path reverses or even queries the state.
- Confidence: high — reproduced against an offline command fixture.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — Drift (draft after enable failure)

### F-13 — Duplicate test identifiers make per-test verdicts order-dependent, and the parser can manufacture duplicates
- Topics: DE-02.3
- Evidence: Per-test evidence is collected "by test name into maps — last duplicate replaces earlier result" (`pertest.rs:210`); parser deduplication can create a collision: "`x, x, x~2` becomes `x, x~2, x~2`" (`testfmt.rs:80-89`); DE-01's header confirms duplicate test-name collapse was reproduced.
- Failure: A test binary emits repeated or `~`-suffixed names (flaky retries, reruns); two distinct tests end up under one mangled identity and one's evidence replaces the other's; the retained per-test verdict reflects whichever landed last and cannot be reproduced.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Source accounting marks unsupplied content as honoured and breaches its own budget
- Topics: DE-03.2, DE-03.3
- Evidence: A range past EOF returns empty text and is then counted as inlined, and "Inlined means requested_honoured" (`source.rs:645`, `source.rs:1164`), with a reversed displayed line interval; "No returned record when nothing is inlined" (`source.rs:1183`) so all-refused reads vanish from the record; elision markers appended after clipping make "body usage exceed both the requested half-budget and the overall budget" (probe: 30,134 requested vs 60,134 body bytes, `source.rs:656`, `source.rs:851`, `source.rs:865`); line-based ls-tree parsing retains git-quoted non-ASCII paths as quoted text, so a tracked file is missed (`source.rs:117`, `source.rs:138`); invalid or duplicate requests consume reserved slots before validation (`source.rs:1091`).
- Failure: The adapter settles requests from these markers (AB-23.21 per DE-03.3), so the model is told a file was supplied when zero bytes were; nights proceed without needed context and fail late as "avoidable operator alerts"; budget overrun roughly doubles prompt size against model limits; non-ASCII paths never reach the model at all.
- Confidence: high — reproduced in the dated audit.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — Drift (ADR-DE-008 delivery) for the honoured/budget items; the quoted-path and slot-consumption gaps are unmarked.

### F-15 — Nobody can state which engine version production runs, and the estate's own stamps disagree
- Topics: DE-01.2, DE-01 header
- Evidence: ADR-2141's pinning bullet names `=0.2.0` (ADR line 31), its last re-verification adopts `=0.3.0` (line 55), while the adapter pins `dream-core =0.4.0` / `dream-gate =0.3.0` (`services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml:26-27`); image activation is recorded only for the 2026-10-16 registry-pinned build (ADR line 47) and "their image activation is not recorded" for 0.2.0–0.4.0; DE-01's header says the pass "does not re-establish deployment or registry freshness" and "Current running version not requalified here." Additionally the file's own verification stamp (front matter `59a3e21…`) differs from the commit its prose says was audited (`815f963`), with a third stamp (`61581d3`) appearing mid-file.
- Failure: An incident arrives and no artefact answers whether production runs 0.2.0, 0.3.0 or 0.4.0; the ADR's deployment evidence points at pins that no longer exist in the manifest, so rollback targets and change attribution are guesses; the mismatched stamps mean even the audit trail cannot say which source it certified.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — Drift (ADR-2141 vs the manifest) for the pin mismatch; the unrecorded 0.2.0–0.4.0 activations and the stamp contradiction are unmarked.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **The agentbox orchestration itself.** The audits repeatedly scope it out ("distinguishes these APIs from the consumer's orchestration"): what the adapter gates before `publish_draft` (`engine.rs:1774`), whether it enforces the night lock, and how it consumes `requested_honoured` markers. F-02, F-04, F-09 and F-14 all change severity depending on this code.
2. **Forge credentials and branch protections on target repositories.** Whether the `gh` commands run with write access to protected branches, and whether required reviews would intercept an unverified auto-merge — the only real mitigation for F-02 and F-12.
3. **Actual deployed versions and images.** Registry state, the image build pipeline, and whether 0.2.0–0.4.0 images were ever built or activated — DE-01 explicitly declines to requalify this, and F-15 cannot be closed without it.
4. **The privilege boundary around the formatter.** What user and filesystem permissions the rustfmt process runs under on the control plane; this determines whether F-01 is an arbitrary-write primitive or a nuisance.
5. **Any evaluator sandboxing at all.** The material shows only the darwin sandbox-flag report (`readiness.rs:228-232`, where `assess` "changes nothing") and shell timeouts; whether evaluators run under any isolation — which bounds F-03, F-06 and the `timed_out=false` classification gap in DE-04.1 — is not stated.

## dream-engine / premortem

# Post-mortem — dream-engine incident review (November 2027)

**Scope.** One client was lost to a production failure of the data/custody/security class, not a cosmetic one. This document reconstructs the most probable root causes from the defects documented and reproduced in the estate corpus dated 1 November 2026. Every claim below is grounded in a cited path:line from that corpus; where a scenario extends beyond what was reproduced, it is marked inferred. Nothing here assumes ordering or concurrency beyond what the cited diagrams state. All findings are independently checkable against the code and tests at the cited lines.

---

### R-1 — The published head was never bound to the tree that was actually evaluated
- Topics: DE-02.2, DE-02.3, DE-04.2, DE-04.3, DE-01.2
- Chain of events: 1) A night prepares a candidate and records the worktree tree hash *before* evaluation (`crates/dream-core/src/candidate.rs:546-558`). 2) Evaluator commands then run; there is no post-evaluation tree check and `candidate::run` returns the prior hash, so evaluator-side modification of working files after the hash is invisible to the record (DE-02.2 prose). 3) The gate is a pure function over caller-supplied receipts, candidate state and typed outcome; it "does not reclassify raw streams or prove the candidate tree exists," and `GateContext::new` omits config-digest checking (`crates/dream-gate/src/gate.rs:461`, DE-02.3). 4) On success the adapter publishes via `Forge::publish_draft` (`../project/agentbox/services/dream-engine/src/engine.rs:1774`); the `DraftPr` request "names checkout, slug and branch — no expected commit field" (`crates/dream-core/src/seams.rs:341`), and "Clean means pushed and Some URL" (`crates/dream-core/src/persist.rs:299`) — even empty successful stdout qualifies as `Some URL` (`seams.rs:512`). 5) If merging is enabled, the merge command "has no expected-head guard" (`seams.rs:481`). 6) The client repository therefore receives a head that no mechanism ever compared against the evaluated tree; conversely, an empty-stdout forge run can be recorded as published when no PR exists.
- Evidence: DE-04's business statement that the merge result "does not prove that a merge happened or that the remotely published head matches the evaluated change"; the DE-02.3 statement that the gate "trusts the caller's candidate tree and typed outcome"; the reproduced absence of any commit-identity field at `seams.rs:341` and `seams.rs:481`.
- Warning signs visible today: the seam audit already records all three gaps (no expected commit, no expected-head guard, empty stdout accepted); DE-01.2 additionally records that image activation for the currently pinned releases was never recorded, so no one could have established which gate behaviour was live when the incident occurred.
- Confidence: high that the guards are absent (stated and reproduced); the specific trigger (evaluator mutation vs branch race vs wrong checkout) is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Formatter and evaluator processes write outside the worktree, undetectably
- Topics: DE-02.2, DE-02.3, DE-02.1
- Chain of events: 1) A candidate file contains a Rust attribute in valid spaced form (`#[ path = …`) pointing at an absolute path. 2) The fixer-selection scan is a literal substring check for `#[path` (`crates/dream-core/src/candidate.rs:115`) and misses the spaced syntax. 3) rustfmt runs as a local process on the control plane (`candidate.rs:129-131`); the reproduced probe shows it followed an absolute path and *modified a file outside the worktree* (the temporary canary). 4) Post-format inspection is git status inside the worktree (`candidate.rs:147`), which "cannot detect that write"; the layout change is then committed and re-guarded as if confined (`candidate.rs:176`, `candidate.rs:206`). 5) The same trust extends to evaluators: "neither mechanism supplies an execution sandbox" (DE-02.3), and on darwin the readiness safety check "only appends an `Unusable::DarwinSandboxMissing` problem … and changes nothing" (`crates/dream-core/src/readiness.rs:228-232`). 6) A nightly run therefore mutates arbitrary host files — other clients' checkouts, credentials, daemon config — with the daemon's privileges, leaving no trace in the run's own evidence.
- Evidence: the canary reproduction in DE-02.2; the authors' own marker, "Tension (ADR-DE-009 confinement): the documented out-of-worktree-write closure is contradicted by the controlled probe"; DE-02.1's statement that static command heuristics are "an admission aid, not proof that an evaluator observes the candidate's behaviour."
- Warning signs visible today: the reproduced out-of-worktree write; the `#[path`-vs-`#[ path` gap; the inert darwin check; the audit's explicit open item on formatter confinement.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism (reproduced); the scale of host damage in this incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Colliding evidence identities overwrite the retained record and can flip the verdict
- Topics: DE-02.3, DE-02.4
- Chain of events: 1) Two evaluator receipts whose names slug identically (`a b` and `a-b`) are written under one filename; "later receipt overwrites earlier one" (`crates/dream-gate/src/receipts.rs:288`, `receipts.rs:297`). 2) Independently, per-test evidence is "collect[ed] by test name into maps — last duplicate replaces earlier result" (`crates/dream-gate/src/pertest.rs:210`), and the test-name parser can manufacture such duplicates: `x, x, x~2` becomes `x, x~2, x~2` (`crates/dream-core/src/testfmt.rs:80-89`). 3) The gate's decision consumes that map and returns "ACCEPT only if claimed and no vetoes" (`crates/dream-gate/src/gate.rs:489`), so a failing entry replaced by a later colliding passing entry yields ACCEPT — the verdict is order-dependent. 4) After the incident, the retained evidence is already destroyed: "same run ID can overwrite prior archive" and all damaged manifests share one fixed archive name (`crates/dream-gate/src/manifest.rs:305`, `manifest.rs:314`). 5) Replay "checks consistency of retained inputs — not execution authenticity or tamper resistance" (`crates/dream-core/src/replay.rs:10`), so the overwritten record replays clean. 6) The operator can neither show the client what actually ran nor prove the acceptance was justified; the custody failure is total even if the underlying change was benign.
- Evidence: DE-02.4's "Atomic replacement of individual files does not preserve every prior record"; the reproduced `a b`/`a-b` collision; the parser dedup collision; the stated order-dependence of the per-test verdict.
- Warning signs visible today: all of the above were reproduced against production source in the 1 November 2026 audit and recorded as the open "candidate and evidence boundaries" item in DE-01.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Untracked evaluator scripts pass admission
- Topics: DE-02.1
- Evidence: readiness inspects command segments and script existence (`crates/dream-core/src/readiness.rs:127`, `readiness.rs:241`), but "its filesystem existence check does not verify tracking at the dispatched commit: an untracked script can pass admission."
- Failure: a night evaluates a candidate against a script that is not in the dispatched tree; the same commit re-run later behaves differently, and the client's audit of the accepted change cannot reproduce the evaluation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Shell-wrapped constant commands are not recognised as non-probative
- Topics: DE-02.1
- Evidence: readiness rejects "some constant commands" (`readiness.rs:127`) but "a shell-wrapped constant command is not recognised as non-probative."
- Failure: an evaluator that always succeeds (e.g. a wrapped constant command — example inferred) passes admission, and the gate accepts changes on evidence that structurally cannot fail.
- Confidence: high (gap stated); the concrete command form is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — An empty required set does not veto acceptance
- Topics: DE-02.3
- Evidence: "An empty required set does not itself veto acceptance"; ACCEPT requires only "claimed and no vetoes" (`crates/dream-gate/src/gate.rs:489`).
- Failure: a misconfigured night with no applicable required evaluator accepts and publishes changes with no enforced check at all.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Config-digest checking is omitted
- Topics: DE-02.3
- Evidence: "`GateContext::new` omits config-digest checking"; the digest is only optional at decision time (`crates/dream-gate/src/gate.rs:461`).
- Failure: a verdict is produced under an evaluator configuration that is never bound into the decision record; later config drift is indistinguishable from the original decision configuration.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — `timed_out` is always false in LocalRunner
- Topics: DE-04.1
- Evidence: LocalRunner "uses a shell timeout but always sets timed_out to false" (`crates/dream-core/src/runner.rs:69`); timeout classification relies on the receipt classifier recognising exit 124 and 137 separately (`crates/dream-gate/src/receipts.rs:239`).
- Failure: any consumer reading `ExecOutcome.timed_out` records timed-out runs as normal outcomes; any timeout path that does not surface as exit 124/137 (wrapper swallowing codes — inferred) is classified as success.
- Confidence: high for the flag behaviour; misrouting scenario inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Ready-then-failed-merge leaves a ready-for-review PR with no undo
- Topics: DE-04.2, DE-04.3, DE-01.2
- Evidence: the offline fixture reproduces "a successful ready command followed by a failed merge command leaves a partial transition" with "absence of an undo command" (`crates/dream-core/src/persist.rs:326-330`, `seams.rs:471-480`); the authors' Drift marker notes `persist.rs:278` documents that the PR stays a draft, but it does not.
- Failure: on any host using the persist helper with merging enabled, the client repository shows a ready-for-review PR that was never reviewed, with no restoration path. (In the primary agentbox deployment this path is dormant: the adapter publishes via `Forge::publish_draft`, which "never merges, whatever `autoMerge` says" — `../project/agentbox/services/dream-engine/src/engine.rs:1774`, `../dream-engine/README.md:130-135` — itself a Drift-marked expectation gap.)
- Confidence: high (reproduced offline)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, DE-04 and DE-01.2)

### F-07 — Nothing prevents publishing the AGPL adapter, and NOTICE is missing from packages
- Topics: DE-05.2, DE-01.1
- Evidence: "the adapter manifest has no publish=false restriction"; offline package lists include the envelope module and "omit root NOTICE from all five permissive packages" (DE-05.2, citing `../project/agentbox/services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml`, `NOTICE:9`); ADR-DE-002 prohibits adapter publication, and the corpus states "a written prohibition alone does not enforce" it, and that the DE-01.1 invariant "does not … enforce the adapter's prohibition on publication."
- Failure: a routine packaging command from the adapter workspace (inferred) ships AGPL product code to a public registry, breaching the licence fence; the permissive crates circulate without the NOTICE recording design lineage, failing downstream provenance obligations.
- Confidence: high for the missing guards; the publication event is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — The self-reference check is blind to vendored engine code
- Topics: DE-04.3
- Evidence: self-reference detection covers "known slug, protected paths or three fixed directories" (`crates/dream-core/src/automerge.rs:114`, `automerge.rs:144`); "a tracked crate named dream-gate under a different directory is not recognised by the fixed-path check."
- Failure: a client repository vendors dream-gate at a nonstandard path; the engine merges a change into the very crate that judges its candidates (escalation inferred), altering future verdicts with no external check.
- Confidence: high for the blind spot (stated); the exploitation scenario is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — A rejected experiment is still published as a draft
- Topics: DE-04.2
- Evidence: "A rejected verdict or self-referential target prevents merge enablement but does not prevent this helper from first publishing a draft" (`crates/dream-core/src/persist.rs:298`); "the adapter separately gates publication before calling the lower-level forge."
- Failure: any host using the persist helper directly (other than the agentbox adapter — inferred) pushes drafts of *rejected* changes into client repositories, where they sit alongside F-06's unrestorable states.
- Confidence: high for the helper behaviour; medium, inferred, for non-adapter consumers
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — The persistence boundary is not durable storage
- Topics: DE-03.1
- Evidence: the verdict is recorded at Persisted before forge and ledger (`crates/dream-core/src/runstate.rs:615`), written via "temporary file and rename — no file or directory sync" (`crates/dream-gate/src/manifest.rs:328`); the authors' Open marker: "temporary-file replacement is not evidence of durable storage after power loss; at-most-once publication requires the persisted journal to survive." The audit also showed restart finalises a matching Persisted record and returns AlreadyComplete "despite a different run ID" (`runstate.rs:415`) — completeness is keyed to the night directory, not the run.
- Failure: power loss after rename loses the Persisted record; the night re-runs and re-publishes to the client (duplicate drafts), or completion state is lost while the directory-level identity still suppresses legitimately new runs.
- Confidence: high (author-flagged dependency)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-11 — Night reset is not crash-atomic and ignores rollback errors
- Topics: DE-03.1
- Evidence: reset takes the lock and links archives (`runstate.rs:441`, `runstate.rs:466`), removes source names separately, and "rollback errors are ignored" (`runstate.rs:477`); "its multi-file sequence and ignored rollback failures do not make it a crash-atomic transaction."
- Failure: a crash mid-reset leaves a half-cleaned night (archives linked but sources present, or the reverse); the next run's identity and attempt-budget checks (`runstate.rs:523`, `runstate.rs:542`) act on partial state.
- Confidence: high for non-atomicity; the corrupted-state scenario is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — "Honoured" source requests can carry zero bytes, reverse their interval, and blow the budget
- Topics: DE-03.3
- Evidence: a past-EOF range "is honoured with zero bytes and a reversed displayed line interval" (`crates/dream-core/src/source.rs:645`); "Inlined means requested_honoured" (`source.rs:1164`); clipping markers made body usage exceed both budgets — the probe records 30,134 requested bytes against 60,134 total body bytes (`source.rs:656`); "no returned record when nothing is inlined" (`source.rs:1183`); AB-23.21 shows the adapter settles requests from these markers; the authors' Drift marker states the delivery accounting is weaker than ADR-DE-008's decision.
- Failure: the model never receives a file the operator explicitly requested (a policy or contract file — inferred), the accounting reports it honoured, budgets silently double, and all-refused reads leave no refusal record; nights produce changes made blind while dashboards report completion.
- Confidence: high (numbers reproduced); client-impact framing inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-13 — Path accounting drops tracked files with non-ASCII names
- Topics: DE-03.2
- Evidence: tracked paths come from "line-based ls-tree output" (`crates/dream-core/src/source.rs:117`, `source.rs:138`); "Git's quoted non-ASCII paths are retained as quoted text by the line parser, so a tracked file can be missed" — confirmed by reading the same file directly with `git show`; additionally, the reservation "count[s] entries before validation," so "invalid or duplicate requests consume positions" (`source.rs:1091`).
- Failure: a candidate touching files with non-ASCII names is generated and gated without the model ever seeing those files; the change ships blind and the honoured/refused accounting cannot reveal it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Rust/TypeScript trimming divergence changes verdicts on real data
- Topics: DE-05.1
- Evidence: the parity suite derives Rust expectations "from reading TypeScript — TypeScript not executed by this suite" (`crates/dream-core/tests/ts_parity.rs:3`); the audit separately executed the old validator and "expos[ed] U+FEFF and U+0085 differences" (`crates/dream-core/src/ledger/read.rs:215`, `crates/dream-gate/src/verdict.rs:394`, `verdict.rs:444`); the migration guide points host retirement at those parity checks (`migrating-from-the-ts-fork.md:43`), and the suite is excluded from the published dream-core package (`crates/dream-core/Cargo.toml:11`).
- Failure: a host retires the TS driver on the strength of the suite; the same ledger and witness data then yields different verdicts under Rust (a record containing U+FEFF or U+0085 parsing differently — inferred), so decisions diverge from the historical system the client was billed against.
- Confidence: high for the divergence (reproduced); the production verdict flip is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — The deployed daemon's version is unrecorded and the decision record contradicts the manifest
- Topics: DE-01.2
- Evidence: ADR-2141's pinning bullet names `=0.2.0` (`ADR-2141-...md:31`) and its last re-verification adopts `=0.3.0` (`:55`), while the adapter pins core `=0.4.0` and gate `=0.3.0` (`../project/agentbox/services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml:26-27`); "their image activation is not recorded in the ADR"; the qualification pass "does not re-establish deployment or registry freshness," and the estate diagram carries "Current running version not requalified here."
- Failure: during incident response nobody can establish which engine behaviour was live; fixes believed shipped across 0.2.0–0.4.0 (requested-source honouring, pre-gate formatting, hypothesis screening) may never have been activated, so response decisions are made against a version fiction.
- Confidence: high for the record gap; its operational consequence is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

## estate / critical

### F-01 — Shipped compose defaults to an anonymous-read, owner-less, dev-mode posture, and a standalone Cloudflare tunnel can put it on the public internet invisibly to the port audit
- Topics: ES-10.3, ES-10.6, ES-09.8, ES-01.3
- Evidence: Code defaults are fail-closed (`rbac_gate.rs:135-141` `unwrap_or(false)`; `src/main.rs:809-819` refuses owner-less boot), but the shipped compose inverts them: `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS: ${RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1}` (docker-compose.unified.yml:121), `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1` (:122), and `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1` (:105) — the last a total, peer-agnostic auth bypass (ES-10.6: "BYPASS IS TOTAL — no NIP-98, no token, no peer check"). The standalone `docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:3-8` "fronts WHATEVER profile the backend booted", so with this posture "anonymous /api reads reach the public internet via the Cloudflare tunnel" (ES-10.3 CFT). The tunnel's Public Hostname mapping is configured in the Cloudflare dashboard, "NOT reviewable from this repo" (docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:5-6), is outbound-initiated and "invisible to the ADR-2013 compose port-audit" (:6-8), and `VISIONCLAW_INGRESS=lan` does not stop a hand-started standalone tunnel (ADR-2119…md:64-66).
- Failure: an operator brings the stack up with defaults and a previously-started tunnel; the public internet gets anonymous API reads (and, if the tunnel fronts the dev service, the dev-mode full bypass) with nothing in the repo showing the mapping.
- Confidence: high for the demo-open-plus-tunnel compound (stated); inferred for the tunnel fronting the dev-mode service specifically.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT1, DRIFT2, CFT, LANGAP markers) — but each marker understates the compound exposure.

### F-02 — The email-privacy invariant says corpus content "never leaves the LAN", while compose arms a cloud advisory path by default
- Topics: ES-06.1, ES-06.5, ES-06.6, ES-07.3, ES-05.7, ES-08.13, ES-09.8, ES-01.3
- Evidence: ES-06.1 INV2: "the Loom IS the email privacy system. It delegates ONLY to a LAN/local model, never to a cloud endpoint, so mail content never leaves the LAN." Yet the unified compose arms `LOOM_JEV=1` with `LOOM_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD` default 1, reaching the agentbox proxy with backend TypeSafe (docker-compose.unified.yml:387-397, :395; loom `jev.rs:353`), while the façade's own header "still justifies local-only by private corpus content" (`jev.rs:30-34`). The same pattern spreads: memory rerank sends "the query and capped value snippets of the shortlist" to cloud TypeSafe by default (`memory-jev-rerank.js:16-18,107`; `agentbox.toml:895` local_only empty, `:910`); the governance rubric's card text "reaches the selected backend, TypeSafe by default" (`governance-evidence-rubric.js:118`; `agentbox.toml:902-903`); the pod reranks "the forge's already-authorised repo and issue listings" with `ALLOW_CLOUD=1` (solid-pod-rs-server `lib.rs:5130`, `jev.rs:153-181`; `agentbox.toml:921`). The corpus also contradicts itself on whether this is live: ES-09.8 says the System One network is "LIVE: both activation conditions … happened on 2026-10-30", while ES-01.3 says "the running container still dials loopback" and ES-06.6 says "CONFIGURED, taking effect when the new agentbox image is activated".
- Failure: once the rebuilt image is active, ontology scaffold candidates derived from private email corpora, agent-memory snippets and governance card text egress to a third-party cloud API, in direct contradiction of the stated privacy invariant; and no one can tell from the docs whether it is already happening.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — each site is marked (Open/Drift/Tension); the contradiction with the INV2 invariant and the ES-09.8-vs-ES-01.3 liveness conflict are not marked.

### F-03 — Production forum edge runs a known-vulnerable crypto stack while the fixed main is undeployed
- Topics: ES-05.14, ES-11.4
- Evidence: "forum main (`1b27f79`) is fixed for RUSTSEC-2026-0387, with k256 0.14.0, p256 0.14.0 … but forum production still runs kit `9c36b60`, with crypto-bigint 0.5.5 and k256 0.13.4, so main is fixed and the edge is not" (ES-05.14, citing `dreamlab-ai-website/.github/workflows/workers-deploy.yml:44`).
- Failure: the relay worker that verifies event signatures and enforces the governance gates (rationale check, receipts, `check_rationale` before `save_event`) runs the vulnerable curve library; exploitation depends on the RUSTSEC's specifics, which this material does not state.
- Confidence: high that the divergence exists; the security impact on the relay's verification path is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — stated as plain fact, no Tension/Drift marker.

### F-04 — The pod ledger ships hand-rolled Bitcoin crypto and a reachable "free-money oracle" deposit on a publicly-tunneled server
- Topics: ES-08.12, ES-03.12, ES-08.1, ES-10.11
- Evidence: the released crate "HAND-ROLLS … BIP-341 scripts, the TapSighash and key-path Schnorr on raw k256, with big-endian mod-n scalar arithmetic written out by hand" (ADR-2008…md:22-26); the TXO stand-in deposit "credits sats for any parseable TXO URI guarded only by a replay key … a chain-settled estate must not keep a reachable free-money oracle" (:67-71); the crate's own README says "not to carry value through the payment routes until it is fixed" (:72-75); the port is landed unreleased (:119-158), so production runs the hand-rolled code. That server is tunneled to the public internet: `cloudflared-pod` maps pods-native.dreamlab-ai.com to agentbox port 8484 (docker-compose.solid-pods.yml:26-33), and the ledger is a live store of record today (ADR-2008:29). Separately, the sidechain producer is gated on the enabled key itself rather than the separate signer key ADR-2098 requires (ADR-2098…md:48-50 vs flake.nix:321, agentbox.toml:1921), so one key "dominates mirrors, faucets and poker citizens".
- Failure: any internet caller who can reach the pod's payment route credits arbitrary sats for fabricated TXO URIs; a scalar-arithmetic bug in the hand-rolled Schnorr mis-signs or mis-verifies value movements.
- Confidence: medium (inferred) — the material shows the tunnel maps to the pod server and the deposit exists in the released crate, but not what authentication guards that specific route, nor whether real value flows today.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (PROPOSED D6 and the PRE invariant frame it), though the public-tunneling compound is not drawn.

### F-05 — A consumer restart silently swallows governance decisions: the checkpoint marks unfinished work as seen
- Topics: ES-13.2, ES-13.1
- Evidence: events are added to the shared seen map before dispatch (`relay-consumer.js:464`) and queued (:496); the completion callback "serialise[s] whole seen map, including B" (`relay-consumer.js:470,395`); on restore, a redelivered B is "reject[ed] as duplicate, no handler runs" (:459, restore at :369,376). "Governance records remain on disk, but omit the original signature; recovery by another reconciler was not demonstrated." A deterministic fixture "reproduced suppression of B before its original handler completed". Deployed relay, D1 and browser behaviour are outside the fixtures (ES-13 preamble).
- Failure: the consumer crashes or restarts mid-decision; the human's signed 31403 is persisted as delivered but never applied, with no signature on disk to reconcile from — a governance decision is lost silently.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION).

### F-06 — Corpus mutation does not wait for, and cannot be vetoed by, the forum's own case state
- Topics: ES-13.1, ES-05.14
- Evidence: "Explicit promote checks request tag and IRI, then applies without reading projection receipt or expiry" (`local-process-manager.js:419,444,461`); "corpus mutation does not wait for the forum's successful state transition" (`relay-consumer.js:497`); "The consumer's independent path does not turn a closed forum row into a mutation veto" (ES-13.1). The forum's expiry receipts close open/under_review/reopened cases (`cron.rs:764,794,834`) and projection commits only for matching request and allowed case state (`receipts.rs:185,188`) — checks the consumer never consults.
- Failure: a proposal whose review deadline expired, or whose case the forum closed or refused to project, is still promoted into the agentbox corpus by a signed event the consumer honours; demotion at the forum cannot undo it.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION / GAP node).

### F-07 — Erasure is a lie across stores: pod deletes leave RuVector embeddings semantically searchable, and no backup exists to restore from either
- Topics: ES-08.9, ES-07.9, ES-07.1
- Evidence: `agentMemory.ts:196-204` "has no reverse-tombstone dispatch to mcp claude-flow memory_delete or equivalent"; "this deletion path leaves that row untouched. No joined key-to-row lookup or live post-delete search was performed in this audit"; the governing doc calls this "an erasure hole" (docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md Known divergences); Invariant 6 requires a dropped secondary write to be logged — "this call path does not even attempt the secondary write, let alone log its absence" (ES-08.9). ES-07.9 D5: "Largest erasure hole. No point-in-time RuVector backup exists, so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO."
- Failure: a user exercises their right to erase agent memory; the pod resource is deleted, the embedding row stays live in HNSW search forever, and there is no snapshot from which to reconstruct what should have been removed.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE D5, plus DATA-authority-erasure Known divergences).

### F-08 — The pod store — the durable authority for sovereign identity and preferences — has no backup, no RPO, no RTO, and an untested restore path
- Topics: ES-08.8, ES-08.1, ES-10.10
- Evidence: "no point-in-time backup for the pod store, scripts backup-sqlite.sh covers SQLite only … no cross-store consistent restore, no declared RPO or RTO" (ES-08.8 note, citing docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md). The pod runs as the supervised port-8484 service (flake.nix:2680) and is fronted by a public tunnel (docker-compose.solid-pods.yml:26-33), so it is both the only copy and Internet-reachable. The two backup paths that do exist disagree in posture: VisionClaw's `backup-secrets.sh` writes an ordinary ZIP; agentbox's Rust backup hardens to 0600 — "Its existence does not replace the legacy script or prove deployed adoption" (ES-10.10 D2).
- Failure: disk loss or ransomware on the pod volume destroys sovereign identities, ACLs and preferences permanently; the only "backup" covers a different store.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in ES-08.8; D2 in ES-10.10).

### F-09 — The shipped default puts every signing key in one UID's environment, hands it a world-writable host Docker socket, and the register confirming any custodian is empty
- Topics: ES-10.12, ES-10.10, ES-04.5, ES-05.15, ES-02.1
- Evidence: with `role_isolation = false`, "the shipped default", the entrypoint "widens the host Docker socket o+rw for devuser" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:598-602`) and "PID 1's environment, .env included, reaches all of [the programs]" (ES-10.12). The widening is persistent: "the socket is the HOST inode, already widened by earlier boots … only a host-side chmod does" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:605-621`; docker-compose.override.yml:115-117). "SOPS never executed … VisionClaw .env is PLAINTEXT today" (ES-10.10 D5). "Every custodian, deployed location, rotation cadence and incident-response window in the agentbox custody register is UNCONFIRMED" (ES-04.5 D5, ES-10.10 ST). The house key 11ed6422 "stays admitted for every use until its roles are listed as withdrawn, and that list is empty" (g5-key-split.json:32). Within the box, "a process inside the agentbox container can still stamp any pubkey over loopback" (ES-02.1 Debt, agent-event-auth.js:36-39).
- Failure: any code execution as devuser — including a compromised sidecar via the socket — reads every secret in `.env` and obtains host-root equivalence; there is no rotation cadence or confirmed custodian to fall back on.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (multiple markers: OPEN2/R2, D5, ST, Debt), but presented as separate facts rather than the compound default-posture exposure it is.

### F-10 — Nothing verifies a built release artefact omits dev-auth; the hygiene check is a no-op stub in exactly the builds that need it
- Topics: ES-09.5, ES-09.11, ES-10.2
- Evidence: "ADR-2037 (proposed, implementation_status none): no CI or image-build assertion yet verifies a shipped release binary omits dev-auth — a mis-targeted pipeline could promote the stubbed-hygiene binary to production undetected" (ES-09.5); `enforce_release_env_hygiene` is "a no-op stub (src/main.rs:169)" in dev/dev-auth builds. The blocking CI gate is a "hermetic text assertion over the committed Dockerfiles/entrypoints" (ci.yml:146-155, 163-168) — it checks recipes, not artefacts.
- Failure: one mis-ordered build flag or hand-built image ships a production binary in which `SETTINGS_AUTH_BYPASS` works and the boot hygiene check cannot detect it; no gate anywhere notices.
- Confidence: high that the gate is absent; the triggering pipeline mistake is hypothetical.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in ES-09.5).

### F-11 — The System One proxy listens on 0.0.0.0 inside the container on a bridge shared by a dozen sidecars, so any of them can spend the vendor key
- Topics: ES-10.8, ES-01.4, ES-01.5
- Evidence: "the System One proxy binds 0.0.0.0 inside the container when network is true (agentbox.toml:893-894), never published, so any container on visionclaw_network can reach it and spend the vendor key, an accepted consequence (ADR-2139…md:287)"; it is on the listener list (check-ports-loopback.mjs:740-745) with only Origin/content-type 403s (server.rs:151-169). The bridge joins browsercontainer, gui-tools, voice, xr-runtime, android, openmed, isaac-sim and ming sidecars (ES-01.5), and the port gate is "structurally blind to a container-internal 0.0.0.0 bind on a shared bridge" (ADR-2062, proposed).
- Failure: one compromised or buggy sidecar drains the paid vendor API key or exfiltrates through the proxy; nothing in CI or the port audit would have flagged the reachability.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (D6 "accepted consequence"; ADR-2062 proposed) — the acceptance makes it no less a production exposure.

### F-12 — Agent pod writes are unsigned by default and a degraded boot silently mints a placeholder identity; two topics disagree on whether this is closed
- Topics: ES-04.4, ES-08.2, ES-08.1
- Evidence: ES-04.4 (DIVERGENCE): "pod signing can fall back UNSIGNED … Combined with the did:nostr:local placeholder fallback that agent-identity.js:41-43 says the entrypoint still runs unconditionally, a degraded boot can silently produce a NON-SOVEREIGN identity." ES-08.2 says ADR-2064 resolved this — but only "with sign_requests on": the flag's "default keeps prior unsigned behaviour byte-identical (pod-signer.js:34-35,73)" and "It remains reachable only with the flag off, where unsigned is the declared baseline" — i.e. the shipped default is unsigned writes against a default-deny pod whose ACL must then be open enough to accept them.
- Failure: an agent pod write on a degraded boot is attributed to `did:nostr:local` or to nobody; WAC grants and provenance keyed to real DIDs never match, and the sovereignty guarantee the pod exists to provide is silently absent.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; medium on how often degraded boots occur.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in ES-04.4), but ES-08.2's "RESOLVED" framing contradicts ES-04.4's live-divergence framing without either reconciling which default ships.

### F-13 — The rationale gate can never fire for VisionClaw decisions, because nothing in VisionClaw declares a tier
- Topics: ES-05.12, ES-05.13, ES-05.14
- Evidence: VisionClaw's 31400/31402 carry "no tier or task-property field" (`events.rs:211-217, 91-101, 308-323`); the relay therefore "folds every VisionClaw case to the advertised default, medium, and check_rationale only bites at high or critical: NEITHER rationale gate fires on a VisionClaw case" (`governance.rs:769, 525`; `wrangler.toml:61`). The host gate likewise only bites when a tier is known (`enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:382-390`). "no VisionClaw writer records a declared tier" (ES-05.12 OPEN).
- Failure: the estate's canon audit lens — no fabricated rationale, humans must supply reasoning — is structurally unenforceable for an entire class of producers; an irreversible enrichment decision ships with no rationale and no gate objects, and ADR-2010/2110 activation stalls for want of a case that can never become high-tier.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN, "DIVERGENCE (narrowed)").

### F-14 — Privacy-filter ordering fails open on three of five adapter slots, and adapter contract versions are frozen placeholders
- Topics: ES-03.9
- Evidence: when a route bypasses `wrapWithPrivacyFilter`, `assertPrivacyFilterApplied` throws `MiddlewareOrderViolation` (`privacy-filter.js:562-571`) — but "alt slot is pods or memory (fail-closed slots) … else other slot: log and continue, fail-open". So beads, events and orchestrator payloads proceed to the adapter unredacted and unmarked. In the same dispatch: "adapter contract versions are STALE PLACEHOLDERS. pods, memory, events and orchestrator all still declare 1.0.0 despite live churn … A consumer cannot tell from the version whether the contract it compiled against still holds."
- Failure: a route refactor drops the privacy wrapper on the events slot; writes continue flowing with PII intact, the only signal a log line and a counter, and downstream consumers have no version signal to detect the contract change either.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the version staleness is marked (DIVERGENCE); the fail-open on three of five slots is stated with no marker.

### F-15 — Cross-repo content addressing is only byte-identical by convention, and the one test that would catch divergence is run by nothing
- Topics: ES-03.6, ES-09.22, ES-02.12
- Evidence: "JS sha12 hashes the raw string via crypto.createHash on the URN or payload string, Rust src/uri/mod.rs content_address hashes caller-supplied bytes directly — equivalence depends on callers feeding byte-identical canonical input on both sides, not enforced by the type system" (ES-03.6 DIVERGENCE; `uris.js:460` vs `provenance_writer.rs:264`). The paired federation fixture that asserts per-kind symmetry is "governed by a record but never run by anything" (`invariants.yml:172-175`, ES-09.22 DEBT), and the colloquy kind allocation "is NOT yet fixture-backed" (agentbox PROTOCOL-registry.md:146-147).
- Failure: one side changes canonicalisation (key ordering, whitespace, encoding); every crossed identifier silently hashes to a different sha256-12, breaking provenance joins and URI resolution between agentbox and VisionClaw — and no CI step detects the asymmetry.
- Confidence: high on the facts; medium (inferred) on the likelihood of the divergent-input bug occurring.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE at ES-03.6; DEBT at ES-09.22; OPEN at ES-02.12) — but the compounding of an unenforced invariant with an ungated test is not drawn together anywhere.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Whether the standalone Cloudflare tunnel is actually running and what it fronts.** The Public Hostname mapping lives in the Cloudflare dashboard (docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:5-6); the repo cannot show whether it points at the dev service (dev-mode total bypass) or only the production service, nor whether the tunnel container is up.
2. **What authentication guards the pod's payment/deposit routes behind pods-native.dreamlab-ai.com** (F-04): the material shows the tunnel maps to the port-8484 server but not whether the TXO deposit or WebLedger endpoints sit behind WAC/NIP-98 or are anonymous-writable.
3. **Which agentbox image is actually running** — the corpus asserts both "LIVE … both activation conditions happened on 2026-10-30" (ES-09.8) and "the running container still dials loopback" (ES-01.3); a runtime read of the deployed container is needed to size F-02.
4. **Real custody facts**: who physically holds `nostr.key`, the vendor keys and the house key 11ed6422; whether `role_isolation` has ever been enabled on a production boot; whether the host socket's `o+rw` has ever been chmod'd back. The register is explicitly provisional and unconfirmed.
5. **Live data state**: whether any RuVector rows today correspond to pod memories already deleted (the material states no joined lookup was performed), and whether any value has ever flowed through the pod ledger — both determine whether F-07 and F-04 are latent or active.

## estate / premortem

# Post-mortem — confidential client data left the estate's trust boundary

**Scope of this document.** The incident class is a data, custody or security failure attributable to this estate: client-confidential material (email-derived content, agent memory, pod resources, graph data) either reached a party it should never have reached, survived an erasure request, or was acted on under a forged identity. The three root causes below are the mechanisms the corpus most strongly supports, ranked by likelihood; all "today" facts are as of the stamped revisions (visionclaw `8692b12ba`, agentbox `6500582df`, late October 2026). Everything an engineer needs to verify a claim is cited as the material gives it; where I reason beyond the material I say so.

---

### R-1 — The System One / JEV path sends private corpus and mail-derived content to a cloud vendor, directly contradicting the estate's LAN-only privacy invariant
- Topics: ES-06.5, ES-06.6, ES-07.3, ES-05.7, ES-08.13, ES-10.8, ES-01.3, ES-09.8, ES-01.1
- Chain of events:
  1. On 2026-10-30 the unified compose arms `LOOM_JEV=1` on the loom service with `LOOM_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD` default 1, pointing at the agentbox System One proxy `agentbox:8094`, selected backend TypeSafe — a cloud endpoint — with gate, rank and support stages on (ES-06.5, ES-06.6).
  2. The same change flips the memory rerank to cloud-by-default ("the query and capped value snippets of the shortlist go to the selected backend, cloud TypeSafe by default", ES-07.3), the pod forge rerank (`SOLID_POD_JEV=1` with `SOLID_POD_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD=1`, ES-08.13), and the governance rubric ("once activated the card text reaches the selected backend", ES-05.7).
  3. The activation was staged on a new agentbox image plus service recreate. The corpus disagrees with itself about whether that happened: ES-01.3 says "the running container still dials loopback", while the ES-09.8 close-out says "LIVE: both activation conditions … happened on 2026-10-30". Twelve months on, the path is live.
  4. The loom façade is the email privacy system; its own invariant says it "delegates ONLY to a LAN/local model, never to a cloud endpoint, so mail content never leaves the LAN" (ES-06.1 INV2). The JEV gate/rank stages sit inside that same façade and now ship request-derived material — engaged scaffolds, candidate rankings — to the proxy and onward to the cloud. The loom and pod authors' own code headers declare this content private and default local-only (`../loom/crates/loom-facade/src/jev.rs:30-34`; `../solid-pod-rs/crates/solid-pod-rs-server/src/jev.rs:24-33`) — the compose defaults override both.
  5. The proxy is a shared door: it binds `0.0.0.0` inside the container on the shared bridge, so "any container on visionclaw_network can reach it and spend the vendor key, an accepted consequence" (ES-10.8 D6), and its thresholds are "unset and uncalibrated" (ES-09.8); in the loom, "the gate may withhold an engaged scaffold" (ES-06.5), so the cloud call can also alter answers.
  6. Client-confidential content reaches a third-party vendor with no measured benefit ("no measured uplift", ES-06.6); the operator cannot demonstrate the LAN-only guarantee; the client terminates on discovery.
- Evidence: ES-06.1 INV2; ES-06.5 JEV note (`jev.rs:232-234`, `../project/docker-compose.unified.yml:387-397`, `jev.rs:353`); ES-06.6 Open (`docker-compose.unified.yml:395`, `jev.rs:30-34`); ES-07.3 Drift (`../project/agentbox/mcp/servers/lib/memory-jev-rerank.js:16-18,107`; `agentbox.toml:895`); ES-05.7 Open; ES-08.13 (`agentbox.toml:921`, `system_one.rs:578-602`, `lib.rs:5130`, `jev.rs:153-181`, `jev.rs:24-33`); ES-10.8 D6 (`agentbox.toml:893-894`, `ADR-2139…:287`, `server.rs:151-169`, `check-ports-loopback.mjs:740-745`); ES-01.3 Open vs ES-09.8 SYS1 (the activation-state conflict); ES-01.1 DIVSP (three solid-pod-rs pins — crates.io alpha.17, Nix alpha.18, guide alpha.13 — where the alpha.18 pin is the one carrying `SOLID_POD_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD`).
- Warning signs visible today: the compose default contradicts the invariant its own consumers rely on; two upstream authors' headers declare the payload private; the uplift is unmeasured; multiple consumers were flipped to cloud-by-default in one change; the corpus cannot agree on whether the path is live.
- Confidence: high that the configuration exists and contradicts the stated invariant; the incident sequence itself is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Erasure is a no-op across stores: the pod delete path has no reverse tombstone into RuVector, so deleted client data stays semantically searchable
- Topics: ES-08.9, ES-07.9, ES-08.8, ES-08.11
- Chain of events:
  1. Client material enters the estate and agents store it as memory through the governed MCP (embed-then-write into ruvector-postgres) and/or as pod resources.
  2. The client requests erasure. The operator invokes `deleteAgentMemory` → `agentMemory.ts` issues an LDP DELETE; the pod resource returns 204, and 404 is treated as success (`ldpClient.ts:206-216`).
  3. The call graph never reaches RuVector: "no reverse-tombstone dispatch to mcp claude-flow memory_delete or equivalent" (`agentMemory.ts:196-204`). The embedding row persists and "stays semantically searchable".
  4. There is no way to repair this after the fact: "No point-in-time RuVector backup exists, so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO" (ES-07.9 D5), and the pod store likewise has "no point-in-time backup … scripts/backup-sqlite.sh covers SQLite only" (ES-08.8). Live erasure acceptance is explicitly outstanding (ES-08.11).
  5. Later, an agent's `memory_search` surfaces the deleted client's content semantically and it appears in a deliverable or a mirrored/governed artifact. The client learns their deletion did not delete.
  6. The governing rule is violated by construction: "Invariant 6 … requires a dropped secondary write to be logged, not swallowed — this call path does not even attempt the secondary write, let alone log its absence" (ES-08.9).
- Evidence: ES-08.9 diagram and notes (`agentMemory.ts:41,196-204`; `ldpClient.ts:206-216`); ES-07.9 D5 ("Largest erasure hole"); ES-08.8 FATAL note; ES-08.11 closeout ("The durable reconciliation journal has no production destructive adapters: subject/authority mappings and live erasure acceptance remain outstanding"); ES-07.1 INV1 (bypass-written rows are invisible to HNSW search, which also makes verifying erasure by search unreliable).
- Warning signs visible today: the authors themselves label it the estate's largest erasure hole; no backup, no RPO/RTO, no destructive adapter anywhere in the tree.
- Confidence: high that the defect is exactly as described; the incident sequence is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — One agent process inside agentbox reaches every secret, can stamp any did:nostr over loopback, and escapes to the host through the widened Docker socket — the same-UID boundary is the whole container security model
- Topics: ES-10.12, ES-02.1, ES-04.1, ES-04.5, ES-10.10, ES-10.8, ES-10.11
- Chain of events:
  1. `role_isolation` remains off — it is the shipped default, rebuild-class since ER-9, pending the owner's boot rehearsal.
  2. With the flag off, "the entrypoint widens the host Docker socket o+rw for devuser (`entrypoint-unified.sh:598-602`)" and "every program but bootstrap and tailscale runs as devuser, and PID 1's environment, .env included, reaches all of them" (ES-10.12). A prompt-injected or dependency-compromised agent process therefore holds every env-borne secret, including `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, gateway tokens and the plaintext `.env` itself (ES-10.10 D5: "SOPS never executed … VisionClaw .env is PLAINTEXT today").
  3. Claude Code sessions run `bypassPermissions` by default and the docker/ssh deny rules "are pattern matches a sh -c wrapper evades, so it is access control, not a vault entry" (`agentbox.toml:698-713`, ES-10.10 C9/D6) — the documented guard does not stop the process that has the socket.
  4. Over loopback the process stamps `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` with any pubkey: at this stamp the header is honoured from loopback peers (`agent-event-auth.js:98-106,117`; `action-plane.js:228-232`), so "a process inside the agentbox container can still stamp any pubkey over loopback (TODO-unified X-1)" (ES-02.1 Debt, ES-04.1 Debt). Agent-events, task spawns and journal attribution are forged under any `did:nostr`, including the operator's, and VisionClaw ingests them as attributed.
  5. As uid 1000 it calls the session-plane socket — the ACL "authorises a UID, not a process" (`session-plane-acl.json:6`, `api.rs:32`, ES-04.5 D3) — driving sessions including the OpenRouter-spending router seed; on the bridge it reaches the System One proxy and spends the vendor key (ES-10.8 D6).
  6. Via the raw Docker socket it mounts the host root; the socket is the host inode and "earlier boots left it o+rw" (ES-10.11 OPEN2). The bridge/unwrap key's default path `/run/secrets/nostr.key` (ES-04.5 K1) sits in the same uid domain — no in-container boundary separates it (inferred from the same-uid statement).
- Evidence: as cited per step above; plus ES-10.12 GR (the one baked mitigation — devuser out of group root — does not affect same-uid reads).
- Warning signs visible today: the corpus states the boundary is same-UID and calls the loopback stamping a known Debt (TODO-unified X-1); the socket degradation marker `degraded:docker-socket` exists because the entrypoint cannot narrow it; the rehearsal that would flip the flag is still open.
- Confidence: high that every link in the chain is as documented; the compromise trigger is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — A Cloudflare tunnel can front the demo-open or dev-mode backend, exposing the graph anonymously to the public internet, invisible to the port audit
- Topics: ES-10.3, ES-09.8, ES-10.6
- Evidence: the shipped compose defaults describe demo-open (`RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1` line 121, `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1` line 122, `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1` line 105) while the code defaults are fail-closed — vs the fact that the standalone cloudflared "fronts WHATEVER profile the backend booted", its route is a dashboard Public Hostname "NOT reviewable from this repo", it is "invisible to the ADR-2013 compose port-audit", and ADR-2119 "leaves this standalone file unaffected" so "VISIONCLAW_INGRESS=lan … does not stop a hand-started standalone tunnel fronting a LAN-declared host" (ES-10.3 LANGAP). The unified cloudflared's `depends_on` accepts "visionclaw OR visionclaw-production (optional)" (ES-09.8), and the dev-mode bypass is total and peer-agnostic across REST and WS (ES-10.6); ADR-2038 refuses findings only in non-debug builds — dev builds log and continue (`security_profile.rs:657-661`).
- Failure: the standalone tunnel (or the unified tunnel profile attached to the dev service) is started while a dev/demo-open container runs; internet clients obtain anonymous reads, or with `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=1` unauthenticated writes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open/LANGAP)

### F-02 — Nothing verifies a shipped release binary omits dev-auth; the CI gate is a text scan of Dockerfiles, and a dev-auth release build ships the hygiene check as a no-op
- Topics: ES-09.5, ES-09.11, ES-10.2
- Evidence: the claim "a production/release image must never carry the dev-auth cargo feature" (ci.yml invariant) vs the fact that "no CI or image-build assertion yet verifies a shipped release binary omits dev-auth — a mis-targeted pipeline could promote the stubbed-hygiene binary to production undetected" (ES-09.5, ADR-2037 implementation_status none); the `dev-auth-release-gate` job is a "hermetic text assertion over the committed Dockerfiles/entrypoints … no cargo, no docker, no network" checking only that no `--release` cargo line names dev-auth (`ci.yml:146-155,163-168`). With the feature on, `enforce_release_env_hygiene` is the no-op stub (`main.rs:169`), so the `SETTINGS_AUTH_BYPASS`/`ALLOW_INSECURE_DEFAULTS` hard-fails of ES-10.2 never execute.
- Failure: any build path outside the scanned files produces release+dev-auth; the bearer dev-session-token bypass and bypass env vars work in production.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (the gap is stated in the ADR-2037 note)

### F-03 — Governance corpus promotion ignores expiry and projection: a promote applies without reading the projection receipt or the deadline
- Topics: ES-13.1
- Evidence: expiry is implemented as a forum case transition (`cron.rs:764,794,834`) vs the fact that the consumer's "Explicit promote checks request tag and IRI, then applies without reading projection receipt or expiry (`local-process-manager.js:419,444,461`)"; labelled TENSION: "corpus mutation does not wait for the forum's successful state transition (`relay-consumer.js:497`)".
- Failure: a proposal the forum has closed as expired is still promoted to the corpus on delivery or replay; the corpus carries a change the governance surface records as dead.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-04 — The consumer's seen-checkpoint persists unfinished decisions, permanently suppressing them after a restart
- Topics: ES-13.2
- Evidence: events are added to the shared seen map before dispatch (`relay-consumer.js:464`) vs the fact that the checkpoint "serialise[s] whole seen map, including B" (`relay-consumer.js:470,395`) and the restored consumer rejects redelivery (`relay-consumer.js:459`); a deterministic fixture reproduced suppression, and "recovery by another reconciler was not demonstrated", with governance records omitting the original signature.
- Failure: a crash mid-handler means a signed human decision never applies and never re-applies; governance state silently diverges until manual reconciliation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-05 — No VisionClaw case can ever trigger a rationale gate: nothing declares a tier, so every case folds to medium
- Topics: ES-05.12, ES-05.13
- Evidence: the canon rule that no surface may fabricate or waive a human rationale (ADR-2010) vs the verified fact that VisionClaw's 31400/31402 carry no tier field (`events.rs:211-217,91-101,308-323`; `elevation_actor.rs:239`), so the relay "folds every VisionClaw case to the advertised default, medium … NEITHER rationale gate fires on a VisionClaw case" (`governance.rs:769,525`; `wrangler.toml:61`).
- Failure: a high-consequence action decided on a VisionClaw panel is accepted with no human rationale; post-incident audit finds the escalation boundary was silently below what the canon requires.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-06 — The production forum edge still runs the kit on k256 0.13.4 / crypto-bigint 0.5.5 while main is fixed for RUSTSEC-2026-0387
- Topics: ES-05.14
- Evidence: "forum main (1b27f79) is fixed for RUSTSEC-2026-0387, with k256 0.14.0 … but forum production still runs kit 9c36b60, with crypto-bigint 0.5.5 and k256 0.13.4, so main is fixed and the edge is not"; the Workers deploy pins that kit (`workers-deploy.yml:44`).
- Failure: whatever the advisory permits is exploitable on the live relay that admits and stores governance events and receipts until the pin moves; the material does not state the advisory's impact.
- Confidence: high that the skew exists; low on impact (unstated)
- Severity: high (signature-stack advisory on the live auth path; impact not characterised in the material)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — The pod ledger's value paths hand-roll Schnorr/TapSighash and keep a reachable TXO "free-money" stand-in deposit, if the settlement activates before the rust-bitcoin port ships
- Topics: ES-08.12, ES-03.12, ES-03.11
- Evidence: the settlement pack proposes the chain as sole value instrument vs the fact that the released crate "HAND-ROLLS it: BIP-341 scripts, the TapSighash and key-path Schnorr on raw k256" (`ADR-2008…:22-26`), its own README says "not to carry value through the payment routes until it is fixed" (`:72-75`), the "TXO stand-in deposit … credits sats for any parseable TXO URI guarded only by a replay key" (`:67-71`), and the rust-bitcoin port is "landed on main, unreleased" (`:119-158`).
- Failure: settlement goes live with real value on the released crate: hand-rolled-crypto bugs lose or mangle funds, or anyone credits themselves via the stand-in deposit.
- Confidence: high on the facts; the failure requires activation and is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (PROPOSED sections carrying the warnings; DIVERGENCE on the three unsynced ledgers)

### F-08 — Secrets at rest: VisionClaw `.env` is plaintext (SOPS never executed) and the backup script writes an ordinary unencrypted ZIP
- Topics: ES-10.10
- Evidence: the custody register's stated purpose vs "SOPS never executed (legacy ADR-109, accepted 2026-05-09) — VisionClaw .env is PLAINTEXT today" and "backup-secrets.sh still writes ordinary ZIP with an integrity check, without explicit encryption or permission hardening"; every custodian, rotation cadence and incident-response window is UNCONFIRMED.
- Failure: the `.env` (reachable to every devuser process per ES-10.12) or a backup artifact leaks and is readable as-is; rotation cannot be demonstrated to the client.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence / two-source-paths)

### F-09 — No point-in-time backup, RPO or RTO for the pod store or RuVector — and an ENOSPC-class persistence failure has already occurred once
- Topics: ES-08.8, ES-07.9, ES-10.11
- Evidence: the estate's data-authority expectations vs "no point-in-time backup for the pod store, scripts/backup-sqlite.sh covers SQLite only … no cross-store consistent restore, no declared RPO or RTO" (ES-08.8) and the same for RuVector (ES-07.9 D5); a prior "ENOSPC block-persistence failure" in the chain plane is on record (ES-10.11 GATEDRIFT).
- Failure: disk-full or corruption on the pod/RuVector volumes destroys agent memory, pod resources and provenance with no restore point; combined with R-2 there is no consistent cross-store state to restore to.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-10 — The model-router privacy guard is pinned by the wrapper, so the console's exit-on-private-tier can never fire; the tier is declared, not detected
- Topics: ES-06.10
- Evidence: INVARIANT "privacy_tier is pinned to public (`agentbox.toml:1744`) and the console EXITS on any other value. Private corpora never reach an OpenRouter model" vs the recorded Tension that "the router wrapper pins the tier to public before it starts the console, so setting AGENTBOX_MODEL_ROUTER_PRIVACY_TIER=private cannot make the wrapper exit non-zero as the acceptance text asks" (ADR-2080 `:129-132`), left to an owner ruling.
- Failure: a task carrying client-confidential content is routed through the router console; nothing inspects content, the declared tier is always public, and the material goes to OpenRouter — plane separation holds only by operator convention.
- Confidence: high on the pinning fact; the content-flow failure is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-11 — A degraded boot silently mints a placeholder `did:nostr:local` identity and pod writes go unsigned while `sign_requests` is off
- Topics: ES-04.4, ES-08.2
- Evidence: the sovereignty claim (all writes attributed to a held key) vs "the did:nostr:local placeholder fallback that `agent-identity.js:41-43` says the entrypoint still runs unconditionally, a degraded boot can silently produce a NON-SOVEREIGN identity" (ES-04.4) and "default keeps prior unsigned behaviour byte-identical (`pod-signer.js:34-35`)"; ADR-2064's fail-closed `requireSigned` applies only with the flag on — "It remains reachable only with the flag off, where unsigned is the declared baseline" (ES-08.2).
- Failure: a boot with a missing or undecryptable key writes pod data unsigned and/or under a placeholder identity; accountability, erasure targeting and provenance are wrong for everything written until it is noticed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-12 — `memory_search` accepts namespace `*` for global cross-namespace search beside the pubkey-scoping invariant — suspected cross-principal read
- Topics: ES-07.1, ES-07.3
- Evidence: ES-07.1 SCOPE invariant "NIP-98 callers are scoped to their own pubkey namespace (user:<pubkey>:proj:<repo-slug>:<ns>); a session cannot read another session's per-project namespace (`agentbox.toml:379-395`)" vs the unqualified ES-07.3 note "namespace '*' performs a global cross-namespace search" — the material never states who may supply `*` or whether scoping precedes expansion.
- Failure: if `*` is caller-supplied and expanded before pubkey scoping, any caller reads every principal's memories, including other users'/clients' material; as written the two statements conflict.
- Confidence: low-medium; inferred (the conflict is real; its resolution is unstated)
- Severity: high (if confirmed)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — The System One proxy's shared vendor key is spendable by any container on the bridge, and the exposure is invisible to the port audit by design
- Topics: ES-10.8
- Evidence: the loopback-publish invariant (ADR-2013) vs "the System One proxy binds 0.0.0.0 inside the container when network is true (`agentbox.toml:893-894`), never published, so any container on visionclaw_network can reach it and spend the vendor key, an accepted consequence (`ADR-2139…:287`)", refusing only an Origin header or non-JSON content type (`server.rs:151-169`) — checks a non-browser client trivially satisfies; the gate "is structurally blind to a container-internal 0.0.0.0 bind on a shared bridge" (D5, PROPOSED).
- Failure: any compromised or future sidecar container silently spends the cloud key and channels private payloads through it (compounding R-1); spend and egress cannot be attributed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (accepted consequence; PROPOSED D5)

### F-14 — The NIP-98 replay cache fails closed when full: any principal holding a valid signing key can wedge authentication for everyone
- Topics: ES-04.3
- Evidence: the stated invariant "on a FULL cache the code REFUSES new auth (ReplayCacheFull) … Bounded memory beats availability" (`nip98.rs:189-197`) vs the stated ordering that entries are claimed only after signature verification (`nip98.rs:573`) — so only genuinely-signed tokens occupy the 4096 slots, and any accepted key (including a subverted agent process per R-3) can mint unlimited unique valid tokens.
- Failure: a flood of unique valid tokens fills the cache; every other principal's NIP-98 auth on that surface is refused until TTL pruning — an estate-wide ingress outage caused by an authenticated insider.
- Confidence: medium; the mechanism is stated, the attack is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (presented as a deliberate invariant)

### F-15 — The host-vs-container build trap: a "fix" applied from inside the container silently serves stale code with health green
- Topics: ES-09.1, ES-09.2, ES-01.5
- Evidence: the claim that a launched change is deployed vs the ENV-FACT/TRAP that a compose command "issued from inside CC" resolves bind paths against the host filesystem so the "Running dev container serves the image-build-time COPY'd source, NOT the just-edited host file … the trap: container starts, health check passes, edits never take effect" (ES-09.1); the same trap is repeated for agentbox builds (ES-01.5 D2).
- Failure: during incident response an operator or agent applies a security fix from inside the container; health stays green, the vulnerability persists, and exposure continues while the team believes the fix shipped.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (labelled TRAP in the material)

## nostr-rust-forum / critical

### F-01 — Production runs the crypto stack with an unpatched RUSTSEC advisory; the fix exists only on main
- Topics: NF-01.4, NF-06.18
- Evidence: NF-01.4 Drift states "The RUSTSEC-2026-0387 dependency bump is fixed on main (`1b27f79`): k256 and p256 are `0.14.0` … Production is not fixed: the edge still runs kit `9c36b60`, which resolves crypto-bigint `0.5.5` and k256 `0.13.4`". The edge pin `9c36b60` is recorded as live in `../dreamlab-ai-website/docs/architecture/kit-compatibility-record.md:30`. Every signature verification, NIP-44 and ECDH path (NF-12.2, NF-12.4) in the running deployment uses the old versions; main's `Cargo.lock` describes a different deployment than the one serving traffic.
- Failure: whatever RUSTSEC-2026-0387 describes is exploitable on the live edge (event admission verification, DM encryption) until the pin is advanced. A green audit on main says nothing about production.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, NF-01.4)

### F-02 — Mixed-case pubkeys bypass the self-review and original-signer guards in the governance core
- Topics: NF-10.11, NF-06.15, NF-02.10, NF-06.6
- Evidence: NF-10.11 N6 (Debt KF-6): "the self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-SENSITIVELY while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:29`), enforced in `record_decision` (`nostr-bbs-core/src/governance.rs:1709`) and on the supersession path (`:1806`). NF-06.15 N1 proves the same key legitimately verifies in two casings ("the event id is recomputed over that string, so the SAME key can legitimately present in two casings and both verify", `governance_api.rs:98-100`), and the receipt endpoint already had to fix this exact class case-insensitively (`governance_api.rs:1181`, `:1189`).
- Failure: a case owner who holds decision authority re-cases their hex in a 31403; the case-sensitive equality against the stored lowercase creator misses, `CaseError::SelfReview` never fires, and the independence invariant of the whole human-oversight loop is violated by the same technique the registry already had to fix.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-6), though it is filed as debt rather than as a live bypass

### F-03 — The static-admin variable is declared in exactly one template — with a real stranger's pubkey — while the two workers that read it ship without it
- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-07.2, NF-10.6 (DD3), NF-10.7 (S2)
