- Evidence: `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` is read by the auth worker (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/src/admin.rs:71`) and the relay (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/auth.rs:185`) but "DECLARED in exactly one wrangler template - the search worker, `nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:41`", which ships "a REAL 64-hex pubkey … the only non-generic value in any template". The relay template asserts the opposite ("no ADMIN_PUBKEYS reader in src/", `nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:18-21`) and `SETUP.md:147-151` never lists it. The search worker uses it as its ONLY admin source (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/src/auth.rs:52-54`).
- Failure: two modes. (a) A by-the-book deployment has no static admin on relay or auth; with no D1 admin rows either, there is no in-product first-admin bootstrap — admin functions (agent provisioning, channel-zone writes, moderation) require hand-editing D1. (b) Any deployment that keeps the shipped search value: whoever holds that real key is admin of `/ingest` and can mark content public via `publicLabels` (NF-07.4 N2), exposing private content in search.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT DD3; NF-08.7 N4, N5)

### F-04 — Preview-worker SSRF guard is denylist-only, DNS-rebinding-vulnerable and has no wall-clock timeout; the mitigation flag is set by no template
- Topics: NF-07.6, NF-07.5, NF-10.7 (S3)
- Evidence: the code's own admission: "without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS the guard is denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable" (`nostr-bbs-preview-worker/src/ssrf.rs:13`); "there is NO wall-clock timeout - only redirect-count and body-size limits bound a fetch" (NF-07.6 N3); `nostr-bbs-preview-worker/wrangler.toml:13` declares only `ALLOWED_ORIGIN`.
- Failure: a member posts a link whose hostname resolves differently at check time and fetch time (rebinding), reaching a target the denylist would have refused; or a host that drip-feeds under the 2 MiB/10 MiB caps ties up preview-worker invocations indefinitely. The guard's own author states the mitigation is the allowlist, and no deployment step sets it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE NF-07.6 N3, N4)

### F-05 — Governance read endpoints verify a signature but no membership: "authenticated is effectively anonymous", and the admin-only reviewer view is reconstructable
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-2, KF-2a), NF-02.10
- Evidence: "the governance READ endpoints verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:23-24`), recorded as "an operator decision, taken once for both".
- Failure: anyone who can mint any nostr key — no invitation, no membership — enumerates all broker cases and decisions (titles, subjects, summaries, rationales, outcomes) and rebuilds the reviewer telemetry the API admin-gates (`governance_api.rs:1407`). Governance and moderation deliberations leak off-instance.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-2, KF-2a)

### F-06 — Zone encryption is off by omission: the master gate is declared in no wrangler.toml, so every default deployment stores "encrypted" zones in plaintext
- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.8 (N7), NF-05.16, NF-09.4
- Evidence: `is_encrypted = gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT is_public_read` (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/zone_config.rs:133`, gate read at `:86`), but "ENCRYPTION_ENABLED … declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker … a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF" (NF-08.2 N4). The config validator only rejects `encrypted && visibility = public` (`nostr-bbs-config/src/validate.rs:250`) and cannot see the runtime gate.
- Failure: the operator marks zones `encrypted = true` in forum.toml; no template or SETUP line ever names the env var, so the relay's ciphertext shape gate (NF-08.9) never fires, clients with the same gate off compose plaintext, and members' "encrypted" board content is stored and served in the clear while both the config and the ADR-2016/2018 narrative imply protection.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT NF-08.2 N4) — but only as a doc gap, not as a default-plaintext deployment consequence

### F-07 — Advisory AI flags ship cloud-on: governance case content and member search queries leave to a third party by default
- Topics: NF-02.2, NF-06.19, NF-07.9, NF-08.8 (N8)
- Evidence: the committed auth template sets `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK = "1"` (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:53`) with `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK_LOCAL_ONLY` default off (`rationale_check.rs:38`, read at `:303`), "so the proxy or TypeSafe with `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` may answer"; the route reads "the case row: title, subject, summary, effective tier, task properties" (`rationale_check.rs:279`). The search manifest sets `JEV_SEARCH_RERANK = "1"` (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:32`) with locality off, and "the query text and up to ten public snippets leave the Worker" (`docs/jev-option.md:38`). NF-06.19 records the website overlay deploying from this pin "with no receipt for the auth Worker's secret".
- Failure: a deployment from the checked-in templates ships every reviewer's case material and members' search queries to the TypeSafe endpoint unless the operator knows to find and flip two undocumented-by-default locality switches; discovery after the fact is a third-party data-exposure incident.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open in NF-06.19/NF-07.9) — but framed as an unmeasured research option, not as default egress

### F-08 — NIP-42's relay-tag check is silently skipped while RELAY_URL is blank — the shipped default — and AUTH errors spam the log
- Topics: NF-03.2, NF-08.8, NF-09.5
- Evidence: "own_relay_url unset or blank SKIPS the relay-tag check only. This fails OPEN on the defence-in-depth check" (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/relay_do/nip42.rs:168-172`); `RELAY_URL` ships blank (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:51`) and "handle_auth now logs a console error on every AUTH while it is blank" (`nip_handlers.rs:230`, `nip_handlers.rs:2054`).
- Failure: default-deployed relays accept AUTH events whose relay tag names any relay; the per-session challenge binding still holds, but the documented defense-in-depth layer is absent while NIP-11 advertises NIP-42 (`nip11.rs:207`), and the per-AUTH console error noise masks genuine signals.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (note in NF-03.2; wrangler.toml comment) — stated as an intentional rollout convenience, not as a gap

### F-09 — Replaceable-event storage is not latest-wins: stale duplicates are retained and returned, and a later timestamp erases a higher revision
- Topics: NF-13.3, NF-11.4, NF-11.6
- Evidence: the 1 November audit "reproduces late-older and same-second coexistence and loss of a higher proposed revision"; replacement deletes key on `(pubkey, kind)` / `(pubkey, kind, d_tag)` before insert (`relay_do/storage.rs:98-121`), reads order by descending `created_at` (`storage.rs:320-323`), "Revision tags are not part of the current replacement key or comparison", and the ADR-2031 migration "is still a prerequisite".
- Failure: a late-arriving older kind-0/31400 is retained and served alongside the newer one, so clients pick arbitrarily between two "current" profiles or panel definitions; a same-second pair coexists; a lower-revision publish with a later `created_at` deletes a higher-revision update outright — members see retracted or stale panels as current with no error anywhere.
- Confidence: high (reproduced in SQLite by the audit)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (NF-13 audit; NF-11.4 pointer)

### F-10 — The library's own group-metadata builder produces events the relay rejects, while NIP-11 advertises NIP-29 support
- Topics: NF-13.1, NF-12.6, NF-11.14, NF-10.3 (O2)
- Evidence: `create_group_metadata` emits `h`, no `d` (`nostr-bbs-core/src/groups.rs:137,146`); `build_group_metadata` emits `d`, no `h` (`groups.rs:254,263`); the current gate "requires h and admin" and "rejects missing h" (`nip_handlers.rs:1299-1307`, deny at `:1304`); "Relay-key-generated metadata remains TODO" (`nip_handlers.rs:1300-1301`); `supported_nips` still lists 29 (`nip11.rs:207`).
- Failure: any client or tool using the current builder gets validly signed 39000-39002 events refused — group lifecycle is impossible through the crate's own path — while third-party NIP-29 clients, told the relay supports 29, create legacy-shape metadata that the proposed relay-signed engine (ADR-2030) will not honor.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (NF-13 audit; anomaly O2; NF-12.6 note)

### F-11 — The read path has no group-membership gate: whatever group data exists is readable by any authenticated reader
- Topics: NF-13.2, NF-03.14, NF-11.5
- Evidence: the audit: "Current read protection does not include NIP-29 group membership … Anonymous group kinds pass the tested pure predicates; correspondence remains protected"; "No matching gate ends in Deliver" (`nip_handlers.rs:2372`). The protected set is 4/13/14/1059/30910-16 (`nip42.rs:85`) and excludes group kinds; the audit demands the future rollback "preserve protection for any private group data stored after activation" — implying none exists today.
- Failure: while NIP-29 is advertised, any whitelisted member subscribes to group kinds and reads them without membership; private group content becomes instance-wide the moment any group is actually used.
- Confidence: medium-high (the absent gate is stated; exposure depends on group data existing, which F-10 currently limits)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (NF-13 audit) — as a forward requirement, not as a current-defect statement

### F-12 — The device-key security model is dormant by default, revocation is inert when the flag is off, and moderation gates key on the raw signing key
- Topics: NF-02.7, NF-10.11 (KF-4, KF-9), NF-03.4
- Evidence: both templates ship `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED = "false"` (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:69`, `nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:29`); "with the gate off a known device to owner mapping is IGNORED and revocation has no effect at AUTH" (NF-02.7 DIVERGENCE). KF-4: after the write gate resolves a device key to its owner, "the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW signing pubkey" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:27`).
- Failure: an instance that once enabled device keys and then loses the flag (or deploys fresh over existing rows): a stolen device key keeps authenticating as its owner and revoking it does nothing at AUTH. Where device keys are on, ban/mute gates keyed on the raw key miss owner-level sanctions.
- Confidence: high (all stated); exploitation requires the flag off-with-rows or on
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE NF-02.7; Debt KF-4/KF-9)

### F-13 — Per-IP rate limits and connection accounting live only in DO memory and are wiped on hibernation; every socket funnels through one singleton DO
- Topics: NF-11.1, NF-11.2, NF-03.4, NF-03.1
- Evidence: "EVERY field except state and env is volatile in-memory cache. Hibernation wipes all of it" (NF-11.1) — including `rate_limits` (`relay_do/mod.rs:90`) and `connection_counts` (`:95`). Wake-time recovery restores "challenge, subscriptions, authed_pubkey" (`relay_do/session.rs:186-218`) and the material names no restoration of rate-limit or connection-count state. The DO is a singleton: "every socket goes to get_by_name main" (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/lib.rs:230`).
- Failure: an attacker pacing bursts across DO hibernations resets the per-IP EVENT limit (NF-03.4 step 1) and the AUTH-frame limiter each wake, so effective ceilings are per-wake, not per-minute; after hibernation, connection counts undercount recovered sockets, letting one IP exceed the slot cap; and all sessions share one DO's frame processing, so one hot channel's per-session broadcast matching delays every other session.
- Confidence: medium — the volatility and singleton are stated; the reset/undercount consequences are inferred from what recovery is documented to restore
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — The channel-creator persistence write result is discarded: a silent failure or pre-existing channel falls back to the deletable kind-40 event
- Topics: NF-03.7 (N4, N5), NF-11.4, NF-10.3 (O5)
- Evidence: "the creator INSERT result is discarded (let _ =) … a channel whose write failed has no row and is still on the deletion-exposed fallback" (`relay_do/storage.rs:171`, fallback at `nip_handlers.rs:2549`); "Channels created before the table existed have no row either" (`nip_handlers.rs:2534-2535`); a kind-5 deletion of the kind-40 event removes the fallback source (`nip_handlers.rs:2457`) — the exact O5 regression reopened.
- Failure: a D1 hiccup during channel creation, or any channel predating the table: the TL2 creator later loses the ability to edit their own channel metadata once the kind-40 event is deleted, silently, with no error surfaced anywhere.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT NF-03.7 N4)

### F-15 — Account recovery distributes the root nsec in a URL fragment, contradicting the "key never leaves the device" posture
- Topics: NF-05.8, NF-05.2, NF-05.3
- Evidence: the recovery sheet renders the nsec as an in-WASM QR (`nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/components/recovery_sheet.rs:66`, `:294`) and a "/connect magic link with the nsec in the URL FRAGMENT" (`recovery_sheet.rs:290`); the material defends only that "browsers do not send the fragment to the server". NF-05.3 claims "The private key never leaves the device". NF-10.2 K6: the root recovers every subkey.
- Failure: the full URL including `#k=nsec` lands in the recovering device's browser history, tab sync and screen shares; anyone with later access to that machine or synced history obtains the member's master key — root compromise of the identity, not a contained subkey.
- Confidence: medium (mechanism stated; history/sync exposure inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. The content and reachability of RUSTSEC-2026-0387 under the production pin `9c36b60` — the advisory text and that pin's `Cargo.lock` are needed to size F-01.
2. The actually deployed vars/secrets per worker versus the template gaps — whether production sets `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED`, `ADMIN_PUBKEYS`, `RELAY_URL`, `PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS` and `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` anywhere outside the committed templates (sizes F-03, F-04, F-06, F-07, F-08).
3. Live D1 contents: whether any NIP-29 group events exist, whether every channel has a `channel_creators` row, and whether any mixed-case pubkeys are already stored in `broker_cases`/`broker_decisions` (sizes F-02, F-09, F-10, F-11, F-14).
4. Durable Object runtime behavior the design leans on: real hibernation frequency against the 60s/300s caches, whether connection counts are recomputed on wake, per-DO concurrency and socket limits, and whether D1 `batch` is truly transactional for the receipts and trust-sweep paths that claim atomicity.
5. The external halves this repo's guarantees depend on and this material cannot show: agentbox's NIP-98 termination and JS key-derivation mirror, VisionClaw's dispatch journal (ADR-2010's consumer half), and the payment chain source's reorg/staleness handling now that production runs `PAY_ENABLED = "true"`.

## nostr-rust-forum / premortem

Post-mortem, twelve months on. Basis: only the cited topic material; every claim below carries the citations the material itself gives. Where I reason past what the diagrams state, the Confidence line says so.

---

### R-1 — "Encrypted" zones store plaintext whenever the deployment omits one undeclared variable
- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.8, NF-08.9, NF-03.12, NF-05.16, NF-09.4, NF-09.5
- Chain of events:
  1. Operator deploys by SETUP.md. No step sets `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` (NF-09.5; the secrets step covers two unrelated secrets, SETUP.md:155), and no wrangler template declares it in `[vars]` anywhere (NF-08.8 N7).
  2. The relay loads zone config from env (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/zone_config.rs:86`) and computes `is_encrypted = ENCRYPTION_ENABLED gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT is_public_read` (`zone_config.rs:133`). Gate false ⇒ every zone is treated as unencrypted.
  3. The encrypted-zone write gate (`nip_handlers.rs:1458`) and the NIP-44 shape check (NF-08.9) never fire, so plaintext kind-42 content is accepted into zones the operator marked `encrypted` in forum.toml.
  4. Client-side, grant sync is dormant until `encryption_enabled()` is true (`nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/app.rs:796-800`, `zone_crypto/mod.rs:99`), so member keys/encryption never engage; anything published — including a script publishing straight to the relay — is stored and served in cleartext.
  5. Family/business zone content sits readable at rest in relay D1, and relay text search matches it on content (NF-05.10 N4: only ciphertext posts are discarded from search). A client learns the "end-to-end encrypted" zones were cleartext at rest and searchable; confidentiality failure, client leaves.
- Evidence: NF-08.2 N4 ("ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is read from env at `zone_config.rs:86` but declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker … a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF"); NF-08.8 N7 (same, "undeclared in every wrangler.toml"); formula at `zone_config.rs:133`; gate at `nip_handlers.rs:1458`; NF-05.16 N3 (client dormant with gate off); NF-09.4 (validator rejects only `encrypted && visibility = public`, `validate.rs:250` — nothing validates the runtime gate).
- Warning signs visible today: the drift note itself; the contrast with `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED`, which at least has declared defaults (NF-08.8); the config validator's blindness to the runtime gate (NF-09.4).
- Confidence: high that the silent-off behaviour exists (stated); the end-to-end incident chain is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Member and governance-case data leaves the operator's boundary to a vendor by shipped default
- Topics: NF-06.19, NF-07.9, NF-08.8, NF-02.2, NF-02.6
- Chain of events:
  1. The committed auth template sets `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK = "1"` (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:53`) and the search template sets `JEV_SEARCH_RERANK = "1"` pointing at TypeSafe `jev-latest` with a `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` secret (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:32-36`).
  2. Locality is per-consumer and default off: `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK_LOCAL_ONLY` (`rationale_check.rs:38`, read at `:303`) and `JEV_SEARCH_RERANK_LOCAL_ONLY` (`rerank.rs:22`) — so by default the vendor may answer.
  3. An admin runs a rationale check; the handler reads the case row — title, subject, summary, effective tier, task properties (`rationale_check.rs:279`) — and the proxy/TypeSafe answers (`rationale_check.rs:303`). Governance cases are projected from kind-31402 requests (NF-03.10, NF-06.6), i.e. moderation action requests naming members.
  4. In parallel, search sends query text plus up to ten public snippets off-box (`docs/jev-option.md:38`).
  5. A client or regulator discovers moderation-case content about identifiable members, and member queries, have been transiting TypeSafe since the 2026-10-30 pin; privacy/custody failure.
- Evidence: NF-02.2 Open ("since the 2026-10-30 pin, cloud by default … the proxy or TypeSafe with `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` may answer"); NF-06.19 N2 ("the shipped manifest is ON and cloud-allowed" is the search twin; the website overlay deployed from this pin "with no receipt for the auth Worker's secret"); NF-07.9 (query + ten public snippets leave the Worker); NF-08.8 N8 (all three templates ON, TypeSafe endpoints, secret-named key).
- Warning signs visible today: library defaults off but templates on (deployment-surface inversion, NF-02.2 Open); no measured uplift recorded anywhere (`docs/jev-option.md:11`, NF-07.9 Open), so the egress buys nothing documented; the route is advisory and no governed path depends on it (NF-06.19) — pure risk, no offsetting need.
- Confidence: high that default-on egress occurs (stated); the client-loss framing is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Preview worker runs denylist-only SSRF protection against DNS rebinding, with no fetch timeout
- Topics: NF-07.5, NF-07.6, NF-07.7, NF-10.7
- Chain of events:
  1. The template declares only `ALLOWED_ORIGIN` (`nostr-bbs-preview-worker/wrangler.toml:13`); `PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is never set, so the egress allowlist step (`ssrf.rs:323`) is inert.
  2. The code itself states the guard is then denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable because the Workers runtime has no resolve-then-pin primitive (`nostr-bbs-preview-worker/src/ssrf.rs:13`).
  3. A member posts a link whose hostname resolves to a permitted IP at check time and rebinds to a blocked target at fetch time; each redirect hop is re-checked but bounded only by `MAX_REDIRECTS = 3` (`ssrf.rs:33`) and there is no wall-clock timeout (NF-07.6 N3).
  4. The worker becomes an attacker-directed fetch proxy with Cloudflare egress, parsed and cached (cache keyed per target URL, NF-07.5), and unbounded in time per request.
- Evidence: NF-07.6 N3 ("NO wall-clock timeout"); N4 (acknowledged rebinding vulnerability at `ssrf.rs:13`; template sets only `ALLOWED_ORIGIN`); NF-10.7 S3 (same, as a deployment-shaped divergence); redirect re-checks at `ssrf.rs:174`/`:192`; size guards exist (`ssrf.rs:246`, `:257`) but are not the hole.
- Warning signs visible today: the vulnerability is acknowledged in the code and the mitigation (the allowlist) is named and absent from the shipped template.
- Confidence: high that the default posture is denylist-only and rebinding-vulnerable (stated); concrete impact on the Workers platform is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Production edge runs the pre-advisory crypto stack while the fix sits undeployed on main
- Topics: NF-01.4
- Evidence: "Production is not fixed: the edge still runs kit `9c36b60`, which resolves crypto-bigint `0.5.5` and k256 `0.13.4`" vs main `1b27f79` with k256/p256 `0.14.0` (`nostr-rust-forum/Cargo.toml:104-105`), the bump being the RUSTSEC-2026-0387 fix; the 0.14 line also needs a wasm32-only `getrandom` pin the edge build lacks (`crates/nostr-bbs-core/Cargo.toml:60`).
- Failure: the advisory is exploited or surfaces in a client's security audit of the live signing/verification path (`verify_event_strict`, NIP-44, NIP-98) months after the fix existed on main.
- Confidence: high that the version lag is real (stated); exploitability inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — No admin bootstrap on relay/auth, and the only declared ADMIN_PUBKEYS is a real stranger's key on the search worker
- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-07.2, NF-10.6, NF-10.7
- Evidence: `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` is declared only in the search template (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:41`) yet read by auth (`admin.rs:71`) and relay (`auth.rs:185`); the relay template asserts the opposite (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:18-21`); SETUP.md never lists it (`SETUP.md:147-151`); the search template's value is a real, non-placeholder pubkey — "the only non-generic value in any template".
- Failure: a by-the-book deployment has zero static admins on relay and auth (first admin only via raw D1 insert — lockout), while any deployment keeping the search default grants an outside key `/ingest`, the only mutating search route (NF-07.1 N2), letting it inject entries into members' public search index.
- Confidence: high (all stated).
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT DD3; consequence spelled out in NF-08.7 N5 and NF-10.7 S2)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Moderation gates key on the raw signing pubkey while the write gate resolves device owners
- Topics: NF-03.4, NF-02.7, NF-10.11
- Evidence: pipeline step 6 resolves the device key to its owner (`nip_handlers.rs:1121`) and step 9 checks suspension/silence "for the device key AND its owner" (`nip_handlers.rs:1189`), but KF-4/KF-9 record that the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW signing pubkey (`docs/security/known-findings.md:27`, `:44`); devices ship disabled in both templates (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:69`, `nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:29`).
- Failure: once an operator enables device keys, a member banned for abuse publishes kind-1/42 (NF-03.6's ban-gated set) through a pre-registered device key: the ban gate at `nip_handlers.rs:1213` checks the raw key, which is not banned, and the content is admitted; revocation is equally ineffective (NF-02.7 dormant-state note).
- Confidence: high (defect documented); the exploit path needs devices enabled.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-4, KF-9)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — Case-sensitive pubkey compares let an agent self-review or supersede by hex-casing trick
- Topics: NF-06.6, NF-06.15, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-6 — the self-review and original-signer guards compare case-sensitively while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim (`docs/security/known-findings.md:29`), "the same class the registry closed in NF-06.15, left open in the core aggregate"; NF-06.15 N1 proves the same key can legitimately present in two casings and both verify (`governance_api.rs:98-100`); guards at `governance.rs:1709` and `:1806`.
- Failure: a broker presents its pubkey in a different casing; `SelfReview` and the supersession original-signer check miss the match, so it decides its own case or supersedes a decision it never signed — governance integrity broken on the path that drives moderation outcomes.
- Confidence: high (documented; the registry fixed the identical class at `governance_api.rs:108`).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-6)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — Governance REST reads verify a signature but no membership, so "authenticated" is anonymous
- Topics: NF-02.6, NF-06.9, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-2/KF-2a — governance read endpoints do NIP-98 with no membership check, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read (`docs/security/known-findings.md:23-24`); list agents/cases/decisions/roles are `require_authed` only (NF-06.9 note).
- Failure: any freshly minted key reads moderation and governance decisions — ban/mute case content naming members — from off-instance, and reconstructs reviewer telemetry the operator believes admin-only (`governance_api.rs:1407`).
- Confidence: high (stated, operator-accepted).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-2, KF-2a)
- Severity: medium

### F-06 — Client falls back to a divergent hardcoded relay URL when injected env is missing
- Topics: NF-05.7, NF-05.1
- Evidence: O8 "re-verified and STILL LIVE": `relay.rs` reads `window.__ENV__` inline (`relay.rs:1045`) and its hardcoded fallback (`relay.rs:22`) diverges from the shared resolver's (`utils/relay_url.rs:9`).
- Failure: a mis-deployed static shell (env not injected) makes the client connect, NIP-42-AUTH and publish against the wrong default host — signed events and DM wraps land on a relay the operator does not control.
- Confidence: high that the divergence exists (stated); the wrong-host publication chain is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (ANOMALY O8)
- Severity: medium

### F-07 — Hibernation wipes the DO's in-memory rate limits and connection accounting
- Topics: NF-11.1, NF-11.2, NF-03.4
- Evidence: "EVERY field except state and env is volatile in-memory cache. Hibernation wipes all of it" — including `rate_limits` (`relay_do/mod.rs:90`) and `connection_counts` (`relay_do/mod.rs:95`); only challenge, subscriptions and `authed_pubkey` are persisted (`relay_do/session.rs:186-218`); the per-IP EVENT limit runs at `nip_handlers.rs:1057`.
- Failure: an attacker alternates bursts with idle to force hibernation; each wake restarts the per-IP admission limit and slot counts from zero, so the rate limit and per-IP connection ceiling are effectively unenforceable against a patient flood.
- Confidence: medium — volatility is stated; the evasion pattern is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — Discarded channel_creators write leaves channels on the deletion-exposed fallback
- Topics: NF-03.7, NF-11.4
- Evidence: the creator INSERT result is discarded (`relay_do/storage.rs:171`) and failures never block storage; pre-table channels have no row (`nip_handlers.rs:2534-2535`); the fallback re-reads the kind-40 event (`nip_handlers.rs:2549`).
- Failure: a kind-40 is stored while its `channel_creators` INSERT fails (or predates the table); a kind-5 deletion of the kind-40 then locks the TL2 creator out of their own channel metadata — the O5 failure mode reopened.
- Confidence: high (residual stated as DEBT).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — Closing an expired case retracts nothing already broadcast
- Topics: NF-06.16, NF-03.10, NF-06.11
- Evidence: Tension (2026-10-31): "Closing a case does not retract a decision already stored and broadcast"; the sweep closes without a decision (`cron.rs:731`, `CaseState::Closed` at `governance.rs:1346`).
- Failure: a decision on a since-expired ontology proposal remains live and projected; the elevation consumer applies a promote/demote whose premise (digest currency, NF-11.13 N8) no longer holds, mutating the corpus to the wrong state with no retraction path.
- Confidence: high (stated); downstream application is the documented consumer path (VC-24).
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — The zone-bound PWA bakes a key into a distributable artifact
- Topics: NF-05.11, NF-05.12
- Evidence: `bake_local` "binds the baked key to a zone" (`nostr-bbs-bbs-client/src/pwa.rs:185`) and persists a BootProfile (`pwa.rs:219`, `:466`); the `app.rs:132` guard makes it one-shot; where the key is minted versus embedded is unstated.
- Failure: if the baked key is embedded in the served bundle rather than minted per download, every recipient of the PWA holds the same signing identity bound to a zone and can publish as it.
- Confidence: low — inferred; the material does not state the key's provenance.

### F-11 — Recovery flow puts the nsec in a URL fragment on a deliberately public route
- Topics: NF-05.8, NF-05.2
- Evidence: the `/connect` magic link carries the nsec in the fragment (`recovery_sheet.rs:290`); `/connect` "must NOT be auth-gated" (`app.rs:926`); the stated protection is only that "browsers do not send [fragments] to the server".
- Failure: the full link — fragment included — is pasted into a ticket, chat or screen share; the recipient imports the member's key wholesale. The fragment defends against server logs only.
- Confidence: medium — mechanism stated; leak vector inferred.
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — ADR-2010's consumer half is absent: no receipt proves a decision was applied
- Topics: NF-11.10, NF-06.11, NF-10.8
- Evidence: ledger row "proposed / partial / staged" (`docs/adr/ADR-2010-durable-governance-outcome-receipts.md:5-7`); "Relay projection receipt is not external application proof" (NF-06.11); the commit path is not yet witnessed live (`ADR-2010:157`, NF-11.10 N5).
- Failure: a relay OK plus projection-committed reads as success while the consumer never applied the decision; an operator acts for months on moderation outcomes that never took effect, with no stage in the data able to say so.

### F-13 — Search index split across R2 and KV with no joint-write guarantee
- Topics: NF-07.4
- Evidence: the deserializer parses ONLY the Vec segment (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/src/store.rs:185`); the id→label mapping, model and `publicLabels` live in SEARCH_CONFIG KV (`lib.rs:345`) — "the two stores must be written together" (NF-07.4 N1); no transaction spans R2 and KV.

- Confidence: medium — the split is stated; the partial-write scenario is inferred.

### F-14 — NIP-11 advertises NIP-29 while the two metadata builders are incompatible and relay-signing is a TODO
- Topics: NF-13.1, NF-11.14, NF-03.4, NF-10.3
- Evidence: `supported_nips` includes 29 (`nip11.rs:207`); the gate requires `h` + admin (`nip_handlers.rs:1299`, `:1303`, `:1307`) and relay-key metadata "remains TODO" (`nip_handlers.rs:1300-1301`); the legacy builder emits `h` (`groups.rs:137`, `:146`), the newer emits `d` and is rejected (`groups.rs:254`, `:263`; `nip_handlers.rs:1304`).
- Failure: a consumer implementing against the advertised NIP-29 with the newer builder is silently denied; meanwhile any admin client's signature is accepted for group metadata the protocol says the relay issues (O2, "Confirmed STILL LIVE").
- Confidence: high (incompatibility and TODO stated).
- Marked by authors: yes (ANOMALY O2 for the admin-client acceptance; the builder split is laid out in NF-13.1)

### F-15 — NIP-42 relay-tag check silently skipped while RELAY_URL is blank
- Topics: NF-03.2, NF-03.3, NF-08.8
- Evidence: unset/blank `own_relay_url` "SKIPS the relay-tag check only. This fails OPEN on the defence-in-depth check" (`nip42.rs:168-172`); `RELAY_URL` ships blank (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:51`) and the only signal is a console error per AUTH (`nip42.rs:230`, `nip_handlers.rs:2054`).

- Confidence: high that the skip occurs (stated); significance beyond defence-in-depth is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

## ontomark / critical

### F-01 — The legacy VisionClaw producer still ships the SEV-1 publication faults in production, and the ADR meant to repin it is inactive
- Topics: OM-00.3, OM-05.5, OM-06.5
- Evidence: OM-00.3 EXT: "VisionClaw's vault build job still ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged until VC-60 retires" (ADR-OM-005…md:11). S1.1 is the inherited fail-open redactor (`../project/crates/vault/src/projection.rs:230`); S1.2 is the body-only credential scan while render publishes frontmatter (`vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160`, `:196`). OM-05.5: that job runs `vault --repo vault-source build --vault knowledge` (`ontology-publish.yml:172-175`) and its output feeds `pack-pod-resources.py` (`ontology-publish.yml:247`). OM-06.5 STATUS: ADR-2136 is `proposed/partial/inactive` (`ADR-2136…md:5-7`) and its close-out found "not Decision 1's repin of the `vault` source" (`ADR-2136…md:97-102`).
- Failure: a regex compile error in the legacy redactor silently publishes unredacted private prose; a credential in a frontmatter value (title, sources) is published because only the body is scanned. Pod resources built from this output carry private identities and possibly secrets today, and the decision that would retire the faulty producer is not implemented.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (EXTERNAL in OM-00.3; STATUS note in OM-06.5)

### F-02 — Machine-scope and public bundles share `content_digest` and `ontology_digest`, so any digest-keyed cache conflates them and can serve working drafts to the public site
- Topics: OM-06.1, OM-06.5
- Evidence: OM-06.1 SAME: the two scopes' data/ artefacts are byte-identical "and ontology_digest and content_digest are equal" (test `machine_and_public_builds_share_every_ontology_artefact`, `ontomark-compile/tests/machine_scope.rs:143`). C3 requires that "a consumer cache keyed on ontology_digest must also key on scope" (`sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:148-152`). Loom's own marker reader ignores `scope` entirely (`loom-facade/src/mirror.rs:266-285`, `:394-437`).
- Failure: an estate runs `build --scope machine` for the pod bundle (AB-P6) and a public build beside it; a CDN or artifact cache keyed on `ontology_digest` returns the machine bundle — containing `api/pages/working/` and the merged search index — for the public site. Nothing in the bundle identity prevents it; only an undocumented consumer obligation does.
- Confidence: medium (the shared digest is verified; the leak requires a scope-blind consumer, inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (INVARIANT note plus the C3 caveat)

### F-03 — ADR-OM-009 says every published closure carries a derivation certificate; the gate is opt-in and default builds write and check nothing
- Topics: OM-03.8, OM-04.1
- Evidence: ADR-OM-009 is Accepted and "active from v0.6.0" deciding every published closure carries a certificate (`docs/adr/ADR-OM-009…md:3-5`, `:14` as cited in OM-04.1 NC). But `certificate_out` defaults absent: "certificate_out is None → nothing written, nothing checked" (`build/mod.rs:730`; `ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:401`). OM-04.1 NC DRIFT: "the C3 file listing names no certificate output (`sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:111`), while ADR-OM-009 decides every published closure carries one."
- Failure: any build without `--certificate-out` publishes a bundle with no warrant and no parity check; an emitter change that breaks closure parity ships undetected, and the estate's promise of verifier-checkable derivations is unmet for those bundles.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open in OM-03.8; DRIFT in OM-04.1)

### F-04 — `publish_out` is written after `promote`: a failure there leaves the new bundle live beside the old publication tree, with a marker listing files that were never written
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-03.1
- Evidence: OM-03.5 N2: "write_to runs AFTER promote, so a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree that swap_in kept, the two no longer one generation" (`build/publish.rs:356`; `build/mod.rs:1018-1020`). The publish files were already stamped into the marker as hashed external artefacts before `write_to` (`build/mod.rs:1009-1013`, `:361-363`). The module doc still says "promote atomically" (`build/mod.rs:12`).
- Failure: disk-full or permission error during `write_to` after a successful swap: the site serves the new bundle's data while `quartz/content` keeps the previous generation's pages; the promoted `.generation.json` lists publish artefacts whose sha256 matches no file on disk, so verification steps in the style of Loom's marker-vs-file check fail, and operators cannot tell which generation is live.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE N2 and DOC-DRIFT in OM-03.5)

### F-05 — `content_digest` omits the attribution roster (and the profile) while C3 claims it covers "every byte the build read"; on flat vaults the roster is read from the wrong root entirely
- Topics: OM-03.6, OM-03.2, OM-03.7
- Evidence: OM-03.6 N1: "the attribution roster shapes the Turtle and the page API but is not an input to content_digest" (`build/mod.rs:680-685` vs `:963-983`). C3 states the digest is over "every byte the build read" (`sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:159-163`, quoted in OM-03.7). N3: dirtiness reads `repo_root/ontology` (`build/mod.rs:933`) but the roster is read from `vault_root.parent()/ontology` (`build/attribution.rs:32`); OM-03.2 Debt: on a flat layout the vault root is `repo_root` itself (`profile.rs:514-518`), so the roster is looked up one directory above the repository, and an absent file is an empty roster (`attribution.rs:29-37`). The stated digest input list — pages, journals, vocabulary (`build/mod.rs:963-983`) — names no profile.
- Failure: an attribution or profile change produces different published bytes with an identical `content_digest`; consumers keying on the digest, as C3 instructs, serve stale attribution indefinitely. On a plain flat vault, attribution silently vanishes from Turtle and the page API with no error and no digest movement.
- Confidence: high (roster exclusion and wrong-root read stated; profile exclusion inferred from the stated input list)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE N1/N3, Debt) — the direct contradiction with C3's "every byte" wording is not itself marked

### F-06 — Governance apply is not atomic across file, git and ledger: no writer lock at compare/rename, pushes include commits made after the count, and the ledger can reject after the write persisted
- Topics: OM-07.3, OM-07.1
- Evidence: RACE (Open): "compare and rename are separate; no writer lock at this boundary" (`write/apply.rs:401`, `:411`). OTHER (Tension): the writer can add a commit after the ahead-count check and the push then includes it (`ontology-apply.js:872`, `:879`). LEDGER: attestation failure is returned after the write (`ontology-apply.js:742`, `:900`); ADR-2109 deliberately keeps ledger failure from undoing a completed write.
- Failure: two concurrent applies (or an editor saving during apply) interleave the re-read/rename window and one change is silently lost; an unrelated local commit made between count and push is published to the shared branch under a governance decision it was not part of; the ledger records a failure for a change already live, so the forum's record of what changed diverges from the corpus.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, Tension)

### F-07 — Explicit promote lacks the proposal-digest equality check that approve performs before reaching the same amendment writer
- Topics: OM-07.1
- Evidence: X (Tension): approve resolves the proposal digest and requires it to equal the decision tag (`local-process-manager.js:379`, `:397`, `:400`); explicit promote checks only the request tag and signed IRI (`local-process-manager.js:444`, `:450`) before dispatch to the same writers (`ontology-apply.js:372`, `:692`). The material states the probe "reproduces the discrepancy below signature admission".
- Failure: a promote request whose tag matches a decision but whose proposal payload is another case's applies that case's amendment; nothing on the promote path compares the applied proposal's digest to the decision, so the governance record and the applied change can be different objects.
- Confidence: medium (the missing check is stated; reachability past signature admission is not established by the probes)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION)

### F-08 — `--vault all` silently publishes knowledge-only when the working directory is absent; the only guard is one consumer's bespoke CI check
- Topics: OM-03.2, OM-05.5
- Evidence: `sibling_vault(Working)` is loaded "only if that directory exists" (`build/publish.rs:365-376`; `build/mod.rs:604-605`). OM-05.5 n3: "visionGraph treats the staging tree as wrong unless both pages/ and working/ exist, because ontomark silently accepts a knowledge-only build" (`publish.yml:189-195`).
- Failure: `working/` is renamed or mis-spelled after a refactor; every subsequent `--vault all` build succeeds and publishes the site with no working pages — no warning, no non-zero exit, no census anomaly. Any consumer without visionGraph's hand-written existence check ships a silently incomplete corpus.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the behavior is stated inside an INVARIANT note explaining the consumer's compensating check, not flagged as a defect

### F-09 — The applied-change receipt names the proposal page while creation writes a file named from the staged title
- Topics: OM-07.2
- Evidence: GAP (Drift): "staged title B can write B.md while adapter receipt names proposal page A" (`ontology-apply.js:570` vs `write/create.rs:258`); the CLI outcome names the actual title and path (`write/create.rs:304`) but the adapter receipt uses the original proposal page (`ontology-apply.js:634`); the optional `page_sha256` does not substitute for a semantic digest check.
- Failure: whenever the staged title differs from the proposal page slug, the forum/ledger records point at page A while the corpus gained page B; reviewers auditing "which approved change reached which page" get the wrong page, and a revert driven by the receipt targets the wrong file.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT)

### F-10 — Three wikilink resolvers disagree on fragments, `.md` suffixes and case, emitting relations to non-existent IRIs
- Topics: OM-02.1, OM-01.5, OM-00.1
- Evidence: OM-02.1 n2: `PrivateLinks` strips a `#fragment` and `.md` before lookup (`build/redact.rs:219`, `:227`) but `Corpus::build` passes the target whole to `names.get` (`ir/model.rs:338`), so "a fragment link redacted as private is resolved here to namespace + slugify of the whole string"; the shacl resolver has no slug key and matches ids case-sensitively (`shacl.rs:1201-1207`). OM-01.5 note3: curated links use `target_slug` verbatim (`ir/model.rs:424`) while the resolve fallback uses `slugify` (`ir/model.rs:342`), diverging on kebab input with leading/trailing/doubled dashes (`input/slug.rs:65-75`).
- Failure: a public page containing `[[Some Page#Section]]` publishes a relation to an IRI whose local name is `some-page-section` — a class that does not exist — surfacing in Turtle as a dangling reference and being silently dropped by the tiers (undeclared targets, `graph_tiers.rs:297-298`), while the SHACL export resolves the same link differently.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-11 — Turtle's stub list is hard-coded to 13 property names, so vocabulary-added relations get declared but their external targets get no stub — and existentials over them are silently dropped
- Topics: OM-04.2, OM-00.1
- Evidence: the stub list is a hardcoded array of 13 names (`turtle.rs:446-460`) beside the vocabulary-driven `declare_object_properties` (`turtle.rs:678-691`); "a relation the vocabulary adds is declared but never stubbed" (OM-04.2 DIVERGENCE; OM-00.1 R5). Existential restrictions are emitted "only both endpoints declared" (`turtle.rs:358-385`).
- Failure: the estate adds a relation to `vocabulary.yaml` and pages use it against pages without ontology declarations: the property is declared but the targets never become `skos:Concept` stubs, restrictions naming them are silently omitted from `ontology.ttl`, and the tiers drop those edges outright (`graph_tiers.rs:297-298`, `:309-310`) — the published ontology quietly loses axioms with no validation error.
- Confidence: medium (the stub divergence is stated; the restriction-loss interaction is inferred from the stated both-endpoints rule)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE) — the restriction-loss consequence is not marked

### F-12 — NGG1 collapses all twelve relations into one edge type, the dedup key merges distinct relations between the same pair, and edges to unselected targets vanish
- Topics: OM-04.4, OM-06.5, OM-00.5
- Evidence: every relation key becomes `EDGE_RELATION` (`graph_tiers.rs:307-316`); the edge set keyed `(src, tgt, type)` merges two different relations between one pair (`graph_tiers.rs:283`, `:313`); `EDGE_RELATION` is a published wire byte (`ontomark-ngg1/src/lib.rs:144`); edges to targets outside the selection are silently dropped (`graph_tiers.rs:297-298`, `:309-310`) with `stats.json` reporting only declared-vs-shipped. OM-06.5 N1/N2; OM-00.5 OPEN1.
- Failure: the explorer tier renders `part-of` and `related-to` between the same two concepts as one indistinguishable edge and drops cross-domain edges entirely; consumers reasoning over tiers get a strictly poorer graph than `ontology.ttl` with no error, and remediation requires an NGG2 format bump left undecided in ADR-OM-005.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (KEEP/Open in OM-00.5 and OM-06.5; DIVERGENCE in OM-04.4)

### F-13 — The hold-back rule exists in two shapes: census and identity derivation use top-level-only `_misc`, staging holds `_misc`/`misc` at any depth, so published counts and redaction inputs disagree
- Topics: OM-03.3, OM-02.5, OM-00.1
- Evidence: `is_unpublished` covers `_misc` at top level only (`input/page.rs:47`, `:641-646`); `held_back` applies `UNPUBLISHED_DIRS` at any depth plus `HELD_BACK_DIRS` `misc` (`build/publish.rs:131-148`, ER-18/95208e8); "the census and excluded_identities_of still use the top-level-only is_unpublished… which is the divergence that remains" (OM-03.3, `ir/projection.rs:114`, `:231`); OM-00.1 Open asks whether `misc` belongs in `HELD_BACK_DIRS`; the compat path never applies `held_back` at all (`build/mod.rs:671-675`, S3.4).
- Failure: a `public: true` page under `notes/misc/` is held out of `publish/` and forced private in the link path, but the census counts it as a considered public page and `excluded_identities_of` omits its identity — `api/census.json` over-counts published pages, so reconciliation tooling either flags false alarms or trusts a wrong count; under `--no-redact-private-links` the same page appears in the ontology artefacts but not in `publish/`.
- Confidence: medium (the two rules and their consumers are stated; the exact leak surface of the missing identity is partly inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in OM-03.3; Open in OM-00.1)

### F-14 — Ingest request IDs exclude the model endpoint and budgets while a rerun deletes the existing bundle directory first: an incomplete uncached rerun destroys a complete review bundle, and removed-source accounting is lost by the host
- Topics: OM-08.3
- Evidence: the request ID is computed from sources, target digests, model and options (`ingest_cmd.rs:606`) while the endpoint is in the parser and response cache (`ingest_cmd.rs:570`, `:446`) — Drift; "Remove existing manifest directory" before rewrite (`ingest_cmd.rs:771-772`); Tension: "incomplete uncached rerun can replace complete bundle under same ID" (`ingest_cmd.rs:723`, `:769`, `:772`). The library classifies removed IDs (`ledger.rs:224`, `:255`) but the host result records only finished observed sources (`ingest_cmd.rs:841`, `:843`).
- Failure: a reviewer's complete bundle is silently replaced by a partial one after an endpoint outage mid-rerun under the same ID — already-reviewed evidence and proposals are gone; separately, sources removed from the ledger never appear in any host summary, so reviewers cannot see that evidence was withdrawn.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, Tension) — the host dropping the library's removal classification is stated but unmarked

### F-15 — Ingest acceptance grounds relations on quote "mention" only, and a malformed model response drops its chunk without making the run incomplete
- Topics: OM-08.2
- Evidence: "Quote must mention the value" (`accept.rs:533`) with Tension: "mention check does not prove relation meaning — a negated relation can pass"; the audit "reproduced negative sentence still grounds an accepted positive relation". A JSON parse failure records a dropped chunk only (`ingest_cmd.rs:735`, `:396`, `:728`), no skipped pair or stop is added, "complete can remain true" (`ingest_cmd.rs:821`) and the source is recorded finished (`ingest_cmd.rs:842`).
- Failure: the model returns "X is not part of Y"; the acceptor proposes `part-of(X, Y)` with a verbatim quote that passes the mention check and reaches review flagged as grounded. On a flaky endpoint, whole chunks of evidence vanish from the proposal set while the run reports complete, so reviewers believe coverage was total.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual redaction coverage.** The mechanism (marker alternation, private-identity sets) is described, but there is no independent leak audit of published bytes — whether aliases, display variants, or body prose naming private pages can survive the marker set, and what `REDACTOR_SIZE_LIMIT` is versus current corpus headroom (growth past it halts all publication by design).
2. **Cross-process concurrency on `out/` and `publish_out`.** Whether two simultaneous builds, or a build during a Loom promote, are serialised; `swap_in`'s clearing of a "stale retired directory" (`build/mod.rs:306-312`) looks racy against another process mid-swap, but no locking is stated.
3. **Live VisionClaw exposure.** What the pod-resources job has actually shipped to date — whether private prose or credentials from S1.1/S1.2 are already in Pod resources — and the concrete VC-60 retirement gates and timeline.
4. **Governance relay integrity.** Signature admission, replay handling, and whether untrusted input can reach the promote path; both audits explicitly state these are not certified by the local probes.
5. **Consumer inventory for digest-keyed behaviour.** Which caches or pipelines besides Loom key on `ontology_digest`/`content_digest` (to size F-02), and whether anything reconciles `api/census.json` against `publish/` (to size F-13).

## ontomark / premortem

# Post-mortem — published-content exposure with mismatched provenance, twelve months on

**Incident summary.** Confidential client-working content appeared in a published artefact, and the records describing what was published did not match the artefact actually served. The client terminated. The distillation below predates the incident; every candidate cause and latent defect was already visible in it at the stated pins (ontomark 07f4c1c / 560e50c / 0caae5d, agentbox 9dfc27b / 6500582, loom 5ad8608, visionflow 4d361b5). All findings are checkable against the cited paths.

---

### R-1 — The still-running legacy `vault` producer publishes with a fail-open redactor and a body-only credential scan
- Topics: OM-00.3, OM-05.5, OM-00.2
- Chain of events:
  1. The VisionClaw publish job keeps running: it "retires this job, gated, so it keeps running" (ADR-OM-005-estate-alignment-one-producer-explicit-projections.md:11).
  2. It builds the in-tree legacy crate, not ontomark: `cargo build --release --locked -p vault` (ontology-publish.yml:167-168), and runs `vault --repo vault-source build --vault knowledge --out output/vault` with no publish-out and no mirror (ontology-publish.yml:172-175).
  3. That binary still ships S1.1 and S1.2 "unchanged until VC-60 retires" (OM-00.3, EXT node, ADR-OM-005:11): the prose redactor that "failed OPEN on a regex compile error" (OM-00.2 A1; inherited at vault/src/projection.rs:230) and the credential scan that is "body-only but render publishes frontmatter" (OM-00.2 A2; inherited at vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160, 196).
  4. Twelve months of corpus growth enlarges the private marker set; in ontomark an alternation past the size limit now refuses the build (ir/projection.rs:285, 313-315), but the legacy path has the pre-fix behaviour — any redactor construction failure publishes unredacted (inferred trigger; the shipped defect is stated).
  5. Independently, any credential placed in a frontmatter value (a token-bearing `sources` URL, a title) is never scanned there, because the scan reads the body while render re-emits the frontmatter (OM-00.2 A2; OM-00.3 S12).
  6. The whole `output/vault` bundle feeds `pack-pod-resources.py` directly (ontology-publish.yml:247), so unredacted private prose and unscanned frontmatter reach the client-facing Pod with no intervening gate. The shipped gate/conflicts first-vault-only defect (S1.5, inherited at vault/src/main.rs:595-598) further under-reports on multi-vault runs.
- Evidence: OM-00.3 EXT ("ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged"); OM-00.2 A1/A2 and N2 (fail-closed fix exists only in ontomark, ir/projection.rs:313-315); OM-05.5 VC workflow nodes and n2 (the workflow "never invokes ontomark"); OM-00.3 S11/S12/S15 inheritance lines.
- Warning signs visible today: the register itself ranks these SEV-1 and records that the second producer keeps running under four gates; the omnibus fix commit 8d654d1 changed only ontomark; the ADR-2136 close-out found the running image had not repinned the `vault` source (ADR-2136:97-102).
- Confidence: high that the defects ship on a live production path (stated); the size-limit trigger crossing in year twelve is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Public and machine bundles share one identity, and the downstream readers ignore the only distinguishing field
- Topics: OM-06.1, OM-06.5, OM-03.6, OM-05.5
- Chain of events:
  1. Two consumers build from the same repository: visionGraph publishes the public bundle (publish.yml:165-171) and agentbox builds `build --scope machine` (ADR-2136:84; DEPRECATION.md:468).
  2. Across the two scopes, `data/` (bar markers) and `okf/` are byte-identical and `ontology_digest` and `content_digest` are equal — pinned by test (machine_scope.rs:143).
  3. The generation id is `prefix@sha` of the commit plus `+dirty` (build/generation.rs:150-169), so two clean builds of different scopes at one commit carry the same id (equality of digests is asserted; equality of the id follows from the stated derivation — inferred).
  4. The public marker omits `scope` so its bytes do not change (build/mod.rs:1002; build/generation.rs:118-121); what differs is the markers, `api/pages/working/`, the search index and `page_count` (machine_scope.rs:244).
  5. The Loom marker readers read only named fields and "scope is never read, so it is ignored" (loom-facade/src/mirror.rs:266-285); the strict parser does not refuse unknown keys (mirror.rs:394-437); promotion rewrites the marker as a Value, preserving scope without interpreting it (loom-vector-ruvector/src/artifact.rs:168-175, 255-260).
  6. C3 already warns that "a consumer cache keyed on ontology_digest must also key on scope" (sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:148-152). A store or mirror keyed on id/digest that ingests both bundles therefore conflates them and serves machine pages — including working records under `api/pages/working/` — where public content was expected, or the reverse (the mis-serving itself is inferred; the identity collision and field-ignoring readers are stated).
- Evidence: machine_scope.rs:143 and :244; build/generation.rs:118-121, 150-169; mirror.rs:266-285, 394-437; artifact.rs:168-175, 255-260; sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:148-152; publish.yml:165-171; ADR-2136:84.
- Warning signs visible today: the SAME invariant test asserting digest equality across scopes; the C3 consumer warning; the Loom verification that scope is never read; the AB-P6 doc drift naming a `--private-machine-bundle` flag that does not exist (DEPRECATION.md:470).
- Confidence: medium — the collision and tolerant readers are stated; the conflating consumer and the mis-serving are inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — A governance decision was applied to a page other than the one its receipt names, and the ledger attested after the write
- Topics: OM-07.1, OM-07.2, OM-07.3
- Chain of events:
  1. The explicit `promote` path checks the request tag (local-process-manager.js:444) but "lacks approve's proposal-digest equality check" (local-process-manager.js:400) — a proposal whose digest differs from the decision tag can be dispatched (OM-07.1, TENSION).
  2. Creation takes the staged file's title as the target filename (write/create.rs:248, 258) and writes exclusively (write/create.rs:322, 333), while the adapter receipt uses the original proposal page (ontology-apply.js:634) — "staged title B can write B.md while adapter receipt names proposal page A" (ontology-apply.js:570 vs write/create.rs:258), reproduced in the authors' A/B fixture.
  3. The amendment is committed and pushed (ontology-apply.js:828, 879), and only then is the caller's case and digest attested — "failure is returned after write" (ontology-apply.js:742, 900), so an attestation failure leaves the change persisted and unattested.
  4. The client's audit finds published content on a page no approved case names, and a ledger that either names a different page or names nothing.
- Evidence: local-process-manager.js:400, 444; ontology-apply.js:570, 634, 742, 900; write/create.rs:258; the OM-07 audit note that the fixture "shows that the identity fields can disagree at their boundary" and that probes ran "below signature admission".
- Warning signs visible today: the OM-07.1 TENSION node, the OM-07.2 GAP/DRIFT node with the A/B fixture, the OM-07.3 RACE and post-count-push tensions (write/apply.rs:401, 411; ontology-apply.js:872, 879).
- Confidence: high that the check asymmetry and title-vs-receipt drift exist as described (stated and fixture-reproduced); the end-to-end wrong-page publication in production is inferred from those stated gaps.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — `write_to` runs after `promote`, splitting the bundle and the publication tree into two generations
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-00.4
- Evidence: "write_to runs AFTER promote, so a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree that swap_in kept, the two no longer one generation" (build/publish.rs:356; build/mod.rs:1018-1020); promote order at build/mod.rs:304, 411.
- Failure: a remediation rebuild (e.g., withdrawing a leaked credential) promotes the corrected bundle, then the publish-out write fails; the public site keeps serving the old tree while the marker claims the new generation, masking an unresolved exposure.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-02 — An ingest rerun deletes and replaces a complete review bundle under the same request ID
- Topics: OM-08.3
- Evidence: "incomplete uncached rerun can replace complete bundle under same ID" (ingest_cmd.rs:723, 769, 772); "Remove existing manifest directory" before rewriting (ingest_cmd.rs:771, 772); source snapshots, prior target bytes and model requests are not retained (ingest_cmd.rs:802-838, Open).
- Failure: a cache miss or partial model run destroys a reviewer's complete evidence bundle; with no snapshots retained, it cannot be reconstructed.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION/DRIFT/Open)

### F-03 — Governed amendment has no writer lock between compare and rename
- Topics: OM-07.3
- Evidence: "compare and rename are separate; no writer lock at this boundary" (write/apply.rs:401, 411), with the synced temp file and re-read at write/apply.rs:395.
- Failure: two concurrent applies to one page both pass the context compare; the second rename overwrites the first; an approved amendment is silently lost while its proposal digest and receipt exist.
- Confidence: high that no lock exists (stated); the lost update is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-04 — The governance push can include commits made after the ahead-count check
- Topics: OM-07.3
- Evidence: "writer can add a commit after count; push then includes it" (ontology-apply.js:872, 879); commit-one-path and ahead-count at ontology-apply.js:828, 872.
- Failure: an unreviewed commit riding the governed push reaches the published branch.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION)

### F-05 — `sibling_vault` never compares kinds, so one vault can be staged twice under both trees
- Topics: OM-03.2
- Evidence: "sibling_vault never compares its kind with vault.kind, so a library caller with vault_root = working plus with_working_publish stages the same vault twice" (build/publish.rs:365-376); staging paths keyed `pages` or `working` (build/publish.rs:253-260).
- Failure: a library-driven build roots at `working/` with working-publish on: working content is staged under `publish/pages/` as though it were knowledge and mislabelled to consumers.
- Confidence: high (stated); a production library caller is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-06 — The hold-back rule exists in two shapes, so nested `_misc` pages are silently withheld while counted as considered
- Topics: OM-03.3, OM-00.1, OM-01.2, OM-02.5
- Evidence: `is_unpublished` covers `_misc` at top level only (input/page.rs:47, 641); `held_back` applies `UNPUBLISHED_DIRS` at any depth plus site-only `misc` (build/publish.rs:134, 138-148); "the census and excluded_identities_of still use the top-level-only is_unpublished" (ir/projection.rs:114, 231); the lists were made disjoint at 95208e8 but the split remains; the path-walking `excluded_identities` twin survives uncalled (ir/projection.rs:202).
- Failure: a public-flagged page nested deeper under `_misc` is forced private in the projection and skipped by staging, while the census counts it as considered — the author believes it published; the counts and the published tree disagree.
- Confidence: high (stated divergence).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE and Open)

### F-07 — On the compat path a public `misc/` page reaches `api/` and the search index but not `publish/`
- Topics: OM-02.3, OM-00.1
- Evidence: "the compat path never forces misc/ pages private … a public misc/ page stays public there, while stage_redacted skips it" (ir/projection.rs:616; build/publish.rs:220); OM-00.1 H4 records the divergence as kept for byte-parity.
- Failure: with redaction off (`--no-redact-private-links` overlays the profile, ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:815), site-only content appears in the page API and search index that the publish tree withholds — surfaces disagree on what is published.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE kept)

### F-08 — Three wikilink resolvers disagree on fragments, `.md` suffixes and case, producing different targets per surface
- Topics: OM-02.1, OM-00.1, OM-01.5
- Evidence: three resolvers (ir/model.rs:336; build/redact.rs:210; shacl.rs:1201-1207 — no slug key, case-sensitive id, lower-cased title/alias); `PrivateLinks` strips `#fragment` and `.md` (build/redact.rs:219, 227) while `Corpus::build` passes the whole string, so "a fragment link redacted as private is resolved here to namespace + slugify of the whole string" (ir/model.rs:338); curated links take `target_slug` verbatim (input/slug.rs:65-75) while the resolve fallback slugifies (ir/model.rs:342).
- Failure: the same link is private-redacted on one surface and becomes a dangling or wrong IRI on another; the SHACL surface can bind by case-insensitive title to a page the publisher considers unresolvable — artefacts disagree about what exists and what is private.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-09 — Attribution shapes the outputs but not the digest, and the roster is read from a second root
- Topics: OM-03.6, OM-03.2
- Evidence: "the attribution roster shapes the Turtle and the page API but is not an input to content_digest" (build/mod.rs:680-685 vs 963-983); "the vocabulary directory is hard-coded to repo_root/ontology for dirtiness, and the roster is read from vault_root.parent()/ontology, two roots for one directory" (build/mod.rs:933; build/attribution.rs:32); on a flat vault the roster is looked up above the repository (profile.rs:514-518, 549-550).
- Failure: an attribution correction is republished with an unchanged `content_digest`, so digest-keyed consumers keep serving the old authorship; on flat vaults the build reads roster data from outside the repository altogether.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE/Debt)

### F-10 — Census and load read each page twice, a time-of-check gap

- Evidence: "each page is still read twice, census then load, so a file edited between the two is classified on one text and parsed from another, a time-of-check gap the digest no longer widens" (ir/projection.rs:118; input/page.rs:493).
- Failure: a file edited between the two reads is validated as one version and published as another — e.g., a `public` flag changed from boolean true to a string after the census is silently parsed as private, withholding a page the census cleared.
- Confidence: high that the gap exists (stated); the silent-withholding direction is inferred from the stated Bool-only gate (input/page.rs:268-273).

- Evidence: every relation key becomes `EDGE_RELATION` (build/graph_tiers.rs:307-316); the dedup key `src, tgt, type` merges two relations between one pair (:283, 313); "Edge to a target that is not a selected node is silently dropped" (:297-298, 309-310); `EDGE_RELATION` is a published wire byte (ontomark-ngg1/src/lib.rs:144); kept at v1 pending an NGG2 bump (OM-00.2 C1, OM-06.5).

### F-12 — The embed endpoint is in the cache and parser identity but not the request ID
- Topics: OM-08.3
- Evidence: "Endpoint included in parser and response cache" (ingest_cmd.rs:570, 446) versus "endpoint omitted from request ID" (ingest_cmd.rs:606); the ID determines the default directory (ingest_cmd.rs:769).
- Failure: a rerun against a different endpoint lands in the same bundle directory; on a cache hit the first endpoint's responses are reused while the run records the new endpoint — the provenance of the evidence is misstated, compounding F-02's replacement.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — The quote-mention check accepts a negated relation
- Topics: OM-08.2
- Evidence: "mention check does not prove relation meaning; a negated relation can pass" (accept.rs:533), beside the located-quote and mention-value checks (accept.rs:415-533); the conflict-review promise is "narrower in the current relation-only implementation".
- Failure: a source stating "X is not part-of Y" grounds a proposed `part-of` assertion with a verbatim quote; a reviewer approving on quote-presence ships a false relation into the corpus.

- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Host persistence and the engine index update are not one transaction
- Topics: OM-06.4, OM-06.5
- Evidence: the OM-06 open note: "the Obsidian host compares the complete base text, rather than rehashing the file, and a later index failure can reject after persistence" (engine upsert rebuild at ontomark-wasm/src/engine.rs:304-314).
- Failure: a note is persisted but the index update fails; the engine serves a stale view until a later upsert, and the acceptance path can reject a write that already happened.

- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-15 — The running image never repinned the `vault` source beside the released ontomark
- Topics: OM-06.5, OM-05.5
- Evidence: the ADR-2136 close-out "found the released `ontomark` 0.6.0 baked into the running image beside `vault`, but not Decision 1's repin of the `vault` source" (ADR-2136:97-102).
- Failure: whatever `vault` source the image carries keeps building the client-facing Pod bundle outside the reviewed line of descent; the S1.1/S1.2 class of defects (R-1) stays live in that image unreviewed and unfixable by repo commits.
- Confidence: medium (the fact is stated; the consequence compounds R-1 and is inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (recorded as a status fact, not flagged as a defect)

## visionclaw#2 / critical

### F-01 — Shipped Compose grants anonymous users the Editor role
- Topics: VC-22.9
- Evidence: `role_store.rs:359` resolves an unassigned pubkey to `default_role` (Editor unless `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE=viewer`). `rbac_gate.rs:135-141` implements `public_reads_enabled()` as `.unwrap_or(false)` (fail-closed in code). The shipped `docker-compose.unified.yml:132,133` sets both `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS=1` (with the `:-1` default), inverting both fail-closed guards simultaneously.
- Failure: any deployment using the shipped compose file gives every anonymous, unauthenticated user the Editor role on all non-public routes. An anonymous client can mutate governed data, change settings, and write to stores.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the note says "CORRECTED ADR-2070" for the code side, but the shipped compose inversion is described as remaining ("The open posture is ADR-2027's deliberate demo default and stays"). The authors treat it as a deliberate demo default, not as an open incident.

---

### F-02 — Deleting agent memory from the Pod leaves a live, searchable embedding in RuVector
- Topics: VC-22.6
- Evidence: `client/src/services/solidPod/agentMemory.ts:191-198` deletes the `.jsonld` resource from the Solid Pod. No code path calls `mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete` or any RuVector endpoint. `docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:88` records the gap. The material adds that the server now holds a direct read-only RuVector client (`Cargo.toml:143-145`), so the orphaned embedding has a second consumer.
- Failure: a user exercises their erasure right via `deleteAgentMemory`; the Pod resource is gone but the vector embedding persists in Postgres and remains searchable. GDPR/right-to-erasure violation, and the deletion is reported as successful.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

---

### F-03 — Oxigraph has no point-in-time backup; GRAPH_PROVENANCE is restore-only and cannot be re-derived
- Topics: VC-22.7, VC-22.11, VC-22.1
- Evidence: `scripts/backup-sqlite.sh:75` lists `REQUIRED_DBS` as settings/enrichment/kpi SQLite files only. The DriftNote in VC-22.11 states "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup - that bullet stands." `oxigraph_ontology_repository.rs:57` holds GRAPH_PROVENANCE, append-only, not re-derivable from any upstream source.
- Failure: an Oxigraph data-directory corruption or host loss destroys the provenance graph and the knowledge graph with no recovery path. Provenance history is permanently lost because it cannot be regenerated from the corpus (only the assert graph can).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (PROPOSED ADR-2103, unresolved)

### F-04 — Full-sync rebuild wipes governed runtime ontology writes with no warning or preservation
- Topics: VC-22.10
- Evidence: `github_sync_service.rs:1026` does `CLEAR GRAPH assert then INSERT DATA` via `save_ontology_graph`. The note states "this CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (github_sync_service.rs:952-954)." A class added via `add_owl_class/add_axiom` between full-syncs is not preserved.
- Failure: an operator runs `FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1` to fix a drift issue. Every governed ontology addition made since the last rebuild through the write door is silently destroyed. If no one notices immediately, the loss is permanent because those writes existed only in Oxigraph.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated plainly in the diagram but not flagged as a Debt/Drift/Tension marker)

### F-05 — Position stream chunk acknowledged without being applied due to the 16ms client throttle
- Topics: VC-32.8, VC-30.10
- Evidence: `wsClient.ts:26-27` returns without applying a frame when `< 16ms` have passed since the last one. `binaryProtocol.ts:393,407` then sends `sendPositionAck` because `updateNodePositions` resolved (not rejected). ADR-2141 decision 5 states a chunk is acknowledged only after it has been applied. The TENSION marker calls this out.
- Failure: during rapid updates, a chunk of up to `streamChunkNodes` (default 2500) node positions is silently dropped client-side. The server records it as acknowledged and will not resend. Nodes in that chunk freeze at stale positions indefinitely because the acknowledged-position baseline now includes data the client never received.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION)

### F-06 — `public: false` in frontmatter is overridden by a valid `owl-class` marker; the inclusion gate is an OR
- Topics: VC-21.2, VC-21.12
- Evidence: `vault/mod.rs:162-163` implements `is_kg_included` as `self.public || self.owl_class.is_some()`. `docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md:236-238` says there should be "no owl-class bypass and no OR." VC-21.12 confirms: "an explicit public false plus a class marker remains included by policy - is_kg_included is an OR, so a valid owl-class overrides public false."
- Failure: an author marks a page `public: false` to keep it private, but the page has an `owl-class` frontmatter key (perhaps from a template or prior edit). The page is ingested into the knowledge graph and exposed through every public read API. The author's privacy intent is silently violated.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in VC-21.12; Drift in VC-21.2 for the doc side)

### F-07 — STT audio is sent to api.openai.com on demand, a third-party speech edge with no consent gate
- Topics: VC-28.7
- Evidence: `speech_service.rs:689-699,722` shows `SetSTTProvider(OpenAI)` is live and `ProcessAudioChunk` then POSTs audio to `https://api.openai.com/v1/audio/transcriptions` when `settings.openai` holds an API key. The default is Whisper (LAN-local), but the OpenAI variant is callable at runtime with no per-user consent check described.
- Failure: an operator or user switches the STT provider to OpenAI; all subsequent voice audio is transmitted to a third-party API. In a system that handles governance decisions and agent coordination, this is unlogged personal-data egress.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT)

### F-08 — Whelk EL++ reasoning has no timeout or axiom-count cap; post-sync reasoning can hang the sync pipeline
- Topics: VC-20.3
- Evidence: `whelk_inference_engine.rs:5` module doc claims "complete EL reasoning," but `infer()` at `whelk_inference_engine.rs:412-467` has no `tokio timeout` or axiom-count cap. The DOC-DRIFT marker records this. `run_post_sync_reasoning` at `github_sync_service.rs:1205` calls this synchronously in the sync path.
- Failure: a large or cyclic ontology causes the reasoner to run for minutes or hours. The post-sync pipeline blocks; deferred edges are never materialised; the boot-sync task stalls. Because the checksum cache only helps when the ontology is unchanged, every sync with a changed ontology re-enters the unbounded reasoning.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)

The SECURITY note says "Attributed means a well-formed pubkey was asserted, NOT that a signature was verified."
- Failure: a compromised or malicious agentbox container publishes events claiming any pubkey. Those events are persisted to `kpi_agent_events` with a false attribution, contaminating Trust Variance and HITL Precision KPIs, and recorded in the provenance trace as attributed to a user who never acted.

The Open marker states: "a store path that drops vc:owlProperty would silently stop ranking. The ADR names this as its review trigger; nothing at boot detects it."
- Failure: a repository migration, SPARQL update, or serialization round-trip drops or renames the `vc:owlProperty` predicate on subclass edges. The DAG ranker computes zero hierarchy pairs silently. Every radial layout with `DagRank` mode renders flat. No error is logged, no health check fires.

Every visitor gets the full application with no authentication prompt. The note states: "three separate relay connections under three different panel identities can exist concurrently, all using the same bridge secret."

More critically, events from the three identities are indistinguishable in forum provenance — there is no way to attribute a 31402 or 31404 to the specific subsystem that emitted it.

PROPOSED ADR-2104 says "execute the SOPS rollout or formally withdraw ADR-109 - plaintext .env is documented as the interim state."
- Failure: all secrets (GitHub PAT, Nostr private key, agent key, API keys) are readable by anyone with filesystem access to the deployment host. A container escape or host compromise exposes every credential simultaneously with no rotation mechanism.
