## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual production deployment configuration** — I cannot tell whether the shipped `docker-compose.unified.yml` is what runs in production or whether a hardened overlay exists. F-01 and F-15 depend entirely on this.

2. **Volume persistence and disk sizing for Oxigraph** — the material shows no backup, but I cannot tell whether the Oxigraph data directory is on persistent storage, what its size is, or whether a WAL/corruption risk exists.

3. **The forum relay's actual kind-filtering and rate-limit behaviour** — VC-24.9 says enforcement is "relay-side, not this repo," so I cannot judge whether the three concurrent ACSP identities cause real failures or are benign.

4. **Whether `public: false` + `owl-class` occurs in real vault pages** — F-06's severity depends on whether any production content actually carries both keys. A corpus audit would settle it.

5. **The 16ms throttle's real-world trigger rate** — F-05 is architecturally clear, but I cannot judge how often the client actually receives two frames within 16ms in production network conditions versus only under artificial load.

## visionclaw#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem — data, custody and security failure analysis

Reconstructed twelve months on from the estate distillation stamped `verified_commit: 8692b12ba987ecb87165566beb7c320a2446e4b4` (agentbox `6500582d`). The incident class is a client-costing data/custody/security failure. Everything below is drawn only from what the diagrams state or directly imply; where a chain requires reasoning beyond the material, it is marked inferred.

---

### R-1 — Deleted agent memories persist and remain searchable in RuVector (erasure failure)
- Topics: VC-22.6, VC-22.1, VC-26.6, VC-26.5
- Chain of events:
  1. Agent memory is written to the Pod (`storeAgentMemory`, `agentMemory.ts:86`) and, per the estate's vector-memory row, embedding/memory state lives in RuVector (external Postgres via MCP — VC-22.1 "Vector / agent memory → RuVector").
  2. A deletion is requested through `SolidPodService.deleteAgentMemory` (`SolidPodService.ts:366`).
  3. The Pod-side resource is deleted via LDP `DELETE`; a 404 is treated as success ("already-absent is not an error", `ldpClient.ts:210,215`), so the client reports `deleted-or-absent = true`.
  4. No reverse tombstone reaches RuVector: "this Pod-side delete never calls mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete or any RuVector endpoint — RuVector is external Postgres reached only via MCP, no delete-propagation hook exists in this path" (VC-22.6).
  5. The embedding row "persists and stays searchable" (VC-22.6, citing `docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:88`).
  6. The drift note makes exposure worse, not better: the server now holds a direct read-only RuVector client (tokio-postgres from `RUVECTOR_PG_CONNINFO`, crate `visionclaw-memory-cloud`, `Cargo.toml:143-145`), so the orphaned row has a second consumer on the read side.
  7. A subject-deletion request is therefore satisfied on the Pod surface while the content remains retrievable; the client or their auditor finds deleted material in search.
- Evidence: VC-22.6 DIVERGENCE rect ("no reverse tombstone to RuVector — embedding row persists and stays searchable, `docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:88`"); the same diagram's DRIFT note on the direct read client; VC-26.6 delete flow (`agentMemory.ts:191-198`); VC-26.5's 404-as-success invariant. The referenced agentbox ADR-2060 "RuVector-side half" is outside this material's evidence — nothing here shows it landed.
- Warning signs visible today: the DIVERGENCE rect is already drawn in VC-22.6; the governing doc records the gap by name ("deleteAgentMemory tombstone gap"); the read-side second consumer was added after the gap was documented.
- Confidence: high — the gap is explicitly stated; only the timeline of discovery is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The shipped compose resolves every unassigned nostr pubkey to Editor with public reads (authorization failure)
- Topics: VC-22.9, VC-18.1, VC-18.10
- Chain of events:
  1. The code is fail-closed: `public_reads_enabled()` ends `.unwrap_or(false)` (`rbac_gate.rs:135-141`) and boot refuses to start owner-less unless `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS` is set (`main.rs:809-818`).
  2. The shipped deployment inverts both: `docker-compose.unified.yml:132` turns public reads on and `:133` (with the `:-1` default) leaves the default role unset, so "an unassigned pubkey resolves to Editor (`role_store.rs:359`)" (VC-22.9).
  3. Key generation is free: identity is a nostr keypair; any generated key authenticates via NIP-98 and `GET /api/admin/rbac/whoami` "resolves to Authenticated before the Admin rule, so any signed-in user may read their own role" (`rbac_gate.rs:160-166`).
  4. With no explicit assignment, `effective_role` returns the default Editor (`role_store.rs:366`) — a mutating role — while public reads leave graph data readable (VC-18.10 explicitly places `/api/graph/data` and `/analytics/cycles` in the public GET class under the same posture).
  5. An uninvited key holder can therefore read the graph and act through every mutation route gated at the authenticated level (e.g. the whole `/analytics` POST family behind `mutations_only()`, VC-18.1), corrupting state that the provenance chain will then attribute to an attacker-chosen key.
- Evidence: VC-22.9 CORRECTED rect — "the CODE fails closed … The shipped compose inverts both (`docker-compose.unified.yml:132, :133` …) so an unassigned pubkey resolves to Editor (`role_store.rs:359`)", with the acknowledgment that "the open posture is ADR-2027's deliberate demo default and stays".
- Warning signs visible today: the compose-vs-code inversion is already documented as a standing divergence in the governing doc (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md` via VC-22.9); whoami's Authenticated resolution for any signed-in user is pinned at `rbac_gate.rs:160-166`.
- Confidence: high — the posture is stated verbatim; the exploitation steps are inferred from it.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — A routine force-full-sync wipes governed runtime ontology writes (data loss)
- Topics: VC-22.10, VC-22.8, VC-20.2, VC-20.4
- Chain of events:
  1. A human-approved enrichment or governed mutation lands via the write door: `add_owl_class`/`add_axiom` (`application/ontology/directives.rs`) or `ApplyMaterializedAxioms` (VC-20.8 routing note) writes into `GRAPH_ONTOLOGY :assert`.
  2. The operator later triggers `FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1` (or `?force_full=true`) to correct ingest drift — a routine act.
  3. `sync_graphs` clears `GRAPH_KNOWLEDGE` (`github_sync_service.rs:406-410`) and `rebuild_assert_graph` performs an atomic `CLEAR GRAPH assert` then INSERT of only corpus-derived, ontology-typed nodes and edges (`github_sync_service.rs:1026`).
  4. "This CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (`github_sync_service.rs:952-954`)" (VC-22.10).
  5. Those runtime writes are not protected: "a class added via add_owl_class/add_axiom between full-syncs is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph — only re-derivation from the corpus … restores it after the next rebuild" (VC-22.10).
  6. `rematerialise_decisions` re-derives only `dl:DecisionRecord` quads from corpus decision pages (`github_sync_service.rs:1065-1105`) and deliberately not attribution — the governed class content is not restored.
  7. The SQLite decision rows still read `approved`/`elevated` (VC-24.1), the forum may carry the 31404 receipt, but the graph content the approval produced is gone — a silent, provenance-invisible deletion with downstream effects on SPARQL answers, DAG ranks (VC-16.8) and physics.
- Evidence: VC-22.10 diagram notes at `github_sync_service.rs:952-954`, `:1026`, `:1042-1048`, `:1065-1105`; the durable-decision/KG split follows the no-2PC fan-out design of VC-22.8.
- Warning signs visible today: the wipe is documented in VC-22.10's own notes; the durability asymmetry (SQLite says approved; assert graph is rebuildable-only-from-corpus) is drawn in VC-22.8/VC-22.10.
- Confidence: high — the mechanism and trigger are explicit; only the operator's decision to run a full sync is routine-inferred.
- Severity: high

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — `owl-class` overrides an explicit `public: false` (private page ingested)
- Topics: VC-21.12, VC-21.2, VC-18.10
- Evidence: "an explicit public false plus a class marker remains included by policy — is_kg_included is an OR, so a valid owl-class overrides public false (`vault/mod.rs:162-163`)" (VC-21.12); the governing doc claims the opposite — "no `owl-class` bypass and no OR" (`docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md:236-238`, VC-21.2 Drift).
- Failure: an author marks a page `public: false` believing the doc; any valid class marker on that page ingests it as a KG node, and under the public-reads posture of R-2 the content is exposed via public GET surfaces.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in VC-21.12; Drift in VC-21.2)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Oxigraph has no point-in-time backup; provenance is permanently losable

- Failure: container/disk loss destroys the knowledge graph, inferred graph and the append-only provenance history in one event; SQLite members restore from `backup-sqlite.sh`, the triple store does not, and the provenance record of every governed decision is gone with no re-derivation path.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift note; PROPOSED ADR-2103)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Decision fan-out has no cross-store 2PC and no depicted retry for a failed KG writeback

- Evidence: "no cross-store 2PC — the durable decision commits in SQLite first (writeback_triggered=true) — the Oxigraph write is best-effort, writeback_committed flips only on Ok (`enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:576, :589`)" (VC-22.8); `mark_writeback_committed` is called only inside the Ok branch (VC-22.3).
- Failure: SQLite commits, the Oxigraph `INSERT DATA` fails (lock/crash); the decision is durably approved with `writeback_committed=false`, the summary facts never enter the KG, and nothing in the material shows a reconciler that retries — the forum may already have published 31403 success.
- Confidence: medium (the split state is explicit; the absence of a retry is inferred from silence)
- Marked by authors: no

ADR-2101 adds that to DecisionElevationActor only" (VC-24.5); the `elevating` map is in-memory (`elevation_actor.rs:1572`).

- Evidence: "three separate relay connections under three different panel identities can exist concurrently, all using the same bridge secret" (VC-24.11); the bridge bot keypair `VISIONCLAW_NOSTR_PRIVKEY` is "shared by both publisher and bridge" (`nostr_bead_publisher.rs:18`, `nostr_bridge.rs:12`, VC-24.8); the decide route is gated by a single shared `VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY` (VC-24.3, constant-time compared but one secret for all agents).

### F-08 — Wire node-id overflow masks and aliases another node
- Topics: VC-23.7, VC-32.6, VC-23.8
- Evidence: "An over-range id is still masked and therefore aliases, but never silently" (`binary_protocol.rs:185-206`, `remap_wire_id :194, :217-219`, ADR-2070 note in VC-23.7); ids are sequential from `NEXT_NODE_ID` (VC-32.6 note).
- Failure: once the counter crosses 2^26 in one process lifetime, every subsequent node shares a masked id with an existing node — positions, cluster/anomaly/community analytics on the wire (VC-32.5) merge two nodes' data across all clients while an `error!` logs. Durable store URNs are unaffected (VC-23.8), so the corruption is confined to the render/analytics plane — persistent and undetectable from the store side.
- Confidence: medium — the masking mechanism is stated; crossing 2^26 allocations in one uptime is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no (the aliasing is stated in a RESOLVED note but not tagged Tension/Debt/Drift/Open)
- Severity: medium

## visionclaw#3 / critical

### F-01 — The dev/unified compose ships with a peer-agnostic full auth bypass armed by default
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-33.3
- Evidence: `docker-compose.unified.yml:116` sets `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: "${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"` — ADR-2108 flipped the default from `:-0` to `:-1`, so `launch.sh up dev` "boots an ARMED backend and the operator opts OUT". When active, `dev_full_bypass_active()` grants every request as `dev-mode-local-admin` with "no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" (`src/utils/auth.rs:203-218`, pubkey at `:96`) — unlike the dev-auth bearer, there is no loopback-peer condition. The material also notes the hazard: "both services load env_file .env, so the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:113-115`). The documented XR deployment relies on this mode on a LAN IP (`XR_BACKEND_WS=ws://192.168.2.132`, "XR_NOSTR_SECRET can be empty", `graph_scene.gd:488-493`), and other bypass sites key off it (`src/settings/auth_extractor.rs:25`, `src/handlers/memory_cloud_handler.rs:66`, `src/handlers/speech_socket_handler.rs:165`).
- Failure: any host that can reach the dev/unified backend (the LAN the headset lives on) is admin with zero credentials — settings, graph writes, broker decisions, memory cloud, speech socket. One shared `.env` or one exposed port makes it a total compromise.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — documented as a decision (ADR-2039/ADR-2108) with an inline caveat; not tracked as a Tension/Open risk

### F-02 — The XR client authenticates only after upgrade, so release-mode servers refuse it — pushing operators into the F-01 bypass
- Topics: VC-48.1, VC-36.3
- Evidence: the XR transport "opens socket with URL string" (`xr-client/rust/src/transport.rs:95`) and sends the signed `authenticate` text only after upgrade (`transport.rs:99,115,118`). A release server "requires Origin" (`src/handlers/socket_flow_handler/http_handler.rs:67,129,131,139`) and verifies "signed subprotocol or legacy bearer at upgrade" (`:146,151,181,183`); "Unauthenticated release upgrade refused" (`:314,321,322`). The material concludes: "A secret used for the post-upgrade text message cannot satisfy earlier release Origin and authentication checks… The client and server implement different handshake phases." ADR-2076/ADR-2058 also deleted the `?token=` query fallback (VC-36.3), leaving the XR client no upgrade-time credential at all. Secondary: the NIP-98 `authenticate` ack is never read, so rejection is invisible (VC-36.3 Open, `transport.rs:117`).
- Failure: against any hardened/production deployment the primary immersive client is refused at upgrade. The obvious remediation an operator reaches for is enabling dev auth — i.e., F-01.
- Confidence: high on the asserted mismatch; medium, inferred, that no alternate client path exists
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension) — but framed only as an ADR-2161 federation prerequisite, not as a "current client cannot connect to release" defect

### F-03 — The material contradicts itself on where the passkey-derived Nostr private key lives
- Topics: VC-33.5, VC-33.9
- Evidence: the logout sequence removes `nostr_privkey` from sessionStorage (`nostrAuthService.ts:460-464`, alongside `nostr_passkey_pubkey`, `nostr_prf`), and `restorePasskeySession()` (`:357,581`) verifies a marker "against derived pubkey on restore" — implying the key survives reload. But VC-33.9's class diagram asserts `LocalPrivateKeyMaterial` is "storage in-memory only, never sessionStorage" (`nostrAuthService.ts:14,200`).
- Failure: if the removal path is live, the signing key that also roots the Solid WebID (`solid:oidcIssuer = did:nostr:hex-pubkey`, `pod-provisioning.test.ts:104`) sits in sessionStorage — XSS or shared-machine access lifts the identity and pod ownership wholesale. If it is dead cleanup, the credential-custody invariant an auditor would rely on is wrong.
- Confidence: high that the material contradicts itself; medium on which side is code-true
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Shipped compose RBAC posture contradicts the fail-closed binary default: public reads on, any unknown signed key becomes Editor
- Topics: VC-33.6, VC-35.7, VC-34.19
- Evidence: structural default is `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=false` (`src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:141`, `unwrap_or(false)`), but `docker-compose.unified.yml:132` sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1`, and an "unassigned authenticated pubkey resolves to Editor via `UserRole::default_authenticated()`" (`src/models/rbac.rs:68-70`, `src/services/role_store.rs:188,196-198`) — "two different postures depending whether you read the binary default or the shipped compose env". Amplifier: unbound voice falls through to the "ungoverned global settings assistant" (`speech_socket_handler.rs:868`) which applies changes via `/api/settings/*` (VC-34.19) — so the only real boundary is this permissive default RBAC.
- Failure: deploy by compose and any Nostr identity that completes NIP-98 gets Editor — graph/settings write rights, including by natural-language voice, with no grant ever issued.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-05 — Codescape runs unauthenticated on the LAN with a spoofable presence identity, and VisionClaw presence has no membership gate
- Topics: VC-39.7, VC-43.1, VC-49.1, VC-39.2
- Evidence: the Codescape main port "runs OpenAccess, reported honestly as auth open" (`codescape-server/src/auth.rs:120-124`, `routes/mod.rs:71`) while `/codescape/` is LAN-published via `nginx.dev.conf:63-65`. The presence shim "accepts the Schnorr signature unverified, the claimed DID is only a label" (`xr_presence.rs:20-23,65-67`; `routes/presence.rs:98,136`), and "Missing Origin is allowed for native clients" (`origin.rs:127`). Separately, the VisionClaw presence join has identity and duplicate checks but "no membership gate or cap" (`room.rs:42,45,53,57`; `presence_handler.rs:281-306`).
- Failure: anyone on the network reaches the shared code scene anonymously, joins presence claiming a colleague's DID (impersonation in a scene people make decisions over), and drives voice commands; in VisionClaw, any signed key joins any room and sees all poses.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN R1/R2; VC-43 Tension; VC-49 business summary) — marked but unfixed

### F-06 — Jev egress: an explicit remote endpoint bypasses the documented locality gate, and the host already grants cloud routing
- Topics: VC-42.2, VC-42.3, VC-39.7
- Evidence: the resolver returns an explicitly configured endpoint at `voice/jev.rs:190` before the local/cloud permission check at `:187,200`; the "local-only" promise (`jev.rs:11`) and resolver contract (`:169`) do not cover that branch. "The current host intentionally enables cloud routing (`docker-compose.unified.yml:505`)". Also the default confidence threshold is 1.0 and an exact-1.0 response passes (`jev.rs:182,409`), while compose calls the option "advisory" (`:507`).
- Failure: an operator relying on the local-only documentation sends user utterances to a remote model endpoint; a model-chosen command executes at confidence exactly 1.0.
- Confidence: high (executable probe)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, Tension)

### F-07 — An approved amendment can change the "immutable" resource; the writer has no after-apply identity check
- Topics: VC-41.4
- Evidence: the writer checks the current resource against the proposal IRI only before applying (`write/apply.rs:229,230`), then applies the patch and re-parses (`:242,258`) and persists (`:282,395`) with "no after-resource equality check" (`:280,282`). The audit drove a real signed producer output through the real CLI writer and observed the fixture page's resource replaced.
- Failure: a sanctioned, approved amendment silently swaps a page's resource identity — violating the corpus immutability invariant; certificates, links and ACLs keyed on the original IRI now reference changed content, with no detection.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT)

### F-08 — Grouped proposals are signable, judgable, and can never be applied by the consumer
- Topics: VC-41.3
- Evidence: the producer groups multi-page changes under the proposal IRI (`vault-core/src/proposal.rs:225,234`; `vault/src/propose.rs:590`) and the group can be signed and posted (`vault/src/main.rs:478,494`), but the consumer requires the note-page slug to answer to the subject IRI (`ontology-apply.js:570,699`) — "real grouped payload fails identity check before invoking writer" (`:571,700`), with further guards refusing grouped application (`:574,702`). "A supported all-or-nothing group apply/recovery contract, or explicit pre-signing refusal, is not established."
- Failure: human judgements accumulate against proposals that cannot land through the apply path; someone hand-splits them after the fact, defeating single-judgement governance and creating decided-vs-applied divergence.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT + Open)

### F-09 — Single-use NIP-98 replay protection appears to be per-process state: a restart or second replica reopens replay
- Topics: VC-33.2, VC-33.3, VC-35.4
- Evidence: tokens are validated by a "server-side single-use replay cache and freshness window — ADR-2002 `src/utils/nip98.rs`" (VC-33.2 note; same cache cited for the speech socket in VC-35.4). `verify_access` "reuses the signer an outer RbacGate or RequireAuth already verified (NIP-98 ids are single-use, a second verification would be a replay)" (`src/utils/auth.rs:272,338`) — the design assumes one process saw the first use. Nothing in the material places the cache in shared storage.
- Failure: with two server replicas (or after a restart clears the cache), a captured `Authorization: Nostr` token is replayable within the freshness window; the chained "outer gate already verified" assumption also fails silently on any route that omits the gate.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the material never states the cache's backing store or replica plans
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Code→concept link records carry no repository/revision provenance, and string literals can fabricate annotations
- Topics: VC-45.1, VC-45.2, VC-45.3
- Evidence: records are emitted with "Repository empty and revision None" (`xr-client/rust/src/link_index.rs:281,284`) even though the snapshot is labelled with repository and revision — "the stream omits these provenance values and the join deliberately supplies empty defaults". The scanner accepts comment-shaped string content (`annotations.rs:125,159` — a compiled raw-string fixture became a bound annotation), and only the first 32 refs per node reach the wire (`annotations.rs:239`) so the consumer cannot count what was dropped.
- Failure: the shared XR scene presents code→concept claims that cannot be audited to any revision, and any file containing a comment-looking line inside a string injects edges into the knowledge view; truncation hides the omission.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — Tension on the scanner; provenance gap stated in the summaries but not marker-tagged

### F-11 — Presence replacement leaves the old handle able to relay, and one bounded queue drops roster events
- Topics: VC-43.2, VC-43.3
- Evidence: on replacement the hub removes the entry and `try_send`s Close (`xr_presence.rs:565-568`), but `relay` "checks held room and avatar only" (`:668`), so the removed member's pose "carries removed local_id" to observers and the replacement (`:673,677`) — "roster removal does not revoke relay authority" against the closure promise at `:550`. All message kinds, including AvatarJoined/AvatarLeft, go through `try_send` on one 64-message queue (`:71,521,524,603`); a full queue loses the announcement, and the client then falls back to a decimal local id (`xr-client/rust/src/presence.rs:633,636`).
- Failure: a reconnect flap (common with headsets) produces ghost poses attributed to removed or wrong members; any slow reader's roster is inconsistent with no resync path.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Drift)

### F-12 — XR frame/visual budget guarantees are violated by the highlight path and under-counted by the accounting
- Topics: VC-46.1, VC-47.3, VC-51.2, VC-51.3
- Evidence: the cap is 512 link *records* (`link_layer.gd:33,317`) but highlight markers are appended after the cap (`:430,431,440,445`) with instance count set to links+markers (`:448`), all charged at 12 triangles (`:167`) — the fixture hit 1,025 instances / 12,300 triangles, exceeding ADR-2164 D8/L5. Portal membranes and fade meshes are "absent from the scene's explicit draw-call sum" (`graph_scene.gd:3079,3080`), the live pass has "no other-call remainder" (`frame_budget_pass.gd:102,125`), and nine constructed frames exceeded the clamped price by 24 triangles. Sparse GPU timing can also leave quality tier 4 requested for 40s (VC-51.3).
- Failure: highlight-heavy or portal-heavy sessions overrun the stereo frame budget — dropped frames and comfort regressions — while monitoring and CI under-count, so the regression is not caught.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Debt)

- Topics: VC-44 (header, 44.3), VC-40

VC-40's exclusive-switch diagrams describe deleted code.
- Failure: the production XR client runs an architecture whose decision record is inactive and whose acceptance evidence doesn't exist; anyone auditing via ADRs believes the code isn't there, and failures in this path surface only in-headset.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

- Topics: VC-35.6, VC-35.12, VC-35.4
- Evidence: Whisper-WebUI is "a BROKEN SYMLINK… so the STT container source is not checked out here" (VC-35.6 DIVERGENCE); Kokoros, Whisper-WebUI and xinference are untracked dangling symlinks (VC-35.12 DIV); the unmute checkout is nested, untracked, with a dirty tree — "read the declared revision, never the files on disk" (DEBT); the upstream compose "must NOT be launched independently" (INVARIANT, discipline-only); and ADR-2151 is `proposed/inactive/none` while "the live manifest has the [voicelink] slot enabled = true" (`agentbox.toml:2226`, TENSION).

### F-15 — Agent polling stops permanently after three consecutive failures
- Topics: VC-34.9
- Evidence: on a thrown request, "retryCount++, retryDelay backoff; break retryCount >= maxRetries (3) → stop() — polling halted, error surfaced via errorCallbacks". Restart occurs only on a new subscriber mount; nothing described resumes a stopped service.
- Failure: a server restart or brief network blip freezes the agent population view on stale data; operators watch what looks like a live feed of agent activity that is hours old until someone reloads.
- Confidence: high on the stated halt; medium, inferred, that no external watchdog restarts it
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual deployment topology and exposure**: which compose services run where, on what networks, and whether any release-mode (non-dev) backend is deployed at all — this determines whether F-01 and F-02 are live incidents or latent, and how far F-05's LAN exposure reaches.
2. **Durability of server state**: the backing store of the RBAC role store (`role_store.rs`), the ADR-2009 session/token table, ACSP broker cases, and XR node pins — what a server crash or restart loses, corrupts, or resets.
3. **Whether auth/session state is per-process memory**: the NIP-98 replay cache, the clarification-gate pending turns, and the token store — needed to settle F-09 for both restart and any multi-replica plan.
4. **Current XR socket/identity code at the deployed pin**: the material itself marks VC-36's graph-socket, polling, edge-multimesh and identity diagrams STALE after the spatial-volume merge, and VC-40/VC-44 supersede each other — a re-read of `graph_scene.gd`/`transport.rs`/`backend_connection.gd` at the revision actually deployed is required before any of the XR findings are actioned.
5. **Whether service secrets in `AppFullSettings` are readable via the settings API**: the LiveKit URL/API key/secret fields exist (`crates/visionclaw-domain/src/config/services.rs:159-168`) and client settings types are generated from that struct (`generate_types.rs`); whether an ordinary authenticated client can GET them determines if this is a live credential leak.

## visionclaw#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — data, custody and security failure, twelve months on

This document reconstructs the most probable root causes from the pre-incident record. Every claim below is tied to a citation the record itself carries; where I reason past the record I say so. Nothing here was independently re-verified against code; the citations are the distillation's own.

---

### R-1 — Codescape cannot be authenticated by construction, and the whole estate's source graph sits inside it
- Topics: VC-39.2, VC-39.7, VC-43.1
- Chain of events: Codescape ships in the unified compose with read-only views of the estate scene and ten member repositories mounted into its container. Its auth module refuses to start if `require_auth` is set, so the main listener (4100) runs OpenAccess and returns no pubkey. Exposure is one configuration step away: `CODESCAPE_PUBLISH_ADDR` changes the loopback-only publish, and the dev nginx on :3001 already proxies `/codescape/` by owner decision. Once any of those reaches a network the operator does not control, an unauthenticated peer reaches the REST/WS surface: reads the code graph over the ten repositories, claims the voice floor, runs typed `Command`s through the same executor as typed input, and — with Loom configured — gets free-text answers spoken back. The presence hub accepts any claimed DID as a label. The client's code structure and shared-session control are disclosed to an outsider.
- Evidence: `auth.rs:137,138` (require_auth=true → refuse startup); `auth.rs:120-124,142` OpenAccess returns no pubkey (`codescape-server/src/auth.rs`); mounts of ten repositories `docker-compose.unified.yml:538-619, :492`; port 4100 published to 127.0.0.1 by default `docker-compose.unified.yml:641`, direct publish only with `CODESCAPE_PUBLISH_ADDR`; `nginx.dev.conf:63-65` LAN-published dev-only waiver, `:66, :74-77` proxy; missing Origin allowed for native clients `codescape-server/src/origin.rs:127`; command execution path `codescape-server/src/routes/mod.rs:291-357`, Loom validation `codescape/crates/codescape-collab/src/llm.rs:209`; presence signature ignored `codescape-server/src/routes/presence.rs:98,136`; egress allowlist explicitly not an egress boundary, ADR-2143 `:82, :144`; ADR-2143 `implementation_status: partial, activation_status: inactive` at `:6-7`.
- Warning signs visible today: the record reports the posture "honestly as auth open" as an open item until P6; the ingest port 4101 got a token but the main port got nothing; the prod `/codescape/` path currently answers via SPA fallback, i.e. it is one nginx edit from being live; presence identity is label-only.
- Confidence: high that auth is open and cannot be enabled without code change (stated); the production-exposure step is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The dev-mode LAN bypass ships armed by default in the compose the estate actually runs, peer-agnostic and un-warned
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-33.6, VC-36.16
- Chain of events: ADR-2108 flipped the dev service's `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE` default from `:-0` to `:-1`, so `launch.sh up dev` boots an armed backend and the operator must opt out. On a dev/dev-auth build with the variable set, `dev_full_bypass_active` grants every request as `dev-mode-local-admin` — no NIP-98, no token, no peer check. The hygiene guard exists only in release binaries; in dev builds the hook is an empty stub, so nothing warns at boot. The team's own deploy ceremony runs debug builds. Twelve months later the unified compose — which by the later stamps hosts agentbox, System One, Jev, pocket-tts and Codescape, i.e. the canonical stack — is brought up for client work; either the dev service definition is reused, or a `.env` shared between services carries the variable (the record flags exactly this sharpend risk). Every peer who can reach the backend becomes admin over the `/vault` corpus, graph writes and settings. Independently, the same compose sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` and resolves unassigned pubkeys to Editor, so even with dev mode off the shipped posture is permissive reads plus a write-capable default role, diverging from the fail-closed struct default.
- Evidence: `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: "${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"` `docker-compose.unified.yml:116`; dev-service scoping and the common-environment exclusion `docker-compose.unified.yml:109-112`; the `.env` sharing caveat `docker-compose.unified.yml:113-115`; release-only refusal `project/src/main.rs:109, :136, :161`, dev stub `project/src/main.rs:169`; bypass acceptance and peer-agnostic grant `src/utils/auth.rs:116-121, :96, :203-218`, banner `project/src/main.rs:299`; debug-library headset runs `xr-client/rust/Cargo.toml:67-68`; `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` `docker-compose.unified.yml:132`; default Editor `src/models/rbac.rs:68-70`, `src/services/role_store.rs:188, :196-198`; the fail-closed/divergence note and the other dev-bypass sites (`src/settings/auth_extractor.rs:25`, `src/handlers/memory_cloud_handler.rs:66`, `src/handlers/speech_socket_handler.rs:165`).
- Warning signs visible today: the flip itself ("the operator opts OUT with VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=0"); the record's own sharpened .env warning; the RBAC double posture; the debug-build deploy norm.
- Confidence: high that the armed default and peer-agnostic grant exist as described (stated); the path from dev compose to a client-facing host is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The proposal writer can swap a corpus resource the format declares immutable, with no after-the-fact identity check
- Topics: VC-41.4, VC-41.2, VC-41.3
- Chain of events: The proposal producer emits a page that changes an existing resource's IRI while keeping the proposal subject as the original resource, and assesses before/after states without any continuity comparison. The CLI writer checks the current resource against the proposal IRI — which passes, because the page still carries its original identity before application — then applies the patch, parses the new page, checks only for introduced errors, and persists. The applied page now carries the replacement resource; no after-resource equality check exists. Because the case digest excludes consequences and generation, and the diff machinery counts content-derived version metadata, an approval can be made to look conservative while the identity of a corpus entity changes underneath it. Over twelve months of amendments, an approved change silently re-keys a vault entity; anything anchored to the old IRI — certificates, inferences, erasure and authority obligations keyed by identifier — now points at nothing or at the wrong entity.
- Evidence: page changes existing Alpha resource `crates/vault/src/propose.rs:468`; assessment without continuity `propose.rs:557, :558, :568`; subject remains original `propose.rs:663, :665`; old-resource check `ontomark/crates/ontomark-compile/src/write/apply.rs:229, :230`; apply and parse `:242, :258`; replacement persists with no after-check `:280, :282, :395`; digest excludes consequences and generation `crates/vault-core/src/proposal.rs:260, :364`; content-derived versionIRI participates in the closure comparison `crates/vault/src/build/turtle.rs:495, :513`, `ontomark/crates/rdfs-certificate/src/lib.rs:603, :630, :642`; the grouped consumer fails before the writer `agentbox/management-api/lib/ontology-apply.js:570, :571, :699, :700`.
- Warning signs visible today: the record demonstrates the drift on real signed producer output through the real CLI writer, and states the governing invariant "needs a before/after identity check, which the inspected amendment path lacks"; it also concedes the probe does not execute live approval.
- Confidence: high that the check is absent (demonstrated on the real writer); the production-scale corruption chain is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Codescape presence verifies no signature; the claimed DID is only a label
- Topics: VC-43.1, VC-39.7
- Evidence: "Signature ignored, DID resolved as label" `codescape-server/src/routes/presence.rs:98, :136`; open item with room bounds only `codescape-server/src/xr_presence.rs:20-23, :65-67` (VC-39.7 R2).
- Failure: an attacker joins the hub claiming the operator's DID; roster entries and poses are attributed to that identity in every consumer, with nothing to distinguish them from the real holder.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-02 — A replaced presence handle keeps relay ability after the roster has moved on
- Topics: VC-43.2
- Evidence: removal and Close at `codescape-server/src/xr_presence.rs:565-577`; `relay` checks held room and avatar only `:668`; poses carry the removed local_id to observers and the replacement `:673, :677`; drop removes only the old local_id `:638, :683`; the contract promises closure `:550` (authors' Tension).
- Failure: a stale or attacker-held old socket injects poses attributed to an id the room no longer associates with the live member, during the window before Close is consumed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-03 — One bounded 64-slot queue drops roster transitions with no resynchronisation
- Topics: VC-43.3
- Evidence: `try_send` for every message kind `codescape-server/src/xr_presence.rs:521, :524`; queue size `:71, :603`; join/leave announcements `:594, :602, :640`; forwarding of queued bytes `codescape-server/src/routes/presence.rs:198, :200`; client falls back to a numeric id when the mapping was the dropped event `xr-client/rust/src/presence.rs:591, :599, :633, :636`.
- Failure: under load a consumer permanently misses an AvatarJoined/AvatarLeft and renders a ghost or mislabels a participant, with no resync path to recover.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Legacy presence rooms admit any verified DID: no membership gate, no cap
- Topics: VC-49.1
- Evidence: identity checks then direct join `src/handlers/presence_handler.rs:210-306`; duplicate-only rejection `crates/visionclaw-xr-presence/src/room.rs:42, :45, :53, :57`, `src/actors/presence_actor.rs:684`; "no membership gate or cap", 33 distinct members accepted in isolation.
- Failure: any holder of any valid key joins any client's room and observes the roster and pose traffic; nothing in the join path asks whether they belong.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Signed grouped proposals can never be applied by the shipped consumer
- Topics: VC-41.3
- Evidence: group subject is the proposal IRI `crates/vault-core/src/proposal.rs:225, :234`; any new page forces create kind `crates/vault/src/propose.rs:590`; consumer requires a note-page slug `agentbox/management-api/lib/ontology-apply.js:570, :571, :699, :700`, further guards `:574, :702`; no all-or-nothing group recovery contract established (Open).
- Failure: a legitimately signed multi-page decision reaches review, is approved, and then dead-ends before the writer — the judgement exists and nothing lands, or a future consumer applies it under assumptions this one forbids.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift and Open)

### F-06 — The advisory consequence delta can count version metadata and exclude the real logical change, while the case number stays stable
- Topics: VC-41.1, VC-41.2
- Evidence: consequences do not automatically raise the level and are advisory `crates/vault/src/propose.rs:576, :599, :611, :615`; graphs include content-derived versionIRI `crates/vault/src/build/turtle.rs:495, :513`; the restriction-filler probe can reduce the diff to version-only triples `ontomark/crates/rdfs-certificate/src/lib.rs:642`; consequences and generation sit outside the case digest `crates/vault-core/src/proposal.rs:260, :364`.
- Failure: a reviewer approves on a conservative-looking consequence list whose non-conservative verdict was caused solely by version metadata while the changed logical expression was excluded — or rejects a sound change for the inverse reason — under a case number that appears to fix the assessment.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — `verify_access` checks only the required level, reusing a signer an outer gate supposedly verified
- Topics: VC-33.3
- Evidence: "verify_access reuses the signer an outer RbacGate or RequireAuth already verified (NIP-98 ids are single-use, a second verification would be a replay) and checks only the level required here" — `src/utils/auth.rs:272, :338`.
- Failure: the invariant is load-bearing on middleware presence and ordering. Any future route that reaches `verify_access` without an outer `RbacGate`/`RequireAuth`, or after a reorder, performs a level-only check on a principal nobody authenticated — an authorisation bypass that looks like normal code.
- Confidence: low (inferred; the record states the reuse as designed behaviour, not the failure)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — The XR client never learns whether its NIP-98 `authenticate` was accepted
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.4
- Evidence: authenticate sent after `Connected`, acceptance never read `xr-client/rust/src/transport.rs:112-119`; the client has no Authenticated state (VC-36.4 note); the visible symptom is the write-denied notice (VC-36.15).
- Failure: a rejected or malformed signature leaves the session anonymous and unread; the operator keeps using a headset whose every write is being refused, and the position read stream continuing makes the session look healthy.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-09 — XR authentication happens after the release upgrade gates it cannot satisfy
- Topics: VC-48.1
- Evidence: the client passes URL and secret for a post-upgrade text frame `xr-client/scripts/backend_connection.gd:316, :322`, `xr-client/rust/src/transport.rs:95, :99, :115, :118`; the release server requires Origin at upgrade `src/handlers/socket_flow_handler/http_handler.rs:67, :129, :131, :139` and a signed subprotocol or bearer at `:146, :151, :181, :183`, refusing at `:314, :321, :322` (authors' Tension against ADR-2161's prerequisite).
- Failure: a release-mode deployment strands every XR content connection at upgrade; the practical pressure is to keep dev-auth builds reachable, which feeds R-2.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-10 — A dormant server-side session-bearer realm no legitimate client ever uses
- Topics: VC-33.3, VC-33.9
- Evidence: ADR-2009 legacy session-bearer fallback (UUID minted at login, plain-equality check against `token_expiry`) accepted in `src/utils/auth.rs` and `src/services/nostr_service.rs:500`; the browser client never constructs it — `getSessionToken()` always returns null `nostrAuthService.ts:435`; the class diagram records the realm as "NEVER constructed by this client" (Drift-adjacent note).
- Failure: unmaintained acceptance surface whose enforcement details (mint path, comparison, expiry handling) no shipped client exercises; any weakness there is invisible to client-side testing and exists purely for attackers.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-11 — The passkey fallback makes a downloaded file the only copy of an identity key
- Topics: VC-33.4, VC-33.9
- Evidence: `downloadKeyBackup()` generates a random key, forces a file download and marks it the ONLY copy `client/src/services/passkeyService.ts:349-377`.
- Failure: the user's Nostr writing identity — the thing that gates graph writes and their WebID bootstrap — lives as one file in a Downloads folder; loss or theft of that file is loss or theft of the identity, with no recovery path.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The Jev resolver returns an explicit endpoint before the locality/permission gate, and compose already routes to the cloud
- Topics: VC-42.2, VC-39.7
- Evidence: explicit endpoint returns at `codescape/crates/codescape-server/src/voice/jev.rs:190-192` ahead of the local/cloud permission check `:187, :200-201`; the local-only promise `:11` and resolver contract `:169` do not cover that branch (authors' Drift); the host enables cloud routing `docker-compose.unified.yml:505`; the same resolver supplies the allowlist URL `codescape-server/src/config.rs:281-282`.
- Failure: operator utterance text leaves the premises through an endpoint that bypasses the documented locality gate, under a config the operator believed enforced local-only.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-13 — Proxy-trust semantics: an empty peer list trusts everyone, and trust follows re-resolved hostnames
- Topics: VC-39.6
- Evidence: trust enabled with an empty list applies to any peer `codescape-server/src/proxy.rs:268-278`; hostname entries resolved at boot and every 60s `:115, :243-262`, `codescape-server/src/main.rs:198-237`; the trusted origin is used to rebuild the NIP-98 verification URL `proxy.rs:368-375`, `routes/mod.rs:463-466`, `routes/ws.rs:263-266`, and the rate-limit key `proxy.rs:295-308`; compose narrows trust to the VisionClaw alias `docker-compose.unified.yml:514, :518-519`.
- Failure: a `--trust-proxy` copied without its peer list, or an alias/DNS takeover on the Docker network, lets a peer forge the forwarded headers that determine the NIP-98 verification URL and rate-limit identity.
- Confidence: medium (mechanism stated; the misuse and takeover paths are inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Code-to-concept links carry no repository or revision provenance, under an ADR that says nothing was built
- Topics: VC-45.2, VC-45.3
- Evidence: records emitted with repository empty and revision `None` `xr-client/rust/src/link_index.rs:281, :284`, deliberately defaulted; ADR-2164's "nothing built" verification predates the implemented producer, join and scene wiring (authors' Drift); the producer caps wire refs at 32 per node so the consumer cannot count omissions `codescape/crates/codescape-ingest/src/annotations.rs:239`.
- Failure: review surfaces present code-to-concept edges that cannot name which revision supplied the claim; a stale link from an old checkout renders identically to a current one, and its omission is unmeasurable downstream.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, for the ADR-verification part)

### F-15 — The annotation scanner accepts comment-shaped lines inside string literals
- Topics: VC-45.1
- Evidence: line-oriented recognition of a comment leader and marker shapes `codescape/crates/codescape-ingest/src/annotations.rs:125, :159`; a comment-looking line inside a Rust raw string enters the same branch; the contract describes a comment annotation (authors' Tension).
- Failure: anyone who can commit code plants `ontology_refs` inside string content, silently creating authoritative-looking code-to-concept links in every consumer of the graph.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

## visionflow / critical

Findings ranked by production damage: security, then custody/data loss, then correctness, operability, maintainability. Unmarked items are called out explicitly.

### F-01 — Signed-request replay is prevented only within one process; a second replica or a restart re-opens the window
- Topics: VF-08.2
- Evidence: "VisionClaw process-local single-use cache — the event id is recorded atomically after validation (`nip98.rs:164`)" and "Restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring require separate proof. EXTERNAL: the cache is declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself `nip98.rs:210`". The same signed NIP-98 request is also verified by the pod seam, forum/worker contracts and agentbox proxy boundary, each with its own unproven replay semantics.
- Failure: the deployment grows to two VisionClaw replicas (or restarts mid-window); a captured NIP-98 authorization is replayed against the instance that did not record the event id and passes validation, repeating whatever action it authorized.
- Confidence: medium — the single-process boundary is stated; whether the event's own timestamps bound the replay window is not stated.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (stated as a limitation in the VF-08.2 LIMIT node, "requires separate proof")

### F-02 — The Jev advisory sends cited sibling-source lines to an external backend by default, including lines from a private repository, labelled data-class public
- Topics: VF-05.14, VF-06.15, VF-03.1, VF-06.12
- Evidence: since 2026-10-30 the claim review "is no longer local-only by default: it sends excerpts of sibling checkouts to the shared proxy's selected backend (TypeSafe) unless `VISIONFLOW_JEV_CLAIMS_LOCAL_ONLY=1` pins it local" (`scripts/check-augmentation-citations.cjs:13-16`, `docs/jev-option.md:12`); the diagram review sends "the diagram line that carries it plus the cited lines, sent as a typed question set, data class public" (`scripts/diagram-index-gen.cjs:815-818`); those citations "reach sixteen repositories at forty-one declared revisions, two of them unfetchable here (a private repo, a local-only clone)" (`.github/workflows/diagram-index.yml:30`), and one source repository is the PRIVATE cross-owner vault `jjohare/visionGraph` (`scripts/estate-health/roster.json:17`). Agentbox projects `VISIONFLOW_JEV=all` into shells (`docs/jev-option.md:16`).
- Failure: an operator runs the gate locally with the private vault checked out; vault lines are transmitted to a third-party endpoint under a public data class with no gate, no opt-in prompt and no stated log of what was sent.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — each link is stated (private repo among sources; cited lines sent; default now non-local) but the material never states private-repo lines were actually transmitted.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — presented as a decision with an opt-out; the only OPEN marker on Jev concerns the unfitted 0.9 threshold, not data egress

### F-03 — The night engine has fabricated candidate branches, PRs and ADR citations before, and nothing in this repo enforces the human gate that is the only control
- Topics: VF-04.8, VF-04.9, VF-04.6
- Evidence: the 2026-09-07 OPERATOR audit: "no branch and no PR ever existed for the 09-03 or 09-04 candidates; both patches targeted a path that has never existed in this repo, and the on-disk receipts contradict the reported counters. The parent receipts and findings are genuine; the candidate halves are not" (`docs/dream-cycle/LEDGER.md:20`); "nothing in this repo enforces the draft-PR shape — the branch prefix, the labels and autoMerge false are declarations the external engine honours — no workflow here rejects a dream/ branch that skipped the gate" (VF-04.9 note); reports have cited "ADR-0057, ADR-056 and ADR-2024 — none exist" (`dream.config.json:73`).
- Failure: the cycle runs unattended nightly; a report with fabricated or wrong receipts reaches a human who merges on trust of those receipts, the merge triggers `deploy.yml`, and a fabricated patch reaches the public site. The precedent shows the engine does fabricate when it fails.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE note in VF-04.9; the fabrication audit is itself a ledger OPERATOR row) — but the standing exposure is treated as accepted, not tracked

### F-04 — Financial settlement rests on a custodial root chain with one signer we operate
- Topics: VF-01.14
- Evidence: ADR-2012 P5: "custody is stated honestly: the root chain is LEVEL 1 with one signer we operate and is CUSTODIAL; the k-of-n federation is rung 2 of the hardening path, not reached" (`docs/adr/ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:84-87`), while the eight sidestr crates are published and probed (`roster.json:73-80`) and the public site carries the sidechain tiles.
- Failure: the single signing key is a single point of failure and full custody concentration — key loss, host compromise or operational error freezes or loses settled funds, with no quorum fallback and no date given for reaching rung 2.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — P5 is written as honest disclosure ("custody is stated honestly"), not flagged as a risk; no Tension/Debt/Open marker attaches to it

### F-05 — Inbound customer contact has no delivery receipt and no server-side copy: a failed browser publish loses the message silently
- Topics: VF-02.3, VF-02.2, VF-02.11
- Evidence: "the form gift-wraps the message (NIP-17) in the browser and publishes it to the DreamLab relay, nothing is stored by this site" (`website/static/js/contact.js:5-6`, `contact.js:112`, `website/assets.manifest.json:13-14`); the transport is "a 57 KB vendored minified" file "rebuilt in dreamlab-ai-website and committed here, not compiled here" (`website/static/js/nostr-contact.js:1-2`); the publication path is browser-free — "The publication path has no browser gate at all" (VF-02.11 DRIFT).
- Failure: a customer submits the form; the relay publish fails (relay down, wrap bug, stale vendored transport) after the UI accepted the send. No copy exists anywhere, no gate exercises the code (presence in `dist/` is all that is checked), and the message is unrecoverable.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — browser-only publish and zero storage are stated; the UI's failure behaviour is not
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the CONTACT note describes the design, not the loss mode; the no-browser-gate DRIFT is marked but never connected to the contact channel

### F-06 — A release manifest can record `released`/`match` while comparing nothing: the canonical revision is not bound to the hashed bytes, and missing consumers do not fail
- Topics: VF-07 (dated audit), VF-07.3, VF-05.6
- Evidence: "The generator verifies that the commit exists, then hashes the current directory rather than that commit's tree (`scripts/generate-release-manifest.sh:179`, `:194`). A temporary-repository probe emitted the same canonical revision with different corpus hashes after an uncommitted edit. A second probe returned `released`/`match` with zero matched and two missing consumer fixtures, because only checksum mismatches fail the comparator (`scripts/check-fixture-drift.sh:257`)"; additionally "consumers is always an empty array" (`generate-release-manifest.sh:205`) and the comparator's unqualified default points at a directory "REMOVED" in 2026-06-29 so unqualified runs "exit 2, never 0" (`check-fixture-drift.sh:82-93`).
- Failure: an operator cuts a `released` manifest from a dirty tree or with consumers absent; the artefact asserts estate-wide fixture parity that never happened, and downstream consumers treat compatibility as verified.
- Confidence: high — reproduced by probe in the material's own 2026-10-31 audit
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt marker, dated audit correction; DIVERGENCE for the empty consumers array and the stale default)

### F-07 — The estate-health verdict can be OK while measuring nothing, and a failed build renders publicly as "no CI by design"
- Topics: VF-03.4, VF-03.6, VF-03.11
- Evidence: "A schema tag and current timestamp without any repository/surface arrays returns `ESTATE-HEALTH-OK`; a 2099 timestamp also returns OK" (`scripts/estate-health.mjs:967`, `:984`, `:1012`); "AMBER prints a line but does NOT change the verdict" and readable-false rows are amber-class (`estate-health.mjs:978`); knowledgeGraph's "last Build and verify run failed on an older commit, so the HEAD rule reports none and ci_by_design turns that into exempt" (`estate-health.json:480`, `:500-501`), and the site renders exempt as the pill "no CI by design" (`website/static/js/main.js:599`); a busy repo reads none because one page of 100 runs "pushes its default-branch runs off it" (`estate-health.mjs:609`).
- Failure: the dream estate-health nights and the reported CI step read OK from a truncated or future-dated snapshot while real failures go unnamed; the public page tells visitors a failing repository has no CI on purpose — a published claim above its evidence tier, which the canon's own rule calls a governance DEFECT (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:315`).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the snapshot-shape and one-page debts carry Debt markers; the failed-build→"exempt" pill path and amber/unreadable verdict-neutrality are stated as behaviour, not flagged as defects

### F-08 — The estate is enumerated three disagreeing ways, and the release roster omits the financial substrate while pinning an archived repository
- Topics: VF-03.1, VF-07.2, VF-07.6, VF-03 (drift note)
- Evidence: the nightly roster holds 18 repositories (`scripts/estate-health/roster.json:5-22`); the release generator's roster holds 16 (`scripts/generate-release-manifest.sh:91`) and its gate test asserts "exactly sixteen" (`tests/gates/release-manifest.test.sh:34-48`) with `minItems 16` hard-coded in the schema (`docs/releases/ecosystem-release.schema.json:14-15`); the repository map lists 12 and defers to the roster "for nightly collection" only (`docs/architecture/repository-map.md:8`, `:25`). The release roster's membership includes dream-machine, logseq, ruvector and RuView (VF-07.2 EXTERNAL note) while the dream-machine fork "is renamed dream-machine and archived" (VF-03 Drift note); sidestr-rs, added to the nightly roster 2026-09-23 (`roster.json:13`), appears in no release-roster citation.
- Failure: a release manifest is cut that pins the archived dream-machine and omits sidestr-rs's eight crates — the financial settlement substrate moves under a release with no record, exactly the defect class the 2026-09-05 fix closed (`generate-release-manifest.sh:11`) — and the "exactly sixteen" test makes adding it a gate failure rather than a silent gap.
- Confidence: medium — assembled from the three rosters' cited contents; no document states the release roster's full membership list
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the dream-engine/dream-machine row carries a Drift marker; the sidestr-rs omission and the 12/16/18 disagreement are unmarked

The publication path has no browser gate at all — every blocking gate in VF-02.6 is browser-free by design" (VF-02.11 DRIFT); "no browser evaluator on either path. The mesh module is read by nothing — verified by grep" (`docs/adr/ADR-2009-webgl-mesh-deep-is-sidecar-only.md:84`); the a11y and perf specs "run only by hand" (`package.json:15`); the CDP browser receipt covering the three mesh scenarios is a manual instrument (`scripts/website-browser-check.mjs`).
- Failure: a runtime regression in `main.js` (estate section render), `mesh-webgl.js` or `contact.js` — the exact classes the manual receipt exists to catch — merges and auto-deploys green through all six blocking gates; the broken page ships until a human happens to open it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT in VF-02.11; "COVERAGE GAP, by decision" in VF-04.10) — marked, then accepted as a decision

### F-10 — The self-description drift counter fails open exactly when its truth sources are unreachable
- Topics: VF-02.8, VF-05.4, VF-01.10
- Evidence: ADR-2005 is implementation_status PARTIAL, "2 of 4 axes unenforced" (`docs/adr/ADR-2005-drift-counter-allowlist-substrate-sourced.md:30`, `:46`); on the publication path the agentbox checkout is optional with "continue-on-error true" (`.github/workflows/deploy.yml:64`) and in REPORTED mode "an axis whose truth lives in a sibling repo cannot be evaluated... so it is not allowed to block. DIVERGENCE: on a runner without the checkout this axis is never actually measured on the publication path, yet the page still ships" (VF-02.8 note); per-axis, an unavailable source degrades to reported (`scripts/drift-counter/drift-counter.mjs:266`); the bridge the tool-count axis watched is already "retired in current Agentbox" (`allowlist.json:32`).
- Failure: agentbox reorganizes its scripts; every axis goes unmeasured, the deploy still ships, and the published page's figures rest on nothing — the canon's rule that "a count without one queryable source" is a defect (`BASELINE-visionflow.md:317`) is unenforced precisely when the estate is changing fastest.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE note in VF-02.8; ADR-2005 'partial' axis note)

### F-11 — Night verdicts live only in a human's working tree: the ledger can silently lose rows, absence is unrecordable, and the engine runs on one annexe
- Topics: VF-04.8, VF-04 (Debt), VF-04.5
- Evidence: "the engine writes each night's row to the working tree and never commits it; the 09-27 to 09-30 rows reached main only in a human commit (`f3d54a0`)... the 09-14 to 09-26 silence cannot be told apart from rows that were written and lost" (`docs/dream-cycle/LEDGER.md:29-34`); "No row at all for 09-14 through 09-26 — thirteen nights with no verdict of any kind" (`LEDGER.md:28-29`); "the ledger has no row for 10-12, 10-16, 10-17, 10-19 or 10-22... no mechanism in the ledger records why a night is absent"; the engine runs on "the HP annexe" (VF-04.5), whose environment faults produced the BLOCKED-ENV rows (`LEDGER.md:27`, `:30`, `:41`).
- Failure: the annexe dies for two weeks (as the 09-14 silence suggests once happened); no alarm fires anywhere because a missing row is indistinguishable from a quiet fortnight, and a crash mid-night loses that row outright. The audit trail the human merge gate depends on has an unprovable hole.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (GAP block in VF-04.8 and two Debt markers in VF-04)

### F-12 — The gates that police the canon's graded claims run only on whichever laptop has the sibling checkouts
- Topics: VF-05.11, VF-05.13, VF-05.7
- Evidence: "run-all.sh itself is wired to NO workflow — it is reachable only through package.json's test:gates / verify scripts" (VF-05.11 CI3); the augmentation-citations gate "resolves every citation against SIBLING checkouts, so it can only ever run locally... the suite silently skips the real-checkout assertion on a hosted runner — the matrix's cells are checked by whoever remembers to run it" (`tests/gates/augmentation-citations.test.cjs:77`); harness-audit omits `--strict-sources` because "strict would fail for the environment, not the templates" (`.github/workflows/harness-fitness-gates.yml:53`).
- Failure: a cell of the graded augmentation matrix is raised to `measured` on a citation that later rots (file moved, line drifts); no CI run ever re-checks it, the published grade overstates its evidence, and by ADR-2010 that is a governance DEFECT the canon says is machine-checked (`BASELINE-visionflow.md:217`) — but only locally, by hand.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in VF-05.13 and CI3, plus Debt lines)

### F-13 — The fixture-parity gate is structurally unable to pass in CI, training waiver culture on exactly the check it exists to perform
- Topics: VF-05.5, VF-05.6
- Evidence: "option (a), wiring sibling checkouts with a deploy key, is blocked on credentials that cannot be provisioned from the tree — so every fixture-touching PR fails this gate deliberately" (`.github/workflows/fixture-drift.yml:19`); until 2026-09-05 "the locate step always set has_canonical=false and the job exited 0 through a 'No fixtures to check' branch" (`fixture-drift.yml:5`); the comparator's unqualified default targets a path "REMOVED" on 2026-06-29, so unqualified runs "exit 2, never 0" (`scripts/check-fixture-drift.sh:82-93`).
- Failure: contributors learn that fixture-drift red is ambient noise; the `waive_reason` path becomes routine, and genuine fixture drift — the thing `released`/`match` manifests cite — reaches a release through a waived or never-run comparison.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the always-fail state and the waiver mechanism are stated; the behavioural drift is reasoned
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE notes in VF-05.5; DOC-DRIFT in VF-05.6)

### F-14 — The accepted record for the corpus contradicts the baseline on who builds it, and the one-corpus invariant has no detector
- Topics: VF-01.15
- Evidence: ADR-2013 D3: "VisionClaw/crates/vault is the only implementation that parses, reasons over or builds the corpus" (`docs/adr/ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:52`), while the baseline's 2026-10-12 note "records Ontomark as the producer of the shared build and VisionClaw as its consumer with its own publish build job retired behind gates" (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:53`); "ADR-2013 has not been amended". Invariant 8 makes equal class counts across Loom/VisionClaw/vault-build mandatory — "a divergence is a defect" (`BASELINE-visionflow.md:327-340`) — yet "nothing in this canon repo currently polls the three counts to detect one", and the record sits `staged` pending a comparison nobody runs (`ADR-2013...md:12`).
- Failure: an operator follows ADR-2013 as the accepted decision and treats VisionClaw's build as the single source; Ontomark actually produces the shared build, the two drift, and the mandated equality check never fires — the estate holds two answers to "how many classes" with no alarm.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension and Open markers in VF-01.15)

### F-15 — The copyright guard watches one directory level of one file extension while the repo publicly handles copyrighted source
- Topics: VF-05.9
- Evidence: "the guard polices only ROOT-LEVEL *.txt by regex. A copyrighted .txt committed one directory down passes, and texput.log at the repo root is a .log, outside the pattern entirely" (VF-05.9 NOTE); the guard exists because "the book's transition chapter reads copyrighted sources that must never enter this PUBLIC repo" (`.github/workflows/copyright-guard.yml:3`); separately, "until a root licence file is added, repo-local docs and website assets need explicit maintainer confirmation before reuse outside the ecosystem" (`LICENSES/README.md:3`, `:5`) and "this checkout has no root licence grant" (`./README.md:163`).
- Failure: a copyrighted excerpt lands under any subdirectory or as `.md`/`.log` and publishes to the public site through every gate; the one control whose stated purpose is exactly this leak misses it, and the absent root licence compounds downstream reuse exposure into a legal incident.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE note in VF-05.9; licence condition stated in LICENSES/README.md)

## Not judgeable from this material

- **Sidestr key management.** Where the single signer's key lives (HSM, disk, host), its backup and rotation, and any freeze/recovery procedure — ADR-2012 P5 states the custody model but nothing about key custody itself.
- **Contact delivery semantics.** Whether the DreamLab relay is estate-operated, its retention, and whether `contact.js` checks any publish acknowledgement before showing success to the user; the material stops at "publishes to the relay, nothing is stored".
- **Sibling execution behaviour.** All substrate citations are path:line without the code: whether the NIP-98 event's `created_at` bounds the replay window, what `plan_fanout` actually does when connectors are deferred, and whether the broker's journalled fast path loses in-flight decisions on crash.
- **The dream engine's own gates.** AB-23 is external: what the engine does with a missing sentinel line, whether it holds push credentials, and what its BLOCKED-ENV classification actually verifies — the repo-side material only shows the config it reads.
- **Access control around the gates.** Branch protection on main, who can edit `allowlist.json`'s `source_pin` or the workflows, and the scope of `ESTATE_READ_TOKEN` — the material shows gate logic and two workflow permission blocks, but not who can change them.

## visionflow / premortem

Post-mortem, written against the diagrams-as-code corpus at its declared revisions (`visionflow/01`–`08`, verified_commit `4d361b5…` and the per-repo map in VF-08). No source beyond what the corpus cites was available; every claim below carries the citation the corpus gives. Where I reason past the material I say "inferred".

### R-1 — Client funds settled on a custodial, single-signer sidechain whose hardening path was never reached
- Topics: VF-01.14, VF-01.11
- Chain of events: the estate's only financial substrate is DreamLab's own sidestr sidechains; the root chain is run at level 1 with one signer operated by DreamLab; a client is settled onto it (or onto the `sidestr:dreamlab` artifact) while the k-of-n federation remains "rung 2 of the hardening path, not reached"; the single signer's key is lost, compromised, or used to double-spend against a client; client funds are gone or contested, and the canon cannot produce the deployment receipt its own invariant demands before an activation claim.
- Evidence: the settlement record states custody plainly — root chain LEVEL 1, one signer, CUSTODIAL, k-of-n not reached (`docs/adr/ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:84-87`); the record sits at proposed / partial / inactive (`ADR-2012…md:5-7`) and its source qualification "promotes implementation only" (`ADR-2012…md:129-130`); BASELINE requires source, publication and activation to be separate claims, with activation needing the owning substrate's deployment receipt (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:319-321`); no regulatory relief is claimed (`ADR-2012…md:91`); the qualification itself records that agentbox "still produces sidestr:dreamlab with upstream JavaScript and no overlay is activated" (`ADR-2012…md:113-128`) — a labeled artifact that is not what its label implies; the compatibility matrix still carries no settlement row (`ADR-2012…md:159-160`).
- Warning signs visible today: the public page claimed Lightning and NWC rails that did not exist until an owner decision corrected it to "testnet research only" (`website/static/index.html:630`, `:1125`; disposition `ADR-2012…md:169`) — the estate has already once published settlement capability it did not have; the custody exposure is recorded but unmitigated and unratified.
- Confidence: medium — the custody exposure is stated; production client use twelve months on is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — A captured NIP-98 credential replayed across a restart or replica bypasses verification on pod and forum surfaces
- Topics: VF-08.2, VF-08.7
- Chain of events: a signed NIP-98 request is captured (relay transit, log, or client compromise); the VisionClaw verifier's single-use cache is process-local, so after a process restart or at another replica the same event id is unknown; the replayed request validates again; the attacker acts on client data or governance surfaces as the original principal; no replica-wide replay store exists to detect it.
- Evidence: the cache is "process-local single-use … the event id is recorded atomically after validation" (`../project/src/utils/nip98.rs:164`) and is "declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself" (`nip98.rs:210`, VC-03); the corpus states the boundary — "Restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring require separate proof" (VF-08.2); pod compatibility is a consumer contract with per-tier replay-store semantics, and "Agentbox's supervised native server must not be relabelled as an in-process embedded library" (VF-08.7).
- Warning signs visible today: the limitation is declared in the substrate's own code as an invariant, and no estate-level test binds replicas or restarts — nothing in this corpus shows one.
- Confidence: high that the defect exists as described; medium, inferred, that it is this incident's cause.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The Jev advisory path ships private sibling and corpus content to a third-party model backend from local runs
- Topics: VF-05.14, VF-06.15, VF-03 (Jev stamp)
- Chain of events: the 2026-10-30 System One unification makes the Jev call-outs no longer local-only by default; agentbox projects `VISIONFLOW_JEV=all` into service and shell environments once the new image activates; a local gate or collector run with Jev enabled sends excerpts resolved from sibling checkouts — the diagram corpus cites sources across sixteen repositories including the private `jjohare/visionGraph` vault — to the shared proxy's selected backend (TypeSafe); the only control is an opt-out environment variable; private corpus lines and paths leave the estate with no measured benefit, since the option's items are "proposed research candidate[s] with no measured uplift".
- Evidence: `scripts/check-augmentation-citations.cjs:13-16`, `:27`, `:96-106` send excerpts of sibling checkouts unless `VISIONFLOW_JEV_CLAIMS_LOCAL_ONLY=1` pins local; `docs/jev-option.md:12` and `:16` (hosted CI and the dream annexe inherit nothing — the exposure is local and agentbox shells); `scripts/diagram-index-gen.cjs:815-818` sends the cited lines per resolved citation; `diagram-index.yml:30` — diagram sources reach sixteen repositories at forty-one declared revisions, "two of them unfetchable here (a private repo, a local-only clone)"; the "public data" argument is made only for the two estate-health JEV items (`docs/jev-option.md:13-14`), never for corpus citations; unfitted thresholds at `docs/jev-option.md:5` and `scripts/lib/jev.cjs:41-42`.
- Warning signs visible today: the design is opt-out rather than opt-in, is default-on wherever agentbox projects it, and the public-data justification covers a different path than the one that reads sibling checkouts.
- Confidence: medium — the sending behavior is stated; that private-vault lines ride the citation path is inferred from the resolver's scope.
- Severity: high

### F-01 — The corpus class-count divergence that Invariant 8 calls a defect has no detector
- Topics: VF-01.15
- Evidence: ADR-2013's `review_trigger` names "the first divergence between Loom's, VisionClaw's and vault build's class counts" (`docs/adr/ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:12`), and BASELINE Invariant 8 makes equal counts a hard requirement (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:327-340`, `:335-336`); the corpus itself states "nothing in this canon repo currently polls the three counts to detect one"; the original failure mode was exactly this — Loom serving 8,146 from a stale bundle while others disagreed (`ADR-2013…md:22-26`).
- Failure: one build path drifts (stale bundle, partial publish) and serves wrong or stale ontology to clients; the divergence the estate defines as a defect ships silently.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — ADR-2013 still names VisionClaw as the only corpus parser while the baseline records Ontomark as the producer
- Topics: VF-01.15
- Evidence: ADR-2013 D3 — "`VisionClaw/crates/vault` is the only implementation that parses, reasons over or builds the corpus" (`docs/adr/ADR-2013…md:52`) versus the baseline's 2026-10-12 note recording Ontomark as producer of the shared build and VisionClaw as consumer with its own publish build retired (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:53`); "ADR-2013 has not been amended".
- Failure: someone enforces the unamended record — deleting the "duplicate" parser/build job — and removes the actual producer of the corpus build; publishing breaks or silently forks.
- Confidence: high that the conflict exists; medium, inferred, for the deletion scenario.
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — The dream engine has already fabricated verification evidence, and nothing mechanical stops a recurrence
- Topics: VF-04.8, VF-04.9, VF-04.6
- Evidence: the 2026-09-07 operator audit found reports claiming candidate branches and PRs that never existed, patches targeting a path that never existed, and on-disk receipts contradicting reported counters (`docs/dream-cycle/LEDGER.md:20`); reports cited ADR-0057, ADR-056 and ADR-2024, none of which exist (`dream.config.json:73`); "nothing in this repo enforces the draft-PR shape" (VF-04.9 note).
- Failure: a future night fabricates a passing receipt or candidate; a human merges on trust; unverified or wrong content lands on the published surface with ledger evidence that looks genuine.
- Confidence: medium (recurrence inferred; the past fabrication is recorded fact)
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: high

### F-04 — A release manifest can say `released`/`match` while comparing nothing that binds the pinned revision
- Topics: VF-07.3
- Evidence: the generator verifies the commit exists, then hashes the current working-tree directory rather than that commit's tree (`scripts/generate-release-manifest.sh:179`, `:194`) — a probe emitted the same canonical revision with different corpus hashes after an uncommitted edit; only checksum mismatches fail the comparator, so a probe returned `released`/`match` with zero matched and two missing consumer fixtures (`scripts/check-fixture-drift.sh:257`; `docs/estate-review/2026-10-31-release-evidence.md`).
- Failure: a release ships asserting fixture parity against a revision whose tree was never read; a cross-repo protocol divergence passes as compared and matched.
- Confidence: high (reproduced by probe)
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — The fixture-drift gate cannot ever pass in CI, training the team to waive it
- Topics: VF-05.5
- Evidence: "option (a), wiring sibling checkouts with a deploy key, is blocked on credentials that cannot be provisioned from the tree — so every fixture-touching PR fails this gate deliberately" (`.github/workflows/fixture-drift.yml:19`), with a `waive_reason` path that records only a warning and exits 0 (`fixture-drift.yml:137`).
- Failure: waiver becomes routine; a genuine fixture drift between substrates is waived alongside the noise and a breaking schema change ships.

- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
