### F-06 — The estate-health checker can return OK without measuring anything
- Topics: VF-03.6
- Evidence: a schema tag and current timestamp with no repository/surface arrays returns `ESTATE-HEALTH-OK`; a 2099 timestamp also returns OK — consequences of the optional empty-array scans and one-sided age test (`scripts/estate-health.mjs:967`, `:984`, `:1012`); "The literal verdict is not proof that the full roster was measured" (2026-10-31 debt note).
- Failure: a truncated or corrupted snapshot scans green; the operator's offline verdict and the dream cycle both read health that was never collected, masking an ongoing estate failure.
- Confidence: high (behavior reproduced); the operational consequence is medium.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-07 — One page of runs makes busy repositories read "none" instead of their real CI state
- Topics: VF-03.4
- Evidence: since judgement call 8 the collector reads one unfiltered page of 100 runs and filters client-side (`scripts/estate-health.mjs:609`, `:614`); "a repository whose pull-request and feature-branch runs fill that page pushes its default-branch runs off it, and the row then reads none".
- Failure: a substrate's default branch goes red during heavy PR activity; nightly health shows "no run on head" rather than red; a real failure is invisible for nights.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — A repository can move from red to "exempt" without ever passing a run, and nothing records the flip
- Topics: VF-03.11
- Evidence: knowledgeGraph "moved from red to exempt by rule rather than by a passing run" — its last run failed on an older commit, the HEAD rule reports none, `ci_by_design` converts none to exempt (`website/static/data/estate-health.json:480`, `:500-501`); `check()` has no branch for exempt (`scripts/estate-health.mjs:968`, `:978`); "no mechanism records why a row flips" (`estate-health.json:11`).
- Failure: a published surface sits permanently outside both the red verdict and the exempt audit trail; its failures never surface anywhere.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — The one private, cross-owner repository is unreadable every night and the local trap hides it
- Topics: VF-03.5
- Evidence: `jjohare/visionGraph` returns 404 to the workflow's repo-scoped token without an `ESTATE_READ_TOKEN` secret (`.github/workflows/estate-health.yml:86`); confirmed live — `summary.unreadable is 1` (`estate-health.json:16`); the recorded TRAP: a maintainer's full-scope local PAT reads it anyway, "so a readable row in a locally produced snapshot is NOT evidence that the CI path works" (`estate-health.yml:92`).
- Failure: the vault's health is never actually monitored in CI; a dead or diverging private corpus is indistinguishable from an unconfigured token, indefinitely.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TRAP)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — The publication path has zero runtime checks; `esc()` is the single defense for attacker-influenced GitHub strings
- Topics: VF-02.11, VF-02.6, VF-03.9
- Evidence: "The publication path has no browser gate at all — every blocking gate in VF-02.6 is browser-free by design"; `website-browser-check.mjs` "is run by hand, never by deploy.yml"; the site renders snapshot data through `esc()` and an href allowlist as the only sanitization (`website/static/js/main.js:620`), and the snapshot carries arbitrary text including "first line of the message" of head commits (`scripts/estate-health.mjs:588`); a11y/perf specs run only against the external sidecar, by hand (VF-02.10).
- Failure: a regression in `esc()` (or a crafted commit title / repo field) injects markup or script into the public page; no deploy gate executes the page, so it ships.
- Confidence: medium; the injection chain is inferred — the no-browser-gate fact is stated.
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence recorded for the missing browser gate; the injection chain is not)
- Severity: high

### F-11 — Self-description counts on the live page are ungated, and the drift counter fails open on the publication path
- Topics: VF-02.8, VF-05.3, VF-05.4
- Evidence: without the agentbox checkout "this axis is never actually measured on the publication path, yet the page still ships" (VF-02.8 divergence; `deploy.yml:139`); ADR-2005 is implementation-partial with "2 of 4 axes unenforced" (`ADR-2005…md:46`); the page asserts 83 CUDA kernels / 6,302 lines with "no CUDA axis, so the page's figures rest on review, not on a gate" (VF-02 invariants note; `website/static/index.html:296`, `:1089`); an allowlist entry polices only what is listed — "absence = invisible" (`scripts/drift-counter/README.md:52`).
- Failure: a sibling changes and the marketing page keeps stale public figures about it; a client contracts against numbers nothing checks.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence; ADR-2005 partial note)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — Retirements that are not supersessions are invisible in the generated ADR index
- Topics: VF-01.6, VF-01.10
- Evidence: the reciprocity walk is warnings-only and "a one-sided supersession still passes the gate" (`scripts/adr-index-gen.cjs:144`, `:146`); today every row shows an em-dash so the edge case is "untested in practice" (`docs/adr/README.md:31`); ADR-2012 and ADR-2013 "each retire prior canon claims in prose rather than by supersession" (`ADR-2012…md:8`, `ADR-2013…md:8`), and "nothing states how a retirement that is not a supersession is represented in the generated index" (VF-01.10 Open).
- Failure: a reader resolves a claim through the index, lands on a record the index still presents as live, and builds against a retired decision (e.g. the retired SHA-256d anchoring note).
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence and Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — A federated decision can be acknowledged by a relay but never applied, and no proof closes the loop
- Topics: VF-08.4
- Evidence: the broker pipeline ends at stages "signed / accepted / projected / received / applied" with "Complete correlated applied-decision proof remains open"; "Relay acknowledgement does not prove external application"; fan-out is planned but the outbound connector and accept-path joins are deferred, gated by `MESH_FEDERATED_KINDS` (`mesh.rs:59`, `plan_fanout` at `mesh.rs:185`).
- Failure: a governance or corpus decision (demotion, precedent, settlement instruction) is believed applied estate-wide because a relay accepted it; one substrate never applies it and serves superseded state.
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (stated open in the panel)
- Severity: high

### F-14 — The estate is enumerated three times with three different memberships, and the rosters do not reference each other
- Topics: VF-03.1, VF-07.2
- Evidence: the health roster carries 18 repositories (`scripts/estate-health/roster.json:5-22`); the release roster is 16 (`scripts/generate-release-manifest.sh:91`, `:106-107`) with "loom, knowledgeGraph, WasmVOWL, visionGraph, dream-machine, logseq, ruvector and RuView could each move under a release with no record"; `repository-map.md:8` lists twelve, names the roster authoritative for nightly collection only (`:25`) and scopes six as supporting (`:29-37`); "The roster still does not reference the map back"; the dream-engine row is stamped before the extraction and the fork was renamed and archived (VF-03 drift, 2026-10-15).
- Failure: a release manifest presents a coordinated view of part of the estate as the whole — exactly what manifest v1 "silently shipped" (`ecosystem-release.schema.json:14` note) — and a changed repository outside the release roster moves unrecorded.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (defect note and drift entries)
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — The public contact form pushes client messages through a vendored, minified transport that no gate inspects
- Topics: VF-02.3, VF-02.4
- Evidence: the form "gift-wraps the message (NIP-17) in the browser and publishes it to the DreamLab relay, nothing is stored by this site" (`website/static/js/contact.js:5-6`, `:112`); the transport is "a 57 KB vendored minified transport, rebuilt in dreamlab-ai-website and committed here, not compiled here" (`website/static/js/nostr-contact.js:1-2`); the asset gate checks only presence and non-zero bytes (`scripts/website-assets.mjs:177`, `:250`) — nothing pins the blob's content to a reviewed source, and no publication gate executes the page (see F-10).
- Failure: a drifted or compromised rebuild of the minified transport ships through every gate and exfiltrates or misdirects client contact submissions; or a mis-keyed wrap publishes message content/metadata to a shared relay.
- Confidence: low-to-medium; inferred — the material does not state the relay's publicity or the wrap's key handling, only the absence of any content-level check.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

## visiontouch-rs / critical

Ranked by production damage. Marker vocabulary (DEBT, DIVERGENCE, DRIFT, OPEN, TENSION, POLICY) is the authors' own.

### F-01 — No path stops force output if the host freezes, the ioctl blocks, or the process is killed
- Topics: VT-02.1, VT-02.5, VT-03.4
- Evidence: The EPP transfer checks "a deadline and a 1000-poll limit, assuming each register ioctl returns… It is not an independent deadline on a blocked ioctl or a guarantee about kernel scheduling" (device.rs:102-109, device.rs:121-125). "Expiry is only evaluated when the worker runs, there is no hardware watchdog" (crates/device/src/lib.rs:59-60, 12-force-feedback.md:91-92, marked DEBT). Neutral-on-exit exists only in-process: "close or Drop attempts shutdown, then release" (device.rs:357-364); SIGKILL runs no Drop, and the business text concedes "It cannot protect against the computer itself freezing or the process being killed, because no timer lives in the hardware that anyone has established."
- Failure: During any force session, a scheduler stall, a stuck ppdev ioctl, or an OOM kill leaves the last DAC offsets latched in the arm with nothing — software timeout, Drop, or device — to zero them. The operator must physically intervene.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially (DEBT on the missing watchdog; the kill/blocked-ioctl consequence is stated only in prose, untagged)

### F-02 — Delivered force has never been physically established; every newton in the system is an unverified model command
- Topics: VT-03.1, VT-03.2, VT-08 (intro), VT-08.2
- Evidence: "ADR-0003 is accepted and complete but its activation is staged until an owner session exists, so the full force ladder has simulation evidence rather than physical acceptance. Existing owner-host input and liveness traces are distinct from delivered-force calibration" (VT-08 intro, staged/OPEN). "Three powered sessions on 2026-10-16 produced no perceptible force" (VT-08.2). The gains are "a black-box fit, physical accuracy is unestablished" (12-force-feedback.md:35-41), and even the SDK ceiling is gain-dependent: "134 counts per newton gain gives 268 counts at 2 N so 300 is the only bound independent of the uncertain gains" (12-force-feedback.md:64-66).
- Failure: First real production session commands newtons on a scale that has never delivered a measured force. Over-delivery, zero delivery, or wrong-axis delivery are all consistent with everything the repository contains.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN/staged, repeated "model commands, not measured forces")

### F-03 — The attempts that fault are the attempts whose evidence is discarded, and surviving records can claim output is off when it is not
- Topics: VT-08 (intro), VT-08.1, VT-08.5
- Evidence: "On a normal controller stop, the worker requests zero, attempts shutdown and then writes evidence. Disconnect and transport-fault paths instead discard the running attempt" (VT-08 intro). DRIFT note: "disconnect and fault discard running attempt, shutdown errors can still end with 'Output is off' text" (worker.rs:433-451).
- Failure: A transport fault mid-force — precisely the event an incident review needs — leaves no attempt JSON or CSV, and the records that are written can assert "Output is off" after a failed shutdown. The evidence base is systematically blind to its worst cases.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially (DRIFT covers the evidence text; the discard-on-fault behaviour is stated untagged)

### F-04 — Every safety verdict, including the Over lock, is erased by any reconnect — and any single transport error forces one
- Topics: VT-08.2, VT-02.3, VT-03.4, VT-03.2
- Evidence: Over is "sticky for the connection" (protocol.rs:783-790) but "only a reconnect clears Over: Session::reconnect forgets everything" (protocol.rs:528-530); N5 confirms ADR, spec and code agree the lock is connection-scoped. Meanwhile "any failed transaction marks the connection unhealthy for good" (device.rs:246-248) and "no recoverable force fault, every sampling error ends the session and needs a reconnect" (crates/device/src/lib.rs:526-528). Registration is also lost on reconnect (crates/device/src/lib.rs:460-466).
- Failure: An over-delivery verdict (the system's only backstop against miscalibrated gains) is escaped by unplugging and replugging — deliberately or after any port glitch. In production with unverified gains (F-02), an operator can cycle through repeated full ladder climbs, each re-exposing them to the same over-delivery. A noisy port also means constant re-registration ceremonies.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; the incident chain is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the per-connection scope is stated as an invariant, not flagged as a hazard)

### F-05 — A failed shutdown destroys the ability to ever send neutral packets on that connection
- Topics: VT-02.4, VT-02.5
- Evidence: "end_force_session clears the pending sequence even when shutdown failed, so Drop cannot resend neutral packets, it only retries the port release" (device.rs:345, device.rs:326-328, DEBT). "A failed shutdown cannot be recovered in place" (VT-02.5). Whether a new connection's zero handshake neutralises a half-shut-down device is nowhere established.
- Failure: The two-packet shutdown is interrupted after the first packet; the device may remain armed with the last offsets, no code path on this connection can send neutral, and the reconnect path's effect on device-side motor state is unknown.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; the device-side consequence is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-06 — ADR-0002 relies on a continuous-force cap that no code enforces
- Topics: VT-03.1, VT-03 (intro), VT-04, VT-08.3
- Evidence: "CONTINUOUS_FORCE_N is documented as the cap for content and ADR-0002 relies on it (ADR-0002…:38) but no code reads the constant, only crates/device/src/lib.rs:50 mentions it" (DEBT). The SDK states "The continuous-force policy and dwell budget belong to the application." Only the Studio controller accrues a budget (controller.rs:519-527), and only per attempt; Mass Lab inherits ForceLimits::default and holds force indefinitely (VT-04).
- Failure: An accepted, "live" decision record promises a continuous-force limit the platform does not implement. Any consumer other than the Studio controller — Mass Lab today, a future VisionClaw bridge — can apply sustained force for minutes with no budget, at a magnitude whose delivery is unverified (F-02).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-07 — The clean-room policy is documented as violated by its own logs, and the mandatory legal re-opening never happened
- Topics: VT-01.1, VT-01.2, VT-01.3, VT-01.4
- Evidence: PROVENANCE.md:20 requires "decompilation by the spec team never the implementer", yet both D-0001 and D-0002 "name a coding agent in the specification role" (DECOMPILATION-LOG.md:42, :82, DIVERGENCE). Trigger T3 "any decompilation of a vendor binary" (POSITION.md:46) "has fired twice… but the position document is unchanged" (DIVERGENCE). The ladder itself is "enforced by convention and commit order, not by CI: only the 'no vendor artefact tracked' step is mechanical", and DESIGN is used on 24 rows while absent from the class table (OPEN).
- Failure: Any move toward release, sale, or outside demonstration — which taking this to production may itself be (POSITION.md:44-47) — proceeds on a clean-room defence the repository's own logs undercut, without the review its own trigger rules require.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE and OPEN)

### F-08 — Registration passes with up to 25% scale error because the ruler scale check only warns
- Topics: VT-08.4, VT-08.2
- Evidence: "sign and off-axis within 15 percent stop the line, scale 0.8 to 1.25 only warns" (registration.rs:18-20, registration.rs:84-87); "Checks::complete unlocks sign pulses" (registration.rs:196-198). No marker tags the warn-only scale rule.
- Failure: The arm is registered with gains off by +25%, sign pulses and the full force ladder unlock anyway, and commanded-versus-delivered mismatch propagates into every rung. The only backstop is the operator-typed Over gauge reading — itself connection-scoped (F-04).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — On Wayland, focus loss defeats the operator dead-man switch while the heartbeat keeps the attempt alive
- Topics: VT-08.1
- Evidence: "on Wayland a held Space can stay latched after focus loss because no key-up or focus event arrives, only the 250 ms heartbeat and Stop remain" (main.rs:323-330, DEBT). The heartbeat is bumped by the UI thread every frame (main.rs:214-215), which focus loss does not stop.
- Failure: During a 2.0 N rung, the operator clicks away or loses focus: the hold remains latched, the heartbeat stays fresh, and the powered attempt continues without anyone holding the key until the rung's time bound or a Stop.
- Confidence: high (inferred only in that the UI keeps rendering after focus loss)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-10 — The thermal attempt removes the hold interlock entirely: release is not a stop for up to 660 seconds
- Topics: VT-08.3, VT-08.2
- Evidence: "a thermal attempt has no hold, only a Confirm every 30 s with a 10 s grace, so release is no longer a stop and the arm relies on Confirm and the 660 s bound" (controller.rs:26-28, controller.rs:61, DEBT); thermal needs the 1.0 N rung and runs capped at 1.0 N (protocol.rs:684-692, README.md:58).
- Failure: The longest powered attempt in the system is the one where letting go of everything does nothing; a distracted or incapacitated operator is protected only by periodic Confirms and an 11-minute bound, at a commanded force whose delivery is unverified (F-02).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-11 — Evidence-integrity tokens are not bound to reality: OutputOff wraps a caller Boolean, and record commit identity comes from the working directory
- Topics: VT-08 (intro), VT-08.5
- Evidence: "OutputOff checks a supplied Boolean and can be retained after rearming; it does not enforce device state through its type" (VT-08 intro); "the Boolean token can be retained after rearming, normal ordering depends on worker control flow" (evidence.rs:24-34, worker.rs:495, DEBT). Separately, "software.commit comes from git rev-parse HEAD in the process working directory so another checkout yields the wrong software identity" (evidence.rs:534-544, OPEN).
- Failure: After a fault and rearm, a writer holding a stale OutputOff records an attempt as output-off while the device is armed; run from any checkout other than the canonical one, every attempt record cites the wrong commit. The verification record — the system's only force evidence — can be silently wrong about both safety state and software identity.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT and OPEN)

### F-12 — The observation evidence chain can fail silently, and nothing in the gate would notice
- Topics: VT-06.2, VT-06.4, VT-01 (intro)
- Evidence: "a failed log write only prints a diagnostic and the run carries on with an incomplete capture" (tools/port-recorder/src/lib.rs:279-281, DEBT). "The recorder shim is meant to stay test-covered yet its check.py is run by hand and no gate step runs it" (AGENTS.md:53, README.md:11 vs quality-gate.sh:35, DEBT). Untagged prose: "on 1 November its memory snapshots returned EPERM in the audit container, despite a green quality gate."
- Failure: The OBSERVE-class captures underlying the provenance rows and specs (VT-01's ladder, VT-02's setup bytes) can be truncated or empty, the run reports success, and the gate stays green. Provenance rows then cite evidence that does not contain what the spec claims.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (DEBT on the two code facts; the EPERM-despite-green-gate gap is untagged prose)

### F-13 — The launcher contradicts the stated security policy: it permits running the preloaded reference software as root
- Topics: VT-06.3
- Evidence: POLICY node: "do not preload reference software as root, loader variables are set after the launcher" (README.md:34-35). But "Its direct-exec branch runs before the root check, so the script does not enforce a universal prohibition on running as root" (scripts/with-port-access:10-17).
- Failure: A root-invoked session (operator habit, recovery, automation) bypasses the intended guard, and with the LD_PRELOAD recorder in the environment the shim runs inside the reference software with root privileges — exactly what the policy exists to prevent.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (POLICY marker states the rule; the script's failure to enforce it is untagged prose)

### F-14 — Sample freshness is not verified: a wedged or echoing device is indistinguishable from a live one
- Topics: VT-02.3
- Evidence: The sample path "checks sequence echo and ready bit, not a fresh-sample counter" (12-force-feedback.md:137-139), and "Sampling twice without a new write can pass when the device returns the same expected echo" (VT-02.3 prose). No marker flags this as a hazard.
- Failure: A device that stops updating but keeps returning the cached packet is accepted as a stream of fresh samples. EncoderState, registration, and every position-derived interlock (speed, excursion, lift-off) then operate on frozen data while force demands continue against it.
- Confidence: medium (the limitation is stated; the device misbehaviour is an inferred scenario)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Counter unwrapping assumes slow motion and nothing checks it, so fast motion corrupts position silently
- Topics: VT-03.2, VT-08.3
- Evidence: "unwrapping assumes less than half a counter revolution between samples, nothing checks it" (crates/device/src/lib.rs:196-197, DEBT). Position feeds the force Jacobian (crates/device/src/lib.rs:276-285) and the Studio's speed and excursion interlocks (controller.rs:265-289, controller.rs:501-505); the guard caps force magnitude but not direction (crates/device/src/lib.rs:410-412).
- Failure: A rapid operator motion, or a scheduling hiccup just under the 25 ms gap bound, wraps a counter; the computed position jumps, the spring force points the wrong way at full capped magnitude, and excursion/speed stops judge a fictitious pose.
- Confidence: medium (the assumption is stated; the wrap rate is not derivable from the material)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether the arm has ever physically delivered any commanded force, and the true counts-to-newton gain — the first attended gauge session does not exist in this material; everything rests on it.
2. Device-side behaviour on host crash, port close, or partial shutdown: whether the arm itself neutralises offsets or holds them; no source establishes any device-side timer or reset.
3. Thread-safety and concurrency of `Device` when the local control socket path, the servo worker, and the UI overlap, and what happens to control jobs when the 8-deep queue's 1 s deadlines expire.
4. Whether `Server::start` recovers from a stale socket file after a crash, given "an existing socket is never unlinked" (control.rs:167-169).
5. Which gate steps actually pass on the owner host versus CI ("syscall policy and dependencies can differ"), and the current state of the hand-run `check.py` coverage for the recorder shim.

## visiontouch-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem: client loss through a data / custody / security failure

Scope note for the checking engineer: every claim below cites the distillation's own anchors at the pinned revisions (visiontouch-rs `7b95fb2…`; the five VisionClaw files byte-identical at `75b72416` at the 1 November review). Where I reason past what the material states, the finding says "inferred". Nothing below relies on an ordering or concurrency the diagrams do not assert.

---

### R-1 — Attended evidence is discarded on any transport fault, and nothing on disk ever represented the attempt
- Topics: VT-02.3, VT-02.5, VT-03.4, VT-08 (intro), VT-08.1, VT-08.2, VT-08.5
- Chain of events:
  1. The owner runs an attended sitting; a long attempt is in flight (the thermal class bound is 660 s, controller.rs:61; README.md:58).
  2. One packet or sample exchange fails. The HAL marks the connection unhealthy permanently (device.rs:246-248) and the SDK ends the session with no automatic rearm (device.rs:255-259; 12-force-feedback.md:30-31).
  3. The worker takes the disconnect/transport-fault path, which **discards the running attempt** instead of writing it (VT-08 developer intro: "Disconnect and transport-fault paths instead discard the running attempt"; worker.rs:433-451).
  4. Nothing existed on disk before that moment: the only CSV/JSON write happens in `Engine::finish` after scoring (worker.rs:990-994; evidence.rs:489), so the attempt's telemetry lived only in memory.
  5. The interface can still end with "Output is off" text on a shutdown error (worker.rs:433-451, DRIFT note), so the operator has no on-screen signal that the record is gone.
  6. `end_force_session` cleared the pending sequence even though shutdown failed (device.rs:345; device.rs:326-328), so Drop cannot resend neutral packets, only retry the port release.
  7. The operator reconnects; `Session::reconnect` forgets every verdict (protocol.rs:528-530) and the new `EncoderState` clears registration (crates/device/src/lib.rs:460-466; ADR-0004:36-37). The sitting restarts from the bottom.
  8. The evidence folder delivered against the engagement lacks the sitting; the promised figures cannot be substantiated and the client is lost.
- Evidence: VT-08 intro sentence on discard; VT-08.1 note "DRIFT: disconnect and fault discard running attempt, shutdown errors can still end with Output is off text worker.rs:433-451"; worker.rs:990-994 (single write at finish); device.rs:246-248 and device.rs:255-259 (permanent unhealthy, no rearm); device.rs:345/326-328 (pending sequence cleared on failed shutdown); protocol.rs:528-530 (reconnect forgets); VT-02.5 ("A failed shutdown cannot be recovered in place… the operator reconnects").
- Warning signs visible today: the discard-on-fault behaviour is stated design, not an accident; the mislabelled "Output is off" text is a marked DRIFT; the failed-shutdown sequence-clearing is a marked DEBT; the reconnect-forgets-everything rule is stated in three places. Together these say: one parallel-port hiccup mid-sitting destroys the sitting's record and its unlock state, with no partial evidence ever written.
- Confidence: high that the loss path exists exactly as described (every step is stated); that this was the incident's mechanism is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The VisionClaw input bridge lands on an unauthenticated loopback seam that ADR-2017 explicitly deferred
- Topics: VT-07.2, VT-07.3, VT-07.4, VT-08.7
- Chain of events:
  1. The documented integration is a pose-and-button stream from a local bridge on the device host to the web or XR client "over loopback, never via the server" (ADR-2017:31).
  2. ADR-2017 leaves bridge authentication "to a future VisionClaw record" (ADR-2017:39, OPEN) — no token, origin or handshake is specified anywhere in the estate.
  3. The bridge is built following the estate's existing local-trust posture: "the socket is not a boundary against code already running as the owner, same-user clients have the trust of the local interface" (17-local-control.md:30).
  4. The VisionClaw side needs no new message type: `nodeDragStart`/`nodeDragUpdate`/`nodeDragEnd` flow through the existing handlers into server position-update routing (useGraphEventHandlers.ts:150, :64, :255; message_routing.rs:52-60).
  5. A page open in the operator's browser, or any local process, connects to the unauthenticated loopback endpoint and emits drag messages; the client's graph is mutated and the mutations propagate through the server routing.
  6. During a client session nodes are moved or scrambled with no attribution of who moved them; the client withdraws.
- Evidence: ADR-2017:31 (loopback, never via the server); ADR-2017:39 (OPEN, authentication deferred); 17-local-control.md:30 (same-user trust as estate precedent); useGraphEventHandlers.ts / message_routing.rs routing exists and requires no new server message (VT-07.3); "no bridge crate or stream exists" today (Cargo.toml:2, DEBT) — so any bridge is new code outside every reviewed file.
- Warning signs visible today: the OPEN deferral is the entire security design for the seam; the estate's one built local-input endpoint (the Studio control socket) explicitly disclaims being a boundary; the receiving path is already wired end-to-end on the VisionClaw side.
- Confidence: medium — the deferred-auth seam and the unguarded routing are stated; the build-out and the compromise are inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Published output can carry DECOMPILE-derived or verbatim vendor material; the barriers are one research note, one review, and one extension list
- Topics: VT-01.1, VT-01.3, VT-01.4, VT-06.4
- Chain of events:
  1. Two decompilation entries exist (D-0001, D-0002, 2026-10-16, both about release and safety behaviour). Their findings are DECOMPILE class and excluded from the paper (POLICY.md:8; POSITION.md:33).
  2. That exclusion is held only by the research notes: "no docs/spec file cites P-0036 or P-0041, the exclusion is held by the research notes" (overload-and-calibration.md:9 and :52).
  3. Independently, the repository gate scans only file extensions and paths (quality-gate.sh:38; VT-01.4: "Only rule 5 is machine-checked… Rules 6 and 7 rely on review"), so a verbatim vendor table or trademark pasted into a `.md` or `.rs` file passes CI.
  4. A DECOMPILE-derived constant or verbatim vendor fragment, restated into spec or code months earlier, flows into the paper or a client-shared document.
  5. The legal posture document still says the decompilation trigger has never fired, because T3 fired twice without POSITION.md being revisited (POSITION.md:46 vs DECOMPILATION-LOG.md:11, DIVERGENCE), and the decompilations were performed by a coding agent in the specification role, contrary to the stated separation (PROVENANCE.md:20 vs DECOMPILATION-LOG.md:42, :82).
  6. The rights holder or the client's reviewer finds the derivation; the clean-room claim collapses for those facts.
- Evidence: as cited above, plus VT-01.1 N2 (OPEN: DESIGN is used on 24 rows but absent from the class table and the paper-eligible list, ADR-0001:34) — a second undecided provenance class that can reach publication without a decision.
- Warning signs visible today: three independent control gaps (notes-only exclusion, extension-only scan, review-only rules 6/7), plus two marked divergences that already contradict the method's own documents.
- Confidence: medium — every control gap is stated; the leak itself is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Evidence records can carry the wrong software identity
- Topics: VT-08.5
- Evidence: `software.commit comes from git rev-parse HEAD in the process working directory so another checkout yields the wrong software identity` (evidence.rs:534-544, OPEN) — against the schema's purpose of binding each attempt to the code that produced it (evidence.rs:201, :279).
- Failure: the Studio run from a release checkout or a different branch stamps every attempt with that directory's HEAD; the sitting's force figures are later attributed to, or reproduced against, the wrong source.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — A stale `OutputOff` token can let readings attach while output is on
- Topics: VT-08.5, VT-08.2
- Evidence: `OutputOff` "accepts a caller supplied false Boolean… not bound to device lifetime" (evidence.rs:24-34); DEBT: "the Boolean token can be retained after rearming, normal ordering depends on worker control flow" (evidence.rs:490; worker.rs:495) — against the invariant that readings are typed only while output is off (worker.rs:1014-1018; README.md:68-71).
- Failure: a control-flow change retains the token across a rearm; a reading taken while motors run is accepted, a rung is scored from it, and an Over/Pass verdict locks or unlocks the ladder on an invalid measurement.
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Recorder captures can be silently incomplete yet used as provenance evidence
- Topics: VT-06.2, VT-06.4
- Evidence: "a failed log write only prints a diagnostic and the run carries on with an incomplete capture" (tools/port-recorder/src/lib.rs:279-281, DEBT); "its check.py is run by hand and no gate step runs it" (AGENTS.md:53, README.md:11 vs quality-gate.sh:35, DEBT); "on 1 November its memory snapshots returned EPERM in the audit container, despite a green quality gate" (VT-06.4 prose).
- Failure: an OBSERVE capture is truncated or lacks buffer snapshots, is cited as the basis of a P-row and spec paragraph, and no gate ever flags it.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — The vendor-artefact gate checks only extensions and paths
- Topics: VT-01.4, VT-06.4
- Evidence: "Only rule 5 is machine-checked, and only by file extension and path. Rules 6 and 7 rely on review" (VT-01.4 prose); the scan is over `git ls-files` (quality-gate.sh:38); rules 6 and 7 prohibit verbatim vendor strings/tables and trademarks (POLICY.md:10-11).
- Failure: a verbatim vendor table or trademark pasted into a `.md` or `.rs` file passes CI and ships in the paper or a client deliverable.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-05 — The DECOMPILE-to-paper exclusion is enforced by one research document
- Topics: VT-01.1, VT-01.3
- Evidence: DECOMPILE rows "never reach the paper" (POLICY.md:8; POSITION.md:33), but "no docs/spec file cites P-0036 or P-0041, the exclusion is held by the research notes" (overload-and-calibration.md:9 and :52).
- Failure: a spec paragraph restates a D-finding without citing the row; the fact reaches the paper; the exclusion depended on a single note's survival.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-06 — The legal review trigger has already fired without the review reopening
- Topics: VT-01.4
- Evidence: T3 "any decompilation of a vendor binary" re-opens the review (POSITION.md:46); DIVERGENCE: "T3 has fired twice (D-0001 and D-0002) but the position document is unchanged" (POSITION.md:46 vs DECOMPILATION-LOG.md:11).
- Failure: any external contact (a client demonstration, a shared draft) occurs under a posture document that no longer describes reality; the review that must precede such contact never happened.
