- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: medium

### F-07 — Decompilation was performed by an implementer-role agent
- Topics: VT-01.3
- Evidence: "PROVENANCE requires decompilation by the spec team never the implementer" (PROVENANCE.md:20) while "both entries name a coding agent in the specification role" (DECOMPILATION-LOG.md:42 and :82) — DIVERGENCE.
- Failure: under peer review or rights-holder inquiry, the log itself contradicts the separation the method claims, weakening the admissibility of the findings and the ladder's credibility.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — A failed shutdown clears the pending sequence, so Drop cannot neutralize the arm
- Topics: VT-02.4, VT-02.5
- Evidence: "end_force_session clears the pending sequence even when shutdown failed, so Drop cannot resend neutral packets, it only retries the port release" (device.rs:345; device.rs:326-328, DEBT).
- Failure: a fault during shutdown leaves the last offsets latched on the motors; the process exits believing cleanup was attempted, and the bookkeeping now says nothing is pending.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-09 — No hardware watchdog; guard expiry is evaluated only when the worker runs
- Topics: VT-02 (business), VT-03.1, VT-03.4
- Evidence: "It cannot protect against the computer itself freezing or the process being killed, because no timer lives in the hardware that anyone has established" (VT-02 business); "expiry is only evaluated when the worker runs, there is no hardware watchdog" (crates/device/src/lib.rs:59-60; 12-force-feedback.md:91-92, DEBT).
- Failure: a scheduler stall, process freeze or SIGKILL leaves the last accepted offsets commanded to the motors for as long as power remains.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-10 — A Wayland-latched hold can keep an attempt alive without the operator
- Topics: VT-08.1, VT-08.7
- Evidence: "on Wayland a held Space can stay latched after focus loss because no key-up or focus event arrives, only the 250 ms heartbeat and Stop remain" (main.rs:323-330, DEBT); the heartbeat is bumped by the UI every frame (main.rs:214-215).
- Failure: the operator presses Space, changes focus, releases (no key-up delivered); if the UI loop keeps running, the heartbeat stays fresh while `held` is latched, and the attempt continues under interlocks alone until Stop or the class time bound.
- Confidence: medium — the latch is stated; continued rendering while unfocused is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — The thermal class runs 660 s with no hold, only periodic Confirms
- Topics: VT-08.2, VT-08.3
- Evidence: "a thermal attempt has no hold, only a Confirm every 30 s with a 10 s grace, so release is no longer a stop and the arm relies on Confirm and the 660 s bound" (controller.rs:26-28; controller.rs:61, DEBT).
- Failure: the operator is absent for up to roughly 40 s while the arm is driven within caps; the consent mechanism present on every other class is absent on the longest one.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — The local control socket trusts every same-user client and never unlinks a stale socket
- Topics: VT-08.7
- Evidence: "the socket is not a boundary against code already running as the owner, same-user clients have the trust of the local interface" (17-local-control.md:30, OPEN); "an existing socket is never unlinked" (control.rs:167-169); the socket can prepare a powered attempt up to the consent point (control.rs:410-422).
- Failure: owner-user malware prepares powered attempts and probes the consent path; combined with F-10, one fresh key press can leave consent latched. After a crash, a stale socket path persists and is never removed — bind behaviour at next start is unstated (inferred).
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — `CONTINUOUS_FORCE_N` is policy in documents only; no code reads it
- Topics: VT-03.1, VT-04, VT-05
- Evidence: "CONTINUOUS_FORCE_N is documented as the cap for content and ADR-0002 relies on it but no code reads the constant" (ADR-0002:38 vs crates/device/src/lib.rs:50, DEBT); only the Studio controller enforces a dwell budget (controller.rs:14; controller.rs:519-527).
- Failure: any consumer outside the Studio (Mass Lab today, a future bridge) holds a capped but continuous command indefinitely; the dwell budget the ADR relies on exists nowhere in the enforced path.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Counter unwrapping assumes sub-half-revolution motion between samples, unchecked
- Topics: VT-03.2, VT-07.2
- Evidence: "unwrapping assumes less than half a counter revolution between samples, nothing checks it" (crates/device/src/lib.rs:196-197, DEBT).
- Failure: a fast flick aliases counts; `position_m` jumps by a revolution's worth; Mass Lab computes force toward the wrong point (capped by the guard) and a future drag integration yanks a node across the graph.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — `with-port-access` can run the recorder as root despite the stated policy
- Topics: VT-06.3
- Evidence: POLICY "do not preload reference software as root, loader variables are set after the launcher" (README.md:34-35); but "Its direct-exec branch runs before the root check, so the script does not enforce a universal prohibition on running as root" (scripts/with-port-access:10-17, VT-06.3 prose).
- Failure: an operator who is already root, or a wrapper hitting the direct-exec path, runs the LD_PRELOAD recorder with full privileges; `process_vm_readv` snapshots up to 64 KiB per call of the target's memory (tools/port-recorder/src/lib.rs:103-117) with no gate oversight of the output.
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

## vowl-wasm / critical

### F-01 — A failed npm publish after asset attach permanently wedges the release pipeline, leaving npm and GitHub serving different versions
- Topics: VW-05.3, VW-04.6
- Evidence: The retry path claims recovery ("Attempt npm publish of same tarball", release.yml:173), but the immutable-asset guard (release.yml:86-90) blocks any re-run once the tarball is attached — "including when a prior npm publish failed after attachment. The later registry skip cannot be reached on that retry" (release.yml:163-171). This already happened: "The observed v0.2.2 run attached its asset then failed npm publication; the registry listed only 0.1.2 on 1 November 2026." Existing registry versions are then "skipped without comparing integrity" (release.yml:168-171), and visionGraph therefore installs the 0.2.2 GitHub tarball under the npm package name instead of resolving from npm (publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:25, package-lock.json:598-603). The workflow "supplies no explicit npm token" and relies on OIDC trust that is "external prerequisites, not proved by this YAML" (release.yml:14-21).
- Failure: Any npm-side failure after attachment wedges the tag forever; consumers split across two channels with different content (npm 0.1.2 vs GitHub 0.2.2, which carries the pin/reheat APIs), and nothing in the pipeline compares the two channels' integrity. Every future release risks the same deadlock until someone manually intervenes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Legacy quadtree has no subdivision depth guard: coincident nodes crash `WebVowl.tick()` with an out-of-bounds trap while the sibling CSR tree carries the fix
- Topics: VW-03.3, VW-03.5
- Evidence: "insertion has no depth or minimum-cell guard (src/layout/quadtree.rs:166-238). With 51 nodes pinned at one coordinate, the current WASM `WebVowl.tick()` traps with an out-of-bounds memory access during repeated subdivision." The fix exists only in the other engine: "The separate CSR tree has a maximum depth of 40 (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:110, src/layout/csr_sim.rs:213)", and the authors' own DEBT note says "a fix to one does not reach the other" (VW-03.5 N1). The leaf-force nudge "cannot protect tree construction" (VW-03.3).
- Failure: In the published bundle (WebVowl path), any graph of >50 nodes where multiple nodes share exact coordinates — e.g., host code that pins nodes programmatically at one point before layout, or restores positions — traps the WASM module. A WASM trap kills the worker/module with no recovery short of re-instantiation; the depth-40 guard that prevents this is in the other codebase tree.
- Confidence: high (the trap is stated as reproduced; production reachability of coincident coordinates depends on host code not shown)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes, partly — DEBT (VW-03.5 N1) marks the dual-tree divergence; the trap itself carries only a bolded "Failure boundary" label (VW-03.3), not a formal marker

### F-03 — visionGraph's worker commits a new generation before validating tier bytes: a rejected load lets a later Start publish the old graph's positions as the new scope
- Topics: VW-04.6
- Evidence: "loadScope changes generation/count before validating bytes (physics.worker.ts:271-295). After a rejected tier, a later Start can publish old positions under the new generation. Caller `nodeCount` is not checked against the loaded tier." The protocol's guard is minimal: "isMainToWorker only checks an object with a string `type` (protocol.ts:195-200)".
- Failure: User switches from scope A to scope B; B's bytes are rejected (corrupt or wrong tier); a subsequent Start publishes A's layout tagged with B's generation. The UI renders and labels the wrong dataset, and a count mismatch between the caller's expectation and the loaded tier goes undetected. Wrong data displayed with correct-looking provenance is a data-custody incident, not a render glitch.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no formal marker (bolded "Failure boundary" label only, VW-04.6)

### F-04 — CSR tuning parameters are unvalidated at the boundary and the host protocol forwards non-finite values: NaN layouts and simulations that never settle
- Topics: VW-03.7, VW-04.6, VW-03.5
- Evidence: "CSR parameter setters accept unchecked values (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:620-653, src/layout/csr_sim.rs:692-703). Infinite reheat produces non-finite positions; negative `alphaMin` prevents settling in a 10,000-tick probe." The shipped host adapter feeds this directly: "unchecked non-finite parameter messages reach CSR configuration" because `isMainToWorker` performs no numeric validation (protocol.ts:195-200). `finished` is a latched bool set only when the alpha floor is hit (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:257,448), so a negative alphaMin means `isFinished()` never becomes true.
- Failure: One `setParam` message with `Infinity`/`NaN` (a division by zero upstream, a JSON parse of `null`) makes the entire tier's positions non-finite — blank or garbage rendering in every client. A negative `alphaMin` makes the per-frame tick loop run forever: permanent CPU/battery burn across the deployed user base until page reload, with no error surfaced.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no formal marker (bolded "Input boundary", VW-03.7; "Failure boundary", VW-04.6)

### F-05 — Legacy `pinNode` accepts NaN/Infinity coordinates; one bad drag event can poison the whole layout while CSR pinning rejects the same input
- Topics: VW-02.8, VW-04.1, VW-03.1
- Evidence: "`pin_node_at` accepts non-finite coordinates (src/graph/pinning.rs:82-105)" and "pinNode accepts non-finite positions through the legacy manager (src/graph/pinning.rs:82-102); CSR pinning rejects them" — the same operation has opposite validation in the two engines. The legacy engine's forces are computed from node positions (src/layout/simulation.rs:189-198) and only `visual.fixed` nodes are skipped at integration (src/layout/simulation.rs:250), so a pinned node at NaN still contributes distances to every other node.
- Failure: A JS drag handler that passes a NaN coordinate (cancelled pointer event, undefined read) pins a node at NaN in `WebVowl`; inferred: distances from that node become NaN, propagating NaN velocities and positions across the graph until reload. The engine gives no error at the moment of pinning.
- Confidence: high that non-finite values are accepted (stated); whole-graph propagation is inferred from the force model
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no formal marker (mentioned inside the bolded "Replacement boundary" bullet, VW-04.1)

### F-06 — `loadOntology` keeps stale pin IDs and a settled alpha: a replaced ontology either never lays out or inherits pins from the previous ontology
- Topics: VW-04.1, VW-02.8, VW-03.1
- Evidence: "loadOntology swaps graph/metadata without resetting the pin manager or simulation alpha (src/bindings/mod.rs:56-70). Obsolete pin IDs remain reported and a previously settled engine leaves the new graph unticked until explicitly restarted." `is_finished()` is a pure alpha read (src/layout/simulation.rs:340) and `tick` returns early on it (src/layout/simulation.rs:277-279), so a previously settled engine is a permanent no-op. "Pin IDs are separate manager state: graph replacement does not reset them" (VW-02.8); IDs are strings taken from the input JSON (src/ontology/parser.rs:75-85), so cross-ontology ID collision is realistic.
- Failure: A runtime "load different ontology" feature: the new graph never lays out (alpha below min → every tick a no-op) unless the host remembers to call `initSimulation`; separately, any new-ontology node whose ID matches an old pin is frozen at the previous ontology's coordinates, and `getPinnedCount`/`isPinned` report pins that refer to nothing the user pinned.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no formal marker (bolded "Replacement boundary", VW-04.1)

### F-07 — `max_classes` silently truncates oversized ontologies instead of rejecting them
- Topics: VW-02.2
- Evidence: "ParserConfig (src/ontology/parser.rs:17) has a `max_classes` cutoff enforced at src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64: a `class` array longer than the configured cap is silently truncated, not rejected." This compounds with the other silent ingest losses: root `classAttribute`/`propertyAttribute` tables "are not joined" (VW-02.2).
- Failure: An ontology larger than the cap loads "successfully" with classes missing and no warning or error. Users read the visualization as the complete model — silent data loss at the ingest boundary, and the missing classes also skew degree filtering and statistics computed downstream.
- Confidence: high (behavior stated; the default cap value is not given, so trigger frequency is unknown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — The release-inventory guard is stale on main, checks commit syntax not resolution, and excludes the release workflow and itself from its hash set
- Topics: VW-05.6, VW-05.1, VW-05.3
- Evidence: The TENSION note: "the guard runs on tags and manual dispatch only, so on main a source edit can leave docs/release-inventory.json stale until the next release attempt fails" (ci.yml:50-52, release.yml:79-85). Additionally, unmarked: verify "checks source-commit syntax, not commit resolution, and excludes the release workflow and guard implementation from its source hash set" — so a modified release.yml (including the npm publish or asset steps it gates) is never caught by the guard that is supposed to sit "between a source change and a browser tarball".
- Failure: Two modes: (a) routine merges to main accumulate staleness and the next release attempt fails at verify, blocking delivery; (b) any change to release.yml itself — the pipeline that builds and publishes browser code — ships without the inventory noticing, defeating the supply-chain control exactly where it is most security-relevant.
- Confidence: high (mechanics stated); impact of the workflow-exclusion gap is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes, TENSION (VW-05.6 N2) for the stale-on-main timing only; the hash-set exclusion and syntax-only commit check are unmarked

### F-09 — `#![deny(unsafe_code)]` is overridden and `is_simd_available()` reports the build flag, not browser capability
- Topics: VW-01.1, VW-03.4, VW-01.4
- Evidence: "Crate-level deny(unsafe_code) is locally overridden for SIMD intrinsics (src/layout/simd.rs:24); it is not a guarantee of no unsafe code" — while src/lib.rs:136-137 carries the crate-level deny, the standard signal auditors and downstream users rely on. Separately, `is_simd_available()` on wasm32 "returns the compile-time `cfg!(feature = \"simd\")`; it does not probe browser support" (src/layout/simd.rs:516), and the feature "needs simd128 target-feature" (Cargo.toml:87).
- Failure: Consumers or auditors treating the crate as unsafe-free are mistaken; the leaf-force path calls `unsafe calculate_leaf_force_simd()` (src/layout/simd.rs:386) gated only on the compile-time feature. A custom build with `--features simd` reports SIMD "available" on browsers without SIMD128 and then traps or fails to run — the availability check is a build flag masquerading as a runtime probe.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — `positionsPtr` hands out raw memory views invalidated by the allocations every tick performs; the rebuild obligation is prose, not an API
- Topics: VW-04.2, VW-01.7
- Evidence: The zero-copy path is a "raw pointer into wasm.memory.buffer" that "needs … rebuild view after load or memory growth" (src/bindings/explorer.rs:84,90). At the same time, "ticks allocate force buffers and tree storage (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:386-387, src/layout/csr_sim.rs:186-187); `writePositionsInto` also uses allocating generated glue" (VW-01.7) — i.e., the engine allocates during exactly the activity the view is meant to observe. The one audited consumer rebuilds the view itself (physics.worker.ts:164-192); nothing in the crate enforces it.
- Failure: Any consumer other than the audited worker that caches the `Float32Array` (a natural "zero-copy" optimization the API invites) reads from a detached buffer after WASM memory growth mid-layout: reads yield undefined, coordinates become NaN downstream, and the failure appears far from its cause.
- Confidence: medium (invalidation hazard stated; that growth routinely occurs during ticks is inferred from the per-tick allocation)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — The parser silently drops ontology semantics, narrows u64 cardinalities to u32 unchecked, and `validate_iris` is an unimplemented stub
- Topics: VW-02.3, VW-02.2, VW-02.1
- Evidence: "parsed class equivalences/individuals are not copied to nodes (src/graph/builder.rs:24-31); exact cardinality is discarded when only min/max reach the edge (src/graph/builder.rs:53-54). Bounds without a `cardinality` key are ignored and u64 values narrow to u32 unchecked (src/ontology/parser.rs:389-413). `rdfs:subClassOf` misses the case-sensitive substring mapping (src/graph/builder.rs:121-128)." Also: "validate_iris has no implementation (src/ontology/parser.rs:485-507)" while `validate()` is a required pipeline step (VW-02.1).
- Failure: A cardinality of 2³² or larger wraps silently to a wrong small value on the rendered edge; equivalences and some subclass edges simply never appear, so the visualization understates the ontology with no diagnostic. Running the mandatory `validate()` step invokes an IRI validator that does nothing — false assurance for callers who believe invalid IRIs are caught.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no formal marker (bolded "Semantic loss", VW-02.3)

### F-12 — The two layout engines expose same-named APIs with divergent semantics and share no interface
- Topics: VW-03.7, VW-03.1, VW-03.5, VW-04.2, VW-02.8
- Evidence: `is_finished` is a pure alpha read in the legacy engine (src/layout/simulation.rs:340) but a latched bool in CSR (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:257,448). `reheat` "clamps alpha to (0, 1] and ignores a non-finite or non-positive value" in legacy (src/layout/simulation.rs:71-75) but "assigns `max(value, alpha_min)`" in CSR (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:686-689). Pinning rejects non-finite coordinates in CSR but accepts them in legacy (src/graph/pinning.rs:82-102 vs src/layout/csr_sim.rs:655-666); every CSR load clears pins (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:334,371) while legacy `loadOntology` does not (src/bindings/mod.rs:56-70). `writePositionsInto` writes pairs with "odd tail untouched" in legacy (src/bindings/mod.rs:303-312) but "copies individual floats, including an odd last element" in CSR (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:610-614). The engines "share a design … but not a common Rust interface" (VW-03.7). Related doc contradiction: the legacy `writePositionsInto` doc claims allocation avoidance that wasm-bindgen 0.2.128 glue falsifies (src/bindings/mod.rs:291-296).
- Failure: Host code written and tested against one engine silently misbehaves against the other: `reheat(2.0)` succeeds on one and clamps on the other; `isFinished()` after an alpha-floor hit recomputes false on one engine and stays latched true on the other; a shared positions-copy routine leaves a stale odd element with one engine. Each divergence is individually small; collectively every "works for one engine" assumption is a latent bug for the other, and there is no interface forcing them to agree.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the dual-tree aspect is marked DEBT in VW-03.5; the semantic divergences are not)

### F-13 — `runSimulation(n)` reseeds on every call — including pinned nodes — so frame-loop usage never converges
- Topics: VW-03.1, VW-03.9
- Evidence: "run(graph, iterations) (src/layout/simulation.rs:326) calls initialize … Every call reseeds, including fixed nodes unless the native preservation option applies; repeated `runSimulation(n)` is not a continuation API (src/layout/simulation.rs:89-119)." `initialize_positions` re-circles any node unless `preserve_seeded_positions` is set and the node is already placed, and resets velocities unconditionally (src/layout/simulation.rs:113-119).
- Failure: A UI that calls `runSimulation(50)` per animation frame expecting incremental progress instead re-circles every node each frame: the layout flickers and never settles. Worse, user-pinned nodes are teleported back to seed positions — drag pinning done through the legacy manager is destroyed by any subsequent `runSimulation` call unless the native-only preservation option was configured.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Click hit-testing includes hidden/filtered nodes at a fixed 20-unit radius for all nodes
- Topics: VW-04.3, VW-04.7, VW-04.1
- Evidence: `checkNodeClick` builds "NodeHitTest per node, radius=20.0 fixed, including hidden nodes (src/bindings/mod.rs:423-435)", with the radius a hardcoded constant — the code's own comment concedes "In a real implementation, this could be configurable or per-node" (src/bindings/mod.rs:425-426). The filtering API (`filterByDegree`, `filterHierarchy`, VW-04.1) hides nodes but the hit-test ignores visibility; the authors add that the native bench "does not establish that hidden-node selection … is correct" (VW-04.7).
- Failure: After a user filters the graph, clicks on visually empty canvas select invisible nodes; selection-driven actions (pin, inspect, navigate) apply to entities the user cannot see. All nodes share radius 20 regardless of rendered size, so hit areas also mismatch the visualization for large or small nodes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — The recorded verification evidence is stale, skips the shipped feature set, and the headline perf bench measures no-ops
- Topics: VW-01 (historical qualification), VW-04.5, VW-05.4, VW-05.2
- Evidence: The test record: "cargo test --locked --offline --lib passed 136 default-feature tests at the earlier stamp; that count was not re-run at 89d1a0a … That run covers native logic, not optional `ngg1`, `interaction`, browser loading or a deployed consumer" — while the published bundle is built with exactly `ngg1,interaction` (README.md:201) and every file's front matter claims `verified_commit: 28af4365…`. Locally, `cargo test` without features "silently skips" the ngg1 test via `required-features` (Cargo.toml:109-111). The audit itself bypassed the pipeline: "A stale local pkg/ failed the gate in this audit; current source was tested through a separately generated bundle" (VW-04.5). The bench file's stated targets are "aspirational annotations in the source, not enforced by any CI assertion", and `wasm_tick_operation` "reuses a settling instance so later calls are no-ops" while the serialisation bench "measures `graph.nodes()` rather than the JS method" (benches/phase3_benchmarks.rs:80-87, 110, 240-250).
- Failure: The configuration actually shipped to browsers is the configuration the recorded test run never exercised; perf regressions in the shipped bundle are invisible because the tick benchmark measures no-ops after the first iteration; and the bundle gate can pass or fail on local artifact state rather than pipeline output, so release decisions rest on evidence weaker than the verified-commit stamps imply.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no (the self-qualification paragraph exists but carries no marker)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Production reachability of the quadtree trap (F-02).** Whether node coordinates can arrive coincident through `loadOntology` (the parser fields listed do not include x/y), the default `max_classes` value, and how host code pins nodes — I would need the parser defaults and real host call patterns.
2. **Why the v0.2.2 npm publish failed and whether OIDC publishing is correctly configured** (release.yml:14-21 says registry-side trust is unproven) — registry logs and npm/registry-side settings are outside this material.
3. **The `ontomark-ngg1` 0.1.0 codec's own guarantees** — validation strictness, error taxonomy, version compatibility of the byte format; the crate is external and only referenced (src/ngg1.rs:5, ADR-001:26).
4. **The full consumer population and their call patterns** — only visionGraph's worker is audited; whether other consumers call `initSimulation` after `loadOntology`, cache `positionsPtr` views, or send unchecked `setParam` values determines whether F-06, F-10 and half of F-04 fire in practice.
5. **ADR-001's unclaimed scope** — the Obsidian plugin, the TypeScript reader consolidation and the wider acceptance evidence are explicitly not claimed (ADR-001:89-93, marked OPEN); nothing here permits judging those pieces at all.

## vowl-wasm / premortem

# Post-mortem — vowl-wasm / visionGraph rendering pipeline

**Basis:** reconstructed solely from the distilled documentation (vowl-wasm at `28af4365a68d9aacfd7c04f63299cc96ad717ce3`, visionGraph consumer files at `7b0875f2c26b9e69dc5c97e9c88166acc14c47dc`). The incident itself is not recorded in this material; the causes below are the failure paths the material already documents, ranked by likelihood. Every claim carries the citation given in the source material and must be re-verified against code before action.

---

### R-1 — After a rejected tier load, the worker publishes the previous tier's positions under the new generation
- Topics: VW-04.6, VW-03.6, VW-01.6, VW-04.1
- Chain of events:
  1. Main thread sends `loadScope` for a new tier; the worker increments generation and count **before** validating the bytes (physics.worker.ts:271-295).
  2. `NggExplorer.loadCsr` rejects the bytes; the parse failure is mapped to a plain string `JsValue` (src/bindings/explorer.rs:41-45, src/layout/csr_sim.rs:358-359) — the old simulation state and old positions remain in the worker.
  3. A later `Start` message runs against the still-loaded old tier and publishes its positions, now labelled with the new generation.
  4. The caller-supplied `nodeCount` is never checked against the loaded tier, so the main thread zips `ids[i] ↔ xy[2i], xy[2i+1]` (the index-stable contract of VW-04.1) over mismatched data — positions for one dataset attributed to another.
- Evidence: "loadScope changes generation/count before validating bytes (physics.worker.ts:271-295). After a rejected tier, a later Start can publish old positions under the new generation. Caller nodeCount is not checked against the loaded tier." (VW-04.6, explicit "Failure boundary" callout). Error kinds never cross the boundary, only rendered message text (src/error.rs:43, src/bindings/mod.rs:56), so the host cannot branch on failure class. Worker message validation only checks "an object with a string type" (protocol.ts:195-200).
- Warning signs visible today: the failure boundary is stated verbatim in the current docs; the worker's silent no-op handlers for optional methods (physics.worker.ts:126-128) already normalize invisible degraded states.
- Confidence: high — the defect is stated directly; the triggering production sequence (rejected tier followed by Start) is inferred from the stated ordering.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Client ontologies are silently truncated at `ParserConfig.max_classes`
- Topics: VW-02.2, VW-02.1, VW-02.3, VW-02.4
- Chain of events:
  1. A client ontology with a `class` array longer than the configured cap is parsed.
  2. The cutoff at src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64 **silently truncates** the array — no rejection, no error surfaced to JS.
  3. `GraphBuilder::from_ontology` builds a graph over the surviving prefix; statistics, node counts and metadata (VW-02.4, VW-04.1) are computed over the truncated graph.
  4. The client receives a successful load and a visualization/statistics set that is missing classes; if surviving properties reference truncated classes the load may instead fail at the builder's both-endpoints requirement (src/graph/mod.rs:271-285) — either outcome is uncontrolled from the client's perspective.
- Evidence: "a `class` array longer than the configured cap is silently truncated, not rejected" (src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64, VW-02.2). Corroborating tolerance of partial data in the same path: root `classAttribute`/`propertyAttribute` tables not joined, `validate_iris` unimplemented (src/ontology/parser.rs:17-25, 485-507).
- Warning signs visible today: stated in the current docs; the ingest path is documented as accepting partial/lossy input without error.
- Confidence: high — the defect is stated; occurrence on a large client ontology in production is inferred (it fires deterministically on any input over the cap).
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Unguarded quadtree subdivision traps `WebVowl.tick()` with an out-of-bounds memory access on coincident nodes
- Topics: VW-03.3, VW-03.2, VW-03.5, VW-02.8
- Chain of events:
  1. A `WebVowl` (main-thread) graph with more than 50 nodes engages Barnes-Hut (src/layout/simulation.rs:158, VW-03.2).
  2. Nodes end up at identical coordinates — e.g. pinned/dragged to the same point through the exposed pin API (`pin_node_at` accepts arbitrary coordinates, src/graph/pinning.rs:82-105; VW-02.8), or caller-seeded identical positions kept under `preserve_seeded_positions` (VW-03.9).
  3. `QuadTree::build` subdivides repeatedly; coincident nodes always land in the same child and insertion has **no depth or minimum-cell guard** (src/layout/quadtree.rs:166-238).
  4. `tick()` traps with an out-of-bounds memory access (demonstrated with 51 pinned coincident nodes, VW-03.3); under the release profile's `panic = "abort"` (Cargo.toml:90-95, VW-05.5) the module aborts. The leaf-force coincident-point nudge exists only at query time and "cannot protect tree construction" (VW-03.3).
- Evidence: VW-03.3 "Failure boundary" paragraph; the depth-40 cap exists only in the separate CSR tree (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:110, 213). The two engines' divergence is explicitly flagged as debt (VW-03.5 N1).
- Warning signs visible today: the trap is demonstrated against the current WASM build in the material itself; the pin API is exposed to JS and drag-pinning is a supported interaction (VW-04.6).
- Confidence: high that the defect exists and traps (demonstrated); medium that it is this incident's cause — the trigger requires a >50-node legacy-path graph with coincident nodes, and which consumers use `WebVowl` vs `NggExplorer` is not fully stated (inferred).
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Non-finite numbers flow from worker messages and pin calls into published positions
- Topics: VW-03.7, VW-04.6, VW-02.8, VW-04.1
- Evidence: CSR parameter setters accept unchecked values — "Infinite reheat produces non-finite positions; negative alphaMin prevents settling in a 10,000-tick probe" (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:620-653, 692-703, VW-03.7); the worker's `isMainToWorker` "only checks an object with a string type (protocol.ts:195-200), so unchecked non-finite parameter messages reach CSR configuration" (VW-04.6); the legacy `pin_node_at` "accepts non-finite coordinates" (src/graph/pinning.rs:82-105, VW-04.1) although the CSR `pin` rejects them (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:655-666).
- Failure: a `NaN`/`Infinity` `setParam`/`reheat`/`pinNode` value reaches configuration or a pinned position; non-finite coordinates appear in the `Float32Array` position view, are copied into the transport buffer (physics.worker.ts:164-192) and rendered or persisted as data for the current generation.
- Confidence: high — both ends are stated; the end-to-end publication of NaN is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Released-artifact custody diverges: npm holds 0.1.2 while the reviewed 0.2.2 exists only as a GitHub tarball, and repair paths are blocked
- Topics: VW-05.3, VW-04.6, VW-03.5, VW-03.7
- Evidence: the v0.2.2 run attached its GitHub asset then failed npm publication; the registry listed only 0.1.2 on 1 November 2026 (VW-05.3, release.yml:145-173). Existing registry versions are "skipped without comparing integrity" (release.yml:168-171), and a re-run of the same tag stops at the immutable-asset guard (release.yml:86-90), so the registry can never be reconciled by the workflow. visionGraph installs the GitHub tarball under the npm package name `@dreamlab-ai/vowl-wasm` (package.json:25, VW-04.6). The motion guards (softening + `MAX_STEP_FACTOR`) exist only "since the 0.2.2 pin" (VW-03.5) and the `reheat` binding is newer than the earlier gap (VW-03.7) — 0.1.2 lacks both.
- Failure: any consumer resolving from npm receives 0.1.2: near-coincident seeds flung "thousands of units in one tick" (pre-guard behaviour, VW-03.5), no reheat, older surface — under the same package name, with no integrity comparison ever run against the registry copy.
- Confidence: high for the divergence itself; the second npm consumer is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — `loadOntology` swaps the graph but keeps stale pin IDs and a settled alpha
- Topics: VW-04.1, VW-02.8, VW-03.7
- Evidence: "loadOntology swaps graph/metadata without resetting the pin manager or simulation alpha (src/bindings/mod.rs:56-70). Obsolete pin IDs remain reported and a previously settled engine leaves the new graph unticked" (VW-04.1); "Pin IDs are separate manager state: graph replacement does not reset them" (VW-02.8). The CSR side clears pins on every load (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:334, 371) — the legacy side does not.
- Failure: a replacement ontology that reuses node IDs silently pins different nodes via leftover IDs; `getPinnedCount` reports pins for nodes that no longer exist; a previously settled engine means the new graph is displayed at seed positions until explicitly restarted.
- Confidence: high — stated directly.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Two independent Barnes-Hut implementations; a guard fixed in one never reaches the other
- Topics: VW-03.5, VW-03.3
- Evidence: author-marked DEBT: "ForceSimulation and CsrSimulation each carry their own Barnes-Hut quadtree, a node-reference tree in quadtree.rs and an arena tree over the flat position buffer in csr_sim.rs, so a fix to one does not reach the other" (src/layout/quadtree.rs:126, src/layout/csr_sim.rs:160-169). The concrete asymmetry: CSR has a depth-40 cap (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:110, 213); the legacy tree has none (src/layout/quadtree.rs:166-238).
- Failure: R-3 is the realized case; any future coincident-point or softening fix applied to one engine silently misses the other.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-05 — `GraphBuilder` silently drops parsed semantics: equivalences, individuals, exact cardinality, subclass casing
- Topics: VW-02.3, VW-02.2
- Evidence: "parsed class equivalences/individuals are not copied to nodes (src/graph/builder.rs:24-31); exact cardinality is discarded when only min/max reach the edge (src/graph/builder.rs:53-54). Bounds without a `cardinality` key are ignored… `rdfs:subClassOf` misses the case-sensitive substring mapping (src/graph/builder.rs:121-128)."
- Failure: a client ontology renders and is exported (`getGraphData`) without its declared equivalences/individual counts and with unrecognized subclass edges, while the parse reported success — silently wrong graph semantics delivered downstream.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Cardinality bounds narrow u64 → u32 unchecked
- Topics: VW-02.3
- Evidence: "Bounds without a `cardinality` key are ignored and u64 values narrow to u32 unchecked (src/ontology/parser.rs:389-413)."
- Failure: a declared cardinality above 2³² wraps and is displayed/exported as a different, plausible-looking number; no error at parse or build time.
- Confidence: high — stated.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — `validate_iris` has no implementation; root attribute tables are silently not joined
- Topics: VW-02.2
- Evidence: "Root `classAttribute`/`propertyAttribute` tables are not joined, labels must be strings, and `validate_iris` has no implementation (src/ontology/parser.rs:17-25, 53-114, 485-507)."
- Failure: a WebVOWL-standard ontology whose attributes live in the root tables loads with all attributes defaulted and no error; malformed/duplicate IRIs pass validation into any downstream IRI-keyed join in the consumer.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — `checkNodeClick` hit-tests hidden nodes at a fixed radius
- Topics: VW-04.3, VW-04.1
- Evidence: the binding builds a `NodeHitTest` "per node, radius=20.0 fixed, including hidden nodes (src/bindings/mod.rs:423-435)"; the same surface exposes `filterByDegree`/`toggleDatatypes`/`resetVisibility` hiding (VW-04.1); the radius is "not read from per-node visual size" (src/bindings/mod.rs:425-426).
- Failure: a user filters to a subgraph, clicks apparent empty space over a hidden node, and receives its id — selection or subsequent operations apply to a node the user believed excluded.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — `DebugFlags` zero `log_interval` panics inside WASM
- Topics: VW-01.8, VW-05.5
- Evidence: "The public Rust `log_interval` is unchecked: zero panics in `should_log` (src/debug.rs:58-59)"; flags are set via `enable_debug`/`set_debug_flags` (src/layout/simulation.rs:50-55); release builds use `panic = "abort"` (Cargo.toml:90-95).
- Failure: enabling debug tracing with interval 0 aborts the module on the first logging tick — a crash path reachable from a support toggle.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Reported statistics depend on node insertion order; density can exceed 1
- Topics: VW-02.4
- Evidence: "Parallel/synthetic edges can still make density exceed one. Component DFS follows outgoing neighbours, so component and isolation counts depend on node insertion order (src/graph/statistics.rs:371-399, src/graph/mod.rs:326-339)."
- Failure: the same ontology loaded with classes in a different order yields different component/isolation counts via `getStatistics`; synthetic inverse edges (src/graph/builder.rs:78-89) inflate density — client-facing metrics are non-reproducible.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — `run()`/`runSimulation` reseeds every call, including fixed nodes
- Topics: VW-03.1, VW-03.9, VW-04.1
- Evidence: "Every call reseeds, including fixed nodes unless the native preservation option applies; repeated `runSimulation(n)` is not a continuation API (src/layout/simulation.rs:89-119)"; `initialize` resets alpha and iteration and zeroes velocities (VW-03.9).
- Failure: a host that calls `runSimulation(n)` repeatedly expecting incremental progress instead re-circles positions each call and discards user drags/pins — the layout never converges across calls and user work silently disappears.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The release-inventory guard goes stale on main and verifies commit syntax, not resolution
- Topics: VW-05.6, VW-05.1, VW-04.5
- Evidence: author-marked TENSION: "the guard runs on tags and manual dispatch only, so on main a source edit can leave docs/release-inventory.json stale until the next release attempt fails (ci.yml:50-52, release.yml:79-85)"; the tool "checks source-commit syntax, not commit resolution" and excludes the workflow and guard from its hash set (VW-05.6); a stale local `pkg/` already failed the gate once during the audit (VW-04.5).
- Failure: weeks of main-branch changes accumulate against an inventory hashing an older closure; releases fail late, or hand-updated hashes ship without the review the guard is meant to force — the reviewed-vs-shipped correspondence breaks.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism; the stale-then-fail scenario is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-13 — The worker resolves exports permissively and no-ops missing methods, masking version skew
- Topics: VW-04.6, F-02 context
- Evidence: the worker resolves exported names "permissively (snake_case per Rust, with camelCase fallbacks)" (physics.worker.ts:33-37); drag methods are bound with `optionalMethod` and "the message handlers return silently when the loaded package lacks them" (physics.worker.ts:126-128); the worker notes `vowl-wasm ≥ 0.2.2` for them (physics.worker.ts:50).
- Failure: running against a package lacking `pinNode`/`unpinNode`/`reheat` (e.g. the npm 0.1.2 copy, F-02) silently drops drag pinning — drags appear to take then nodes drift back, with no error anywhere to reveal the skew.
- Confidence: high for the behaviour; the production impact is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Verification evidence does not exercise what ships: no-op bench ticks, wrong-object serialisation, stale test qualification
- Topics: VW-05.4, VW-01 (historical qualification), VW-04.5
- Evidence: "`wasm_tick_operation` reuses a settling instance so later calls are no-ops (benches/phase3_benchmarks.rs:240-250)"; the serialisation bench "measures `graph.nodes()` rather than the JS method" (phase3_benchmarks.rs:110); the numeric targets are "aspirational annotations in the source, not enforced by any CI assertion" (VW-05.4). The only recorded full test run (136 default-feature tests) predates the current pin, "was not re-run at 89d1a0a", and "covers native logic, not optional `ngg1`, `interaction`, browser loading or a deployed consumer" (VW-01) — while the published bundle enables `ngg1,interaction` (VW-04.5).
- Failure: a real-tick performance regression or an optional-feature break is invisible to every recorded gate; the field hit is the first detection.
- Confidence: high for the measurement and coverage gaps; the field regression is inferred.
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — The `simd` feature ships unsafe kernels behind a compile-time-only availability probe, absent from the published bundle and its CI build
- Topics: VW-03.4, VW-01.4, VW-01.1, VW-04.5
- Evidence: crate-level `deny(unsafe_code)` is overridden for the SIMD intrinsics (src/layout/simd.rs:24, VW-01.1); `is_simd_available()` on wasm32 "returns the compile-time `cfg!(feature = "simd")`; it does not probe browser support" and the non-wasm32 stub always returns `false` (src/layout/simd.rs:516, 525, VW-03.4); the feature "needs simd128 target-feature" (Cargo.toml:87, VW-01.4); the published bundle and its gates build with `--features ngg1,interaction` only (ci.yml:176-204, VW-04.5).
- Failure: enabling `simd` for a perf fix without the `simd128` target-feature, or on the assumption of runtime detection, changes the leaf-force kernel path with no runtime probe and no coverage from the shipped bundle — a numeric or trap defect in that path would ship untested.
- Confidence: medium — the unexercised, unprobed combination is stated; the concrete failure is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

---

**Deliberately not ranked:** the `parallel` feature (adds a rayon dependency with no parallel call sites — a perf-expectation gap only, VW-01.4), `SvgRenderer` being unwired from the JS API (documentation-surface drift, VW-01.1), the CSR `writePositionsInto` odd-tail copy inconsistency (VW-04.2), and the "at most 1,500 nodes" default being a tuning comment, not an enforced limit (VW-03.10). None of these is a data, custody or security path on the evidence given.
